The KPI counts restoration only, while each cycle of a robust configuration (rate reduction, power, combining) pays its running cost; under partial observability the agent then prefers a cheap configuration that restores most geometries of a threat, which is the failure measured for benign interference (a channel switch that restores just short of 2x clean). Benign interference is also the main failure of the five flights that make KPI 7's 85-102 km/h band low.
**Decision:** training reward only (validation and test keep the standard reward): variant A, every cost x0.5 as in the sensitivity run (running costs, switching cost, false-switch penalty 40); variant B, running costs x0.5 with the switching cost and the false-switch penalty (80) unchanged. Monitor, drop threshold and discount factor as in the D64 selection (class_drop 3/3, 3 dB, gamma 0.5), 3 seeds each, selection rule unchanged.
**Adoption rule, fixed before the run:** the new agent replaces the D64 agent only if, on the validation split with escalation (`diag_failure_modes.m`, and the full validation set of `train_dqn.m`): the weakest threat (per threat, static and follower modes pooled, the KPI 4 grouping) is higher; no threat is lower by more than 2 episodes; pooled recovery, the follower and the immediate-follower groups and the mean normalized goodput are not lower; and its clean-link false alarms meet the bound. Otherwise the third reading stays final. If adopted: new test flights (test block 8, clean test block 9) and a fourth reading; detection, latency and the survivability maps do not depend on the agent and are not rerun.
**Outcome (2026-09-29):** no run met the false-alarm bound on the independent clean validation geometries. Variant A (penalty 40, switching x0.5): 48 / 57 / 89 of 600. Variant B (penalty 80, switching x1): 27 / 29 / 28 of 600, with pooled recovery 97.0-97.3% and the weakest threat at 89.5-91.1% (path loss), against 94.4%, 87.9% and 15 of 600 for the D64 selection. Lower running costs raise recovery and clean-link false alarms together: the agent acts more readily on every alarm, including the false ones. D66 is not adopted; the run is kept in `archive/v4_D66_not_adopted/`.

## D67 — Lower running costs with a higher false-switch penalty
**Why:** D66 measured the trade-off in the training reward: halving the running costs lifts validation recovery by about 3 points and the weakest threat by 2-3 points, and roughly doubles the clean-link false alarms. The false-switch penalty is the reward term that prices an action on a clean link, so it is the direct counterweight; between D66's variants a higher penalty (with the switching cost restored) cut false alarms from 48-89 to 27-29 of 600.
**Decision:** training reward only: running costs x0.5, switching cost x1, false-switch penalty 120 and 160. Monitor, drop threshold and discount factor as in the D64 selection (class_drop 3/3, 3 dB, gamma 0.5), 3 seeds each, selection rule unchanged.
**Adoption rule, fixed before the run:** as in D66, against the D64 agent. This is the last round on the reward weights: if no run is adopted, the third reading stays final.
**Outcome (2026-09-29):** false alarms on the independent clean validation geometries: penalty 120, 22 / 21 / 32 of 600; penalty 160, 15 / 22 / 21. Selected: penalty 160, seed 42 (validation 96.6%, weakest threat path loss 88.7%, 15 of 600, normalized goodput 1.189; D64 selection 94.4%, 87.9%, 15 of 600, 1.081). Adoption check against the D64 agent (`diag_failure_modes.m`, DQN + escalation): static 95.2 -> 96.4%, follower 93.5 -> 95.5%, immediate follower 90.0 -> 95.1%; no threat lower by more than 2 episodes (path loss 114 -> 112 of 124, reactive jamming -1; jamming +11, spoofing + noise burst +7, spoofing +6); goodput higher; false alarms within the bound. The weakest threat is higher on the full validation set of `train_dqn.m` (87.9 -> 88.7%) but not in the KPI 4 grouping of `diag_failure_modes.m` (90.6%, noise burst + antenna fault -> 90.3%, path loss), so the rule as written is not met. Adi chose to adopt D67 as a documented exception, as in D64: every aggregate measure improved at equal false alarms, and the one shortfall is the two path-loss episodes that the same rule tolerates per threat. New test flights (test block 8, clean test block 9) and a fourth reading, which is the final result whatever it shows. **Fourth reading (2026-09-30, final):** pooled 96.1% [94.8, 97.5] (third reading 94.2%); KPI 4 14 of 16 (benign interference 87.2%, noise burst + antenna fault 88.7%); KPI 6 13 of 600, bound 3.42%; KPI 7 met (speed bands 95.0-97.6%, latency 2.91 / 3.97 ms); combinations never trained on 84.4%. The D66 runs and the false-alarm diagnostic (`diag_false_alarms.m`: most false switches come from path-loss misreads; 4-of-4 confirmation was measured as the alternative and not needed) are kept for the record.

## D68 — v5: the full threat envelope, two further threats, measured channel ranges, signalling delay
**Why:** the user asked for a system tested over realistic ranges and a wide set of concrete threats. A review of the v4 envelope against the sources (2026-09-30) found:
- *Severity range.* The detector trained on in-band interferers up to 16 dB over our signal and the decision layer on 4-16 dB. By the link budget (`link_budget_table.m`), 16 dB is a 10 W jammer as far from the UAV as the GCS; Liu et al. and Yuan et al. use a jammer 30 dB above the signal. At 28 dB the v4 detector read 76.7% of the jamming frames correctly and flagged all of them unknown (`results/unseen_severity.txt`).
- *Eb/N0 range.* At 0-4 dB the uncoded clean link already loses 62-100% of its packets (the clean-link frame error rate of the pools), and the grid stopped at 10 dB: the regime where a real link runs well was barely covered. Nanayakkara et al. report detection studies over -10 to 20 dB.
- *Channel.* K was fixed at 10 dB. Measured air-ground K-factors run from 2-5 dB among trees and -5..10 dB at low elevation in urban areas to about 12 dB (L band) and 28 dB (C band) in open line of sight (Khawaja et al., IEEE COMST 2019, read in full). RMS delay spread is a few tens of ns most of the time against our 1 µs symbol, so flat fading stays justified.
- *Threat set.* A single-tone (CW) jammer is named in four sources (Mekdad et al. attack a 2.4 GHz drone with one; Barajas et al. jam with a CW tone and list barrage, single-tone, pulse and protocol-aware jammers; Tariq et al. list a narrowband jammer; Yang et al. a narrowband jamming class). Airframe shadowing in a banking turn exceeds 35 dB and lasts seconds, largely mitigated by multiple antennas (Khawaja et al.).
- *Instant switching.* A configuration change took effect on the next frame; the GCS has to receive it first (Barajas et al. measure recovery by link switching in 0.14-2.6 s on their hardware).
- *A bug in the evaluation.* `evaluate_policies.m` took the speed of a test episode from the seed of block 2 (the test block of the first reading); from the second reading on the test flights were in other blocks, so the per-speed recovery of KPI 7 in the second to fourth readings was a split of the episodes by speeds they were not flown at. The other KPIs do not use the speed.
**Decision (v5, one rebuild of the whole pipeline):**
- *Threats:* 10 = the 8 of v4 + `tone_jamming` (CW tone at a random offset within ±300 kHz, the flat part of our band, in-band power over our signal as a noise jammer's; own severity field, in-channel) + `airframe_shadowing` (antenna 1 attenuated for the whole run; signal side). Combined threats: 10 (+ jamming during a banking turn, tone jamming + path loss), each at three severities.
- *Severities:* detector dataset 8 levels per threat (`dataset_levels.m`: in-band 0-28 dB, path loss 4-25 dB, spoofer -4..10 dB, fault duty 5-60%, benign -12..-1.5 dB, shadowing 5-33 dB); decision layer 5 levels (`decision_config.m`: in-band 4/10/16/22/28 dB, path loss 6-22 dB, spoofer -3..9 dB, fault 10-60%, benign -10..-2 dB, shadowing 8-32 dB); nominal = the middle level.
- *Eb/N0 grid* 0:3:15 dB (2.8 to 15.7 km for a 100 mW radio; `link_budget_table.m` gives three hardware profiles, up to the radio horizon).
- *K-factor* drawn per flight from its seed, uniform 0-20 dB, for our signal and, independently, the interferers (`channel_k.m`, block 'Kfac'); PHY validation adds K = 0 and 20 dB and checks the tone's in-band power.
- *Receiver measurement* `branch_gap`: gap between the antennas' mean channel gains over the frame (15 link features); it separates continuous shadowing from an intermittent fault.
- *Follower:* the tone jammer is an SDR that can retune after a hop, so it joins jamming, reactive jamming and spoofing among the threats that can follow the link (`link_env.m`); the follower and comb sets are otherwise unchanged.
- *Signalling delay:* a change runs on the link one cycle after it is decided (`C.switch_delay`), the policy knows what it requested and the monitor what the frame was received with; the evaluation also runs the single set with two cycles.
- *Evaluation:* 12 test geometries per cell (8 before); a fourth split of 8 geometries flown at 20-50 and 120-160 km/h for the clean link and the single threats at nominal severity (rotorcraft speeds of 8-20 m/s up to the 161 km/h limit of small UAVs, Khawaja et al.); speeds stored with the pools and read from there (the bug above); latency also on one CPU core.
- *Seeds:* train block 1, validation 5, clean validation 4 as before (K now drawn from the same seeds); new test block 10, clean test 11, unseen speeds 12, survivability options 13 (`pool_seed.m`, blocks from 10 in their own range).
- *Training grid,* selection rule and gate as in D67, widened because the environment changed: monitors class_drop 3/3 and 2/2, false-switch penalty 80 / 120 / 160 with running costs x0.5, gamma 0.5, 3 seeds, 30 000 episodes.
**Adoption:** v5 replaces v4 as the system (a change of scope, not a tuning step). Every choice inside it is made on validation with the rules above; the test flights (blocks 10, 11, 12) are read once, and that reading is the result whatever it shows. The v4 final state is kept in `archive/v4_final_D67_20260930/`.

## D69 — v5: spatially separated antennas, the hit antenna drawn per run, reference-antenna measurements
**Why:** the user asked whether the two antennas work in parallel and whether one could serve as a monitor and the other as a reference (2026-09-30). The review found:
- *Parallel reception is right.* Both antennas always receive and are combined (MRC, or MMSE for nulling). At 6 dB per antenna (`validate_phy.m`): BER 7.9e-3 with one antenna, 3.6e-4 with two (MRC), 1.8e-5 with three. An antenna kept for monitoring only would give most of that up.
- *The array could not produce the shadowing it was given.* The antennas were half a wavelength apart (6.25 cm) under the fuselage, yet airframe shadowing hit antenna 1 only; a wing that hides one of them hides both. Khawaja et al.: shadowing "can be largely (but not always completely) alleviated by using multiple spatially separated antennas"; their example has two bottom-mounted antennas about 1.2 m apart with more than 30 dB of shadowing; omni-directional antennas suit a manoeuvring UAV; most measurements mount the antennas under the fuselage or the wings.
- *Antenna 1 was special.* The fault and the shadowing always hit antenna 1, and the IQ measurements (rssi, sinr, env_corr, iot) and the spectrogram came from antenna 1: a detector could learn which antenna fails, and a test built the same way would not show it. The Eb/N0 estimate of the path-loss alarm (from the antenna-1 SINR) read a hidden antenna 1 as path loss.
**Decision:**
- *Geometry:* omni antennas under the fuselage or the wings over a 1.2 m aperture (two antennas 9.6 wavelengths apart; three antennas, the survivability option, 4.8 apart over the same aperture). Receive correlation 0.3 kept (ground scatterers are seen under similar angles from the UAV, Khawaja et al.). The spatial measurements (coh, mmse_gain, align, branch_dip, branch_gap) use the channel and covariance estimates and hold for any geometry. On the wide pair the spatial signature repeats with direction: 18% of random interferer directions are within alignment 0.9 of the GCS (13% on the half-wavelength pair).
- *Fixed directions* (GUI, threat gallery, PHY validation): 44.1 / -50 / 60 deg, alignment 0.29 / 0.20 / 0.31 (70 deg falls on a repeat of the GCS signature on the new pair). Survivability geometries 41.4 and 6.5 deg: the alignment of 45 and 10 deg on the half-wavelength pair (0.20 and 0.93).
- *Hit antenna:* the fault or the airframe hits one antenna drawn per run from the run's seed.
- *Reference and monitor:* per frame the antenna with the higher SINR is the reference; rssi, sinr, env_corr, iot and the spectrogram are taken on it, and the new measurement `sinr_gap` compares the other antenna with it (16 link features). The Eb/N0 estimate of the path-loss alarm follows the reference antenna, so a hidden or failing antenna is not read as path loss.
- *Third antenna:* stays the survivability option of the proposal (deliverable 7), on the same aperture. In v4 it added 4.2 points on combined threats and made none worse; a system with three antennas is decided on the v5 result.
**Adoption:** part of v5 before any test reading; D68 otherwise unchanged. The run stopped during spectrogram extraction and restarts from A0.

## D70 — v5 scope: the platform of the lecturer's sources, three antennas, ranges within the sources, operating envelope fixed on validation
**Why (2026-09-30, with the user):**
- *Platform.* The lecturer's papers all treat small drones: small-to-medium UAVs on MAVLink/Wi-Fi control links, 2.4 GHz for control at shorter range and cellular or satellite for long-distance, long-endurance flights (Papathanasiou et al.); consumer drones (Oli & Mahalal; Nanayakkara et al.); a four-drone multirotor testbed (Dixit et al.). Tlili et al. classify UAVs by size (mini 0.5-2 m), weight (micro < 5 kg, mini 5-25 kg, close range 25-150 kg) and range (very close 5 km, close 50 km, short 150 km). No source of the project gives the speed of UAVs heavier than 25 kg; above 50 km Tlili's classes are much heavier aircraft on other link types.
- *Ranges.* Every range must lie within what the read sources measured, taking the most severe value there and not beyond (the user's rule).
- *Antennas.* Small UAVs were measured with two, three and four antennas (Khawaja et al., Table III); three antennas null two interferers at once (Shebert et al.), and several combined threats carry two emitters.
- *Selection on the test.* The KPI 1 threshold was found on the test split, and the user asked to report quality inside the ranges where the system meets it; both have to be decided before the test is read.
**Decision:**
- *Profile 1 (main system):* UAVs up to Tlili's close-range class; speed 29-161 km/h (slowest measured UAV 8 m/s; small-UAV limit 161 km/h quoted by Khawaja et al.), trained over the whole envelope, with dedicated test flights at its ends (29-50 and 140-161 km/h); three omni antennas over the 1.2 m aperture (4.8 wavelengths apart); fixed interferer directions 42 / -52.2 / 61.3 deg and survivability geometries 42.2 / 11.4 deg (spatial alignment of 45 / 10 deg on a half-wavelength pair, recomputed for three antennas); one antenna at the GCS. Distances are illustrative only, up to 50 km (close-range class).
- *Profile 2 (comparison):* the same envelope with two antennas, run after profile 1. The profiles live on two git branches, profile-3-antennas (the project folder) and profile-2-antennas (its git worktree profile2_2antennas/ inside the project folder; identical code, profile.json sets two antennas), each with its own data and results. The two runs go side by side; their heavy parallel stages take turns (parallel_turn.m). The report presents profile 2 as the two-antenna comparison.
- *Caps from the sources:* K -5..20 dB (Khawaja et al.); in-band power up to 30 dB over our signal (Liu et al., Yuan et al.); attenuation up to 35 dB (airframe shadowing, Khawaja et al.); the unseen-severity test above the training range stops at these values (30 dB, 35 dB).
- *KPI 1 threshold* chosen on the validation split (same rule) and read on the test split; KPI 6 uses that threshold.
- *Operating envelope:* a (threat, severity) is inside a profile's envelope when the deployed policy restores >= 90% of its recoverable validation episodes (at least 10). KPI 4 is reported inside the envelope, and the result over every level beside it; cells outside are the stated limits of the profile.
- *Training scale* (no shortcuts, the envelope widened): 50 000 episodes per DQN run; 8 training and 6 validation geometries per (cell, Eb/N0) in the decision-layer pools.
- *Proposal:* no commitment on speed, distance, weight, size or number of antennas (KPI 7 now reads "robustness to the UAV's speed"; "several receive antennas").
**Adoption:** part of v5 before any test reading; test blocks 10, 11, 12 unread.

## D71 — Unseen-severity rule in profile 2: antenna fault at 70% duty kept as a stated limit
**Why (2026-10-01, with the user):** the rule fixed before the run fired in profile 2 only: antenna fault at 70% duty, 88.3% same countermeasure (106 of 120 frames; 108 needed); profile 1 90.8%. The rule extends the training levels to the survivability map's levels, which are the training levels (duty up to 60%); the decision layer, the map and the evaluation stop at 60%; the gap is inside the sampling uncertainty, and one profile alone would break the comparison.
**Decision:** the training levels stay in both profiles; profile 2 states antenna fault above 60% duty as a limit.

## D72 — v6: evidence over time, a quiet slot, detector levels up to the sources' caps
**Why (2026-10-01, with the user, after the v5 detector reading):** the user asked for solutions, not limits, for the weak detector classes. The v5 reading traced them to three causes:
- *One frame of 0.5 ms decides.* The fading does not change inside it (coherence 1.2-6.6 ms at 29-161 km/h), so a deep fade on one antenna reads as a hidden antenna (clean link F1 84.6%, airframe shadowing 85.9%, weak shadowing 5-7 dB at 16-64%). In the pools the decision cycles were consecutive frames, 0.5 ms apart (frame-to-frame gain correlation 0.82), which also explains why the CNN-LSTM of D13 gained nothing from the sequence.
- *Two threat pairs were the same physics while we transmit.* A reactive jammer is on whenever our continuous link is on; benign interference is continuous noise like jamming, only weaker (jamming F1 88.5%; reactive jamming learned as a new class 50.6%, 54.9% even from 960 frames).
- *The unknown-threat score read "stronger than trained" as "new":* a 30 dB tone, correctly classified, was flagged unknown in 57.5% of its frames, and a frame flagged unknown loses its class in the monitor.
**Decision (v6, branch v6-dev):**
- *Channel clock of one decision cycle per frame* (`cycle_s` 20 ms): the sum-of-sinusoids fading (ours and the interferers') is evaluated at the cycle times, so consecutive frames are one cycle apart as the decisions are (measured frame-to-frame gain correlation -0.25..0.10 vs 0.82). No extra simulation cost.
- *Quiet slot* (`quiet_symbols` 32, 5.8% of air time): the GCS is silent for the last 32 symbols of every frame (the gap between command packets). The UAV measures the channel without our signal: q_iot (interference over thermal in the slot) and q_react (residual while we transmit over the slot). Jammer detection on unused/empty pilots (Pirayesh & Zeng, 2022 survey); a reactive jammer transmits only while it senses our signal (Xu et al.; Sagduyu et al.). Measured at Eb/N0 9 dB: jamming q_iot 28.3 / q_react -0.2 dB, reactive jamming 0.0 / 24.5 dB, tone 22.3 / -2.0, spoofing 9.9 / -1.4, benign 7.1 / 0.0, clean 0.0 / ~0. The slot is longer than the filter span, so no data symbol straddles two frames (the channel moves on inside it) and the receiver outputs each frame's own bits (delay 0). BER without fading equals v5 and theory (0 dB: 7.6e-3 / 7.7e-3 / 7.2e-3).
- *Temporal fusion* (`temporal_evidence.m`, `fuse_classes.m`, stage B2F `select_fusion.m`): the class of a cycle from the last N cycles: the mean log-probability of the per-frame detector, the gap between the antennas' local-mean gains (Lee: local average over 20-40 wavelengths; shadowing lasts seconds, Khawaja et al.), the share of cycles with the same weakest antenna, and the share with quiet-slot interference; a multinomial logistic regression fitted on the validation split, N and the regularization by 5-fold cross-validation over validation sub-runs. The monitor uses it for the class; the agent's state gets the fused probabilities, q_iot, q_react and the three persistence measurements. KPI 1 is read on the decisions of the system (fused), with the per-frame reading beside it.
- *Unknown-threat score over a window:* the score and its averaging window (1, 2, 5, 8 cycles) are chosen together by the leave-one-threat-out procedure, nested estimate quoted.
- *Detector levels up to the sources' most severe values:* in-band threats 0-30 dB (Liu et al.), airframe shadowing 5-35 dB (Khawaja et al.); the unseen-severity test keeps the levels between the training levels and above them only where the sources go further.
- *Severity ranges of the sources* (the user's rule: the most severe value measured, never beyond; detector and decision layer): in-band interferers 30 dB (Liu et al.); airframe shadowing 40 dB (Sun, NASA measurements: up to 40 dB, events of about 30 s; aircraft antenna diversity mitigates it, GS diversity does not); path loss 22 dB (Cui et al. 2019: building blockage adds 21.9 dB, maximum shadow fading 22.4 dB at 4 GHz); spoofer 30 dB, the jammer's hardware (a 0.2-3 dB advantage captures the receiver, Whitehouse et al.; the hijacker uses the jammer's radio, Mekdad et al.).
- *Antenna fault as a connector opening at an airframe vibration frequency* drawn per flight in 86-672 Hz (motor and frame modes of a multirotor, Verbeke & Debruyne), open for the duty cycle, 30 dB while open (an open contact couples only through its gap capacitance: 0.01-0.03 pF at 2.4 GHz in 50 ohm gives 26-36 dB). The previous 5 kHz toggling was faster than any mechanical cause.
- *Benign interference as WLAN packet traffic* (user approval): packets of 0.27-3.2 ms (Wollenberg et al.), channel occupancy drawn per flight in 5-86% (Cheema & Salous; Wollenberg et al.), exponential idle gaps, on the channel clock; power up to 30 dB over our signal (EIRP 20 dBm, ETSI EN 300 328, against the link budget; interference grows with altitude, Song et al.). The threat block reports the share of each frame with a packet on the air; dataset frames of a benign sub-run without a packet are labelled clean.
- *PHY validation at the envelope's worst-case Doppler (358 Hz) instead of 1000 Hz:* the 1000 Hz served only to collect independent fades quickly; with the channel clock every frame is an independent fade at any Doppler. At 1000 Hz the channel changes inside the 64-symbol estimation window (BER / theory 1.16, 1.40, 2.24 at 2, 6, 10 dB, K = -5 dB, three antennas), which the perfect-CSI theory does not include; at 358 Hz 1.05, 1.13, 1.31. The receiver's estimation windows now run over the frame's data symbols, the last one taking the remainder (no short window next to the quiet slot).
- *Fourth persistence measurement* drop_w: share of cycles in which one antenna drops (20 dB below the next over the frame, or 15 dB inside it).
- *Weak-point analysis* (analyze_weak_points.m, weak_points.m): every result broken down by every factor with bootstrap intervals and the points each cell pulls the overall result down; weak and strong cells flagged. The dataset keeps each sub-run's K-factor and interferer direction for it.
**Pending:** none of the designed items; C2 of the discussion (input pre-processing of Lee et al.) is not adopted, it multiplies the detector's compute per decision (KPI 7).
**Adoption:** before any v6 test reading; v6 draws new test blocks (dataset seed stream 2027; pool blocks 14, 15, 16).

## D73 — v7: a real receiver, flight dynamics and source-corrected severities
**Why (2026-10-01, with the user):** a review of the whole source library (docs/v7_source_review.md, docs/v7_research_notes/) found that the method rests on sources that hold up, but several severity caps and shapes went beyond what the cited sources measured; the ideal receiver (known timing, frequency and symbols) hid how a real receiver fails; hover and turns were missing from the flight envelope. The user's rules: everything RF and only RF (detect, monitor and overcome threats; flight conditions only through their RF effect); where no source backs a value, lower it to what a source backs and state the rest as the system's limit; a good system states its envelope (speed, range, threats).
**Decision:**
- *Frame and receiver.* Quiet slot before the frame (interference measured just before the frame is found) and a guard after it; 802.11-style short and long training (Pirayesh & Zeng) and a pilot block of 4 symbols after every 48 data symbols (802.11: 4 of 52); each radio within ±25 ppm (IEEE 802.11-2007, 18.4.7.4/18.4.7.5), an unknown fractional arrival per frame and the LoS Doppler shift on the air. Synchronization in a whitened domain chosen per frame among the quiet slot, the training region (power inversion, Ogawa et al.) and segment-local power inversion; timing and coarse frequency found jointly on the whole training by an FFT grid search (Morelli & D'Amico; La Pan et al.); residual frequency from the pilot phase progression; first MMSE pass by local whitening; four decision-directed passes weighing the leave-one-out channel with the covariance of the received samples (MVDR), so a wrong decision cannot pull our own direction into it.
- *What the real receiver shows (validate_phy V11, 161 km/h):* 1.0 dB from the ideal receiver on AWGN, 0.8 dB with three antennas; under a 10 dB jammer an error floor the ideal receiver does not have (BER 2e-3 at 10 dB after the MVDR passes, 6.6 dB in the validation before them); against a 30 dB jammer whose channel fades at 222-358 Hz three antennas cannot hold the null (covariance aging: Truong & Heath; Winters-type DMI, US 6,147,985; Shepard et al., Argos), so the decision layer has to answer it (corrected in D75: about half of that loss is the receiver's timing under the jammer). Ground reflection (V12): a specular ray 0.8 of the LoS at the measured 80 ns costs 0.6 dB (ideal) / 0.2 dB (real), so the flat-fading model holds; the receiver has no equalizer for a strong late echo, which the air-ground measurements do not show (late components more than 25 dB down, Sun).
- *Flight envelope.* Speed 0-161 km/h (rotorcraft measured while hovering, Khawaja et al.; Gomez-Ponce et al.; 161 km/h is the FAA small-UAV limit quoted by Khawaja et al.); heading turns per flight at a coordinated-turn rate up to the largest bank angle measured on a small UAV (57.9°, Gross et al.) and never above its largest measured yaw rate (28.7°/s, Allen & Lin); the directions of the GCS and of every interferer rotate across the array. Low edge-speed band 0-21 km/h.
- *Severity corrections (user approval):* spoofer up to 30 dB justified by geometry, a counterfeit GCS on the same radio as ours (Mekdad et al.) about 31 times closer to the UAV (capture from a 0.2-3 dB advantage, Whitehouse et al.); airframe shadowing as measured events, depth 6-25 dB (median 15.5 ± 4.9 dB at C-band, Sun et al.) with the hidden antenna's K-factor at -16 dB, instead of a constant 40 dB (a fading peak); receive correlation drawn per flight in 0.3-0.9 (the 0.3 had no source; on an aircraft underside the amplitudes, LoS included, correlate 0.85-0.99, Sun); antenna fault = a connector open for the flight (Fedde & Carter, US 4,506,385; opens are the most common connector failure, Ginart et al.), 26-36 dB stated as a gap-capacitance estimate (no measured depth exists; the 86-672 Hz vibration model is dropped: those are frame modes, Verbeke & Debruyne); WLAN idle gaps gamma-distributed with the field shape 0.49 (Cheema & Salous, where exponential fits worst), occupancy 5-86% (Wollenberg et al., lab cases; field 4.6-11.5%), level from the ETSI EN 300 328 limits and geometry, cap 30 dB.
- *Labels.* An antenna-fault frame in which the contact never opened was labelled a fault; frames are now labelled by what is on the air (as benign frames without a packet).
- *Hardware.* A gain and phase error per receive chain, fixed per flight (0.1 dB / 1°, Bakr); the adaptive combiner absorbs it. I/Q imbalance of an AD9361-class transceiver (0.2 % / 0.2°, image about 54 dB down) leaves even a 30 dB jammer's image far below our signal.
- *Unknown-threat score.* A new candidate with the input pre-processing of Lee et al. (step chosen on validation against FGSM versions); the leave-one-threat-out selection decides. If it is chosen, its extra gradient pass is measured in the latency stage (KPI 7, see D72).
- *Combined threats.* Four triples added (three emitters; two emitters with a lost antenna; a jammer in a banking turn at long range; a tone and a spoofer in a turn: Liu et al. "one or several jammers"; Yang et al.); every combination is also measured left out of training.
- *Overhead pass (stage OHP):* the dipole null below the UAV (-30 dB measured, Badi et al.) against the shorter distance: every antenna loses alike, up to about 23 dB below the cruise level at 300 m altitude, i.e. path loss within its trained levels.
- *Directional GCS antenna in every profile (stage GCSA, user decision; an earlier version of this entry had dropped it)*, under the rule that applies in Israel (Ministry of Communications exemption: 100 mW e.i.r.p. at 2.4 GHz, as ETSI EN 300 328, whose 10 mW in any 1 MHz caps our signal at 10.2 dBm, 95.5% of its power in its central 1 MHz (eirp_cap_dbm.m; corrected in D75); the stricter reading where unclear). The cap limits the radiated power, not the antenna: a directional antenna helps as far as the radio stays below the cap. The GCS is an AD9361-class radio (0.25 dB steps, up to 7.5 dBm for a CW tone at 2.4 GHz; corrected in D75) at -6 dBm, raised by its largest step under the cap as power_control, on a tracked 12 dBi antenna (the ground antenna of Rodriguez-Pineiro et al.'s air-ground measurements): 6 dBm e.i.r.p., 10 dB over an omni on the same radio, i.e. 10 dB less jammer-to-signal ratio for the same jammer and 3.2 times the range. The power step adds 4.0 dB, up to the cap (power_step_db.m; it was +6 dB on a profile A already at the cap, a breach of the cap, corrected). Pointing: the measured mean errors 5.62 deg azimuth / 1.51 deg elevation with a flying quadcopter (Nugroho & Dectaviansyah), half-normal, ITU-R F.1336 main lobe, drawn per flight and applied in the channel (gcs_pointing.m, block 'GCS'): 0.4 dB on average; no source gives the error versus speed or range, so no separate lag term. A lost target falls back to the omni (Boeing, US 8,503,941): 10 dB, inside the path-loss levels. No K-factor gain is claimed: a directional ground antenna lowered K in one measured environment and was mixed in the other (Rodriguez-Pineiro et al.). Eb/N0 includes the antenna gain; link-budget profile A is this GCS with its omni fallback.
- *Proposal review (2026-10-03, before any v7 reading):* the deployed policy is the DQN with escalation only when it passed its validation gate (recovery >= rule + escalation, false-alarm bound), otherwise the rule with escalation, as the proposal says; KPI 5 always compares the DQN itself with the rule. A reference policy without the detector (rule + escalation, alarm from measured degradation only) shows what the detection adds. The GUI and the latency stage run the decision layer as evaluated (temporal fusion, unknown-score window, antenna gains). Latency (KPI 7) is measured on the idle machine after each version's runs, then KPI, DASH, WEAK and RPT again. Survivability options per profile (one antenna fewer, relay on the system's array).
- *Proposal v4, approved by the user (2026-10-03):* deliverable 2 names the tracked directional GCS antenna, the variable number of UAV antennas and the real receiver, and the antenna profile is selectable in the operator console: each profile exports a bundle (export_gui_bundle.m: trained models, normalisation, fusion, unknown-score model, results; no dataset files) into profiles/rx<n> of the main checkout, and the console's 'UAV antennas' menu reopens on it (n_rx, the profile's directions, the model rebuilt). Deliverable 7 becomes the operating envelope and the survivability boundary: at every point (threat and level, combinations, Eb/N0, speed, altitude, number of antennas) the detection and recovery rates with a 95% interval, inside the envelope a commitment as the KPIs define it, outside none; plus where some configuration restores the link (antenna count, relay). This needs the per-point stage with verdicts fixed before any reading and a per-flight altitude axis within the measured ranges, entering only through its RF effect (both in design, to land before the v7 runs).
- *Proposal v5 (approved by the user 2026-10-03, applied by the user's other session):* abstract: RF jamming is possible with available SDR equipment [1], and the project maps the system's operating envelope; deliverable 5 cites Double DQN [8], [9] and the shield [11]; deliverable 6 cites antenna nulling to Winters, Salz & Gitlin [13]; mitigation 3: balanced sub-runs, WLAN frames without a packet labelled clean, class-weighted loss and selection by macro-F1; risk and mitigation 20: the licence-exempt e.i.r.p. cap bounds the power action and the directional antenna, the GCS transmits at low power; risk and mitigation 21: hover freezes the channel, answered by separated antennas and a separate hover band in the envelope. For combined threats, detection in the envelope counts a component class (combinations are not detector classes).
- *Profile 3 (four antennas):* fixed directions [42.4 -42.3 62.7]° and survivability geometries [42.6 17.5]°, the alignments of the other profiles on the same 1.2 m aperture.
- *Corrections to earlier entries:* "N antennas null N-1 interferers" is Winters, Salz & Gitlin (1994), not Shebert; Shebert's hybrid is a decoder cascade, not our feature fusion; Yuan's 30 dBm vs 0 dBm are transmit powers, not a received JSR; Liu's comb jams three tones, not every channel (D70); Yu 2020 is withdrawn and is not cited; the preemptive shield's guarantee covers converging tabular learners, not a DQN; the hold rule is backed for frequency changes only (W03); history length 4 rests on the jammer's memory (Yuan; S14), Mnih's 4 is one of 3-5.
**Adoption:** v7 code frozen before any v7 test reading; v6 runs to the end as the ideal-receiver reference; v7 runs profile 1, then profile 3, profile 2 last.

## D74 — v7 envelope: a verdict at every point, altitude and distance
**Why (2026-10-03/04, with the user):** deliverable 7 of proposal v5 is the operating envelope: at every point a rate with its 95% interval and, inside the envelope, a commitment as the KPIs define it. That needs verdict rules fixed before any reading, enough flights per point, an altitude axis that enters only through its RF effect, and a distance next to every Eb/N0. The review of the v7 code before launch also found: the K-factor and the heading rate drawn from the same stream of a flight (both at seed + 23); the survivability-options stage reading its frames back under the run ids of another block; the survivability map using the same seed at every Eb/N0; in-band levels with path loss on top reaching 41 dB over our received signal, beyond the 30 dB the sources measured; and a string broken across two lines in validate_phy.m that stopped stage A4v (fixed first).
**Decision:**
- *Altitude, drawn per flight in 15-120 m.* Top: 120 m, the FAA small-UAV ceiling (122 m) of the same rule as the 161 km/h cap (Khawaja et al.; Cui et al. give 120 m). Bottom: 15 m, the lowest height with a full 2.5 GHz channel table (Rodriguez-Pineiro et al., Tables V-IX), above the 0-11 m below-roofline band of Cui et al. It reaches the link only through the UAV dipole's gain toward the GCS (flight_draws.m, the 'GCS' block): elevation asind((h − 10 m mast) / d) at the distance d of the Eb/N0, a half-wave dipole floored at −30 dB (Badi et al. 2019), steeper at every angle than the measured cos²θ of Badi et al. 2020, so the more severe choice; at most 1.04 dB inside the grid (120 m at 15 dB). No altitude term, by source: K (Rodriguez-Pineiro et al., Table IX: 10.9-16.3 dB over 15-105 m, no trend; K stays drawn per flight), Doppler spread (falls with height, so Jakes up to fd bounds it), shadow-fading σ, delay spread, airframe-shadowing depth, interference level. Interferers get no pattern term (a ground emitter sits lower: the severe case); no cos(elevation) in the steering vectors (the GCS at broadside; the uniform ±90° interferer draw covers every cone angle).
- *Distance next to every Eb/N0* (link_distance_km.m): profile A, the tracked GCS (−6 dBm on 12 dBi), UAV 2 dBi, noise figure 5 dB, margin 10 dB, 2 Mb/s, free space: 0/3/6/9/12/15 dB are 1.58/1.12/0.79/0.56/0.40/0.28 km slant (×0.316 on the omni fallback), −3 dB 2.2 km, 18 dB 0.20 km. The power step is not on the km axis (power_control is an action of the policy). Every position of a flight holds only to 0.63 of an edge distance: a two-ray null at ρ = 0.8 is 14 dB (Welling, 2.3 GHz, via Sun), 4 dB beyond the margin. The 0.6 F1 clearance (from 10.2-39.1 km) and the radio horizon (29.0-58.2 km) at 15-120 m never bind (link_budget_table.m, results/link_budget.mat); profile B is printed at 120 m as an illustration, never a commitment.
- *In-band cap 30 dB* (inband_cap_db, inband_cap_amp.m): every additive component reaches at most 30 dB over our received signal (Liu et al.), whatever path loss, pointing or elevation loss our signal has. The cap acts on the component's level before any countermeasure (p.inband_ref): path loss is the same under every configuration, so a capped emitter keeps one power and a countermeasure still lowers it by its own amount.
- *Seeds:* one stream per purpose per flight (seed_stream.m): 64·mod(seed, 2^26) + offset (channel 0, AWGN 1, bits 2, threat 7, interferer directions 11, K 23, heading rate 29, correlation 31, GCS pointing 41, altitude 47, speed 53), so no stream serves two purposes or two flights. Dataset seeds 3 000 000 + run, stored per sub-run; unseen Eb/N0 4 000 000 + k; unseen severity 5 000 000 + k; survivability map 900 000 + 1000 threat + 10 level + Eb/N0 index (every column its own flights); survivability options run id 1300 + r at build and read-back (surv3_run_id.m); a model built without a seed draws one below 2^26. Pool blocks: 17 the second test, 18 and 19 the reduced chain check (run_stage smoke), never test flights.
- *Pools (C1p):* a second test split (block 17) of 60 geometries per (cell, Eb/N0) for the clean link and the single threats: 72 flights per point with the test (12). The combined threats fly the test split and 24 second-test geometries of their own: 36 per point (adopted below). The edge-speed split flies every level of the single threats and the clean link at exact hover (24 per Eb/N0; heading rate 0, since no source gives a yaw rate at hover) and the nominal level at 161 km/h (12): 36 geometries, the first 24 at hover. The test and second test geometries are nested Latin hypercubes over speed 0-161 km/h, altitude 15-120 m, K of our signal −5..20 dB, the first interferer's broadside angle over [−90, 90]° and the receive correlation over 0.3-0.9 (permutation streams 7 100 000 + 100 Eb/N0 index + split): 2 + 10 flights per sixth of the speed range and of the angle range and 4 + 20 per third of the altitude range and of the correlation range at every Eb/N0, the value inside a stratum the flight's own draw; K of the interferers and the other interferers' directions keep the full range. flight_draws.m is the one source of a flight's draws for the pools, link_seed.m and the records: PP.cov per geometry (seed, speed, altitude, both K, heading rate, correlation, pointing loss, elevation gain, directions and their alignment with the GCS), Q.act per frame (the threat's on-air share), per dataset sub-run seed, altitude, elevation gain, K of the interferers, heading rate, correlation, GCS loss and the on-air share. PP.model_tag 'v7-D74' guards the reuse of train and validation pools. *[Amended 30.10.2026 by D117 (registered under decide-after-30min, Adi silent since 30.10.2026 19:37; profile 3 only): the edge-speed split flies 24 geometries per Eb/N0 at 161 km/h, 48 in all with the first 24 at hover; profile 1's split stays as registered.]*
- *Stage EDGE* (edge_map.m, edge_verdict.m), after GCSA and before KPI; KPI reads results/edge_map.mat, and EDGE only reads saved results, so it can rerun alone. The deployed policy as evaluate_policies.m picks it (the same policy_setup.m, policy_episodes.m, policy_run_set.m), with no response, rule + escalation, the one-step oracle, the random policy and the receiver alone beside it. Layers and targets: DET, fused detection of the deployed detector on the threat-active frames at no_action (a WLAN frame with on-air share below 0.1 and a fault frame with the contact closed count as clean) ≥ 90%; SURV, flights some configuration recovers ≥ 90%; REC, held recovery among the recoverable flights (below; C2e keeps KPI 4's "recovered") ≥ 90%; FA, clean-link flights with a configuration change (KPI 6) ≤ 5%; LINK, clean-link packet loss at no_action ≤ 1.4% (802.11's PER < 10% at a 1000-byte PSDU, Keysight AN Table 7, BER 1.3e-5, on our 129-byte frames); SYSTEM = DET, SURV and REC all COMMITTED (the proposal's relative criterion); SYSTEM+LINK its absolute version; the commitment itself is PROT, DET_h, FA, LINK and LINK_T (below). Level basis: the spoofer at 30 dB, the open connector and WLAN at 30 dB are assumed levels, the others measured; pool levels the detector never trained on are marked. *[Amended 2026-10-29 by D108 (Adi's approval): LINK ≤ 5.7%, 802.11a's PER < 10% at a 1000-byte PSDU carried to the 1032-bit frame through the simulated block fading; the verdict at 1.4% printed beside, reported only.]*
- *Interval and verdict* (edge_verdict.m): two-sided 95%, percentile bootstrap over flights (B = 4000, fixed seed). A per-flight binary outcome (a flight succeeds only if all its episodes do) takes the exact Clopper-Pearson interval when every flight has one unit, where both describe the same share, otherwise the hull of the two. A rate takes the hull of the bootstrap and Clopper-Pearson on its effective sample size (below). A rate at its extreme has no bootstrap spread: the lower bound of DET is at most the Clopper-Pearson bound of n successful flights in n, the upper bound of LINK at least that of no lost frame in all its frames (a flight-level bound of 0 in n would keep LINK above 1.4% below 262 flights, so it could never commit). COMMITTED: the interval on the target's side and n ≥ N_MIN = 36, the smallest n whose all-success bound reaches 0.90 (0.9026; 35 gives 0.89997); NOT COMMITTED: the interval entirely on the other side; UNDETERMINED otherwise. At 72 flights COMMITTED takes ≤ 2 failures and NOT COMMITTED ≥ 14; at 36, 0 and ≥ 8; FA at a point's 172 clean flights (72 of the pools and 100 of C1c), ≤ 2 and ≥ 16, and at the 688 of four points ≤ 23 alarms (the commitment's FA, below). At 72 flights a true rate of 0.99 commits 96% of the time, 0.97 63%, 0.95 30%; 0.80 is NOT COMMITTED 59% of the time. *[Amended 2026-10-29 by D108: REC and PROT per onset under the rate rule (D108); the per-flight reading printed beside, reported only.]*
- *Bands and edges:* per (threat, level) over the Eb/N0 from the KPI 1 threshold up (fixed on validation): sixths of the speed range, thirds of the altitude range, 5-dB K bands from −5 dB, the lowest also from −3 dB (the lowest in-flight K near 2.4 GHz, Aoki et al.), hover and 161 km/h; the same for FA and LINK. Edges for every layer apart, fixed-sequence walks that stop at the first point not COMMITTED, each "up to" claim at 5% family error: Eb/N0 from 15 dB down (its km, ×0.63, and the first NOT COMMITTED point), levels from the nominal one down and up (below), the run of speed and of altitude bands around the nominal band, K down from 15-20 dB; a COMMITTED point beyond a NOT COMMITTED one is flagged.
- *Overhead pass (OHP):* the altitude grid against the cruise distance of every Eb/N0; worst drop of the pass with h − mast (overhead_drop_db.m: 15.6 dB at 120 m and 0.56 km; 22.9 dB at 300 m without a mast, as D73), plus 0.37 dB with the tracker on target and 10 dB with the tracker lost. EDGE reads the path_loss commitment (at the model's signalling delay) at the smallest level at or above the drop, at the Eb/N0 of the cruise distance: COMMITTED when the tracker-lost bound is, NOT COMMITTED when the tracked bound is not; a drop above 22 dB is beyond the measured levels, no commitment.
- *Detector beyond the trained ranges:* B4 adds −3 dB (2.2 km) and 18 dB (0.20 km), 36 blocks per class with the levels cycled, read fused and labelled by what is on the air as the dataset is; detector-only verdicts. B4s reads fused and stays generalization evidence (fewer than 36 blocks, UNDETERMINED by construction). The policy is not measured beyond 0-15 dB; no level above the top one is simulated (every top level is the source cap).
- *Weak points:* every set from its own block, the bootstrap over flights (geometry and split), K from PP.cov.
- *PHY check V12b,* informational: a specular ray of 0.8 at 480 ns (234 ns RMS, the median of Rodriguez-Pineiro et al.'s environment II) against V12's 80 ns.
- *Corrections:* D72's "interference grows with altitude (Song et al.)" holds for cellular interference only (Song et al. and AERPAW measure cellular networks); the WLAN and jammer levels get no altitude term. The K row −5..10 dB of Khawaja et al. (Table VI) is Tu & Shimamoto 2009, an airport at 5.75 GHz, not an urban study; the 12 dBi GCS antenna is the gain class of Rodriguez-Pineiro et al.'s fixed sector antenna, here on a tracker.

**The user's decisions (2026-10-03):** 72 flights per point (second test 60 geometries; the 36-flight option, second test 24, is not taken); the K floor stays −5 dB, with the K ≥ −3 dB reading beside it; the LINK target 1.4%. *[2026-10-29: LINK target amended by D108 with the user's approval of 2026-10-29.]*

**Cost per profile:** the second test 349 920 and the edge speeds 19 584 more simulations at about 0.26 s: C1p about 51 h [37-61] instead of 24 h; the pool file about 4 GB (peak memory: two loads of the pools and the other profile's six C1p workers, about 25 of 31 GB); the three C1p runs take turns (parallel_turn.m), about +80 h for the campaign.

**Adopted by the user (2026-10-04):** 24 second-test geometries of their own for the combined threats, so every combination point has 12 + 24 = 36 flights and can commit (N_MIN): +146 880 simulations, +10.6 h per profile, about +32 h for the campaign, the pool file about 4.8 GB (build_policy_pools.m: NCOMB(5) = 24, the second test among the combined cells' splits).

**Cost and resources, re-derived (2026-10-16; the figures above stay as they were written, this note replaces them for planning):**
- *Measured basis.* The first full v7 reading (2026-10-06 to 10-08, D80's code; uav-gcs-logs: interrupted_20261006/, full_v7p1.log, full_v7p3.log), profile 1 (profile 3): A4v 361.7 min (504.2; the two ran side by side), A5 177.5 (178.8), A6 33.2 (32.6), B1 3.6 (3.2), B2 21.1 (32.6), B2F 3.7 (3.6), B3 1.2 (1.0), B3a 46.3 (73.5), B4 32.7 (36.8), OOD 237.4 (222.6), B4s 18.1 (25.3); C1p had finished 34 of 93 cells, about 49% of its simulations, after 37.0 h: about 75 h in all. v6 (D72's code, flat channel), profile 1: C2 363.5 min, C2e 5.6, C2g 155.1, C2u 149.5, SURV 101.9, SURV3 143.2 (profile 2: C2 349.9, C2g 155.2, C2u 155.6, SURV3 162.8; its SURV logged 295.4 min with a wait for the parallel turn inside). LAT 0.6-5.5 min; KPI, DASH, WEAK and RPT under 1 min in every log.
- *Factors since.* D75's delay line: a flight costs 1.28-1.88× the flat one (D75's cost; on v6's simulating stages only, the first reading had it). D84: A5 +3% sub-runs, C1p 980 784 simulations instead of 960 336 (×1.021), 1.751 s per simulation on the shadowing smoke against D83's 1.634 (×1.07), C2 trains twice the runs (d84_dec.md). D85: the idle model ×1.10 (D85's cost) and up to ×1.24 (2.17-2.28 s per simulation on two concurrent D85 smokes); B3n at 4 of A5's 6 sub-runs per cell. A simulating stage of the first reading therefore takes ×1.18-1.33 now.
- *Per profile (profile 1; main.m gives them at its flags).* A4v 6-8 h (measured beside profile 3's, ×1.33 at most); A5 3.6-4.0 h (177.5 min × 1.03 × 1.18-1.33); A6 about 35 min; B1 about 4 min; B2 20-35 min; B2F about 4 min; B3n 2.8-3.2 h (0.67 × (A5 + A6) + B1); B3 about 2 min; B3a 45-75 min; B4 40-50 min; OOD 3.7-4 h; B4s 20-35 min. C1p 90-105 h (75 h × 1.021 × 1.18-1.33 = 90-102 h; cross-check 980 784 × 2.17-2.28 s over 6 workers = 98-104 h). C1c 11-13 h (2 × 300 × 6 × 12 + 68 688 = 111 888 simulations at that rate, plus three loads of the pool file). C2 about 12 h (v6's 350-363 min, twice the runs) *[Correction 2026-10-18: `train_dqn.m` on 9b5c9f1 trains 72 runs (4 monitors × 2 drop thresholds × 3 false-switch penalties × 3 seeds), four times v6's 18. At v6's 18-20 min per run C2 takes about 22-24 h (d90_loop.md sec. 7), and about 38-42 h with d90's 108 runs (D90).]*; C2e 0.5-1.5 h (v6's 5.6 min with 72 instead of 12 test flights per point, plus the clean and edge-speed sets: an estimate); C2g about 3.6 h and C2u about 2.5 h (v6's 155 and 150-156 min, C2g × 14/10 combinations: estimates); SURV 2.6-4.2 h and SURV3 3.6-6 h (v6's 101.9 and 143.2 min × 1.28-1.88 × 1.18-1.33); EDGE 0.5-2 h (an estimate; the smokes took 0.2-0.8 min); LAT 1-6 min. Profile 3 about ×1.0-1.2 on the parallel stages (first-reading ratios: A5 1.01, B4 1.13, A4v 1.39), profile 2 about ×0.85-1.0.
- *Totals.* A profile alone: A0-B4s 19-23 h, the parallel block (C1p, C1c, SURV, SURV3) 107-128 h, C1d-RPT 19-22 h (C2 11.7-12.1, C2e 0.5-1.5, C2g 3.6, C2u 2.5, EDGE 0.5-2, the other nine stages 0.3 h at most together): 145-173 h, about 6-7.2 days. *[Correction 2026-10-18: with C2 at 22-24 h (the bracket above), C1d-RPT is about 29-34 h per profile, and 45-52 h with d90's grid. Profile 1's C1p also runs slower than these figures (D91 part 5).]* The campaign (the launcher runs the three parallel blocks one after another, the other stages beside them): gate 0.5 h + profile 1's A0-B4s + the blocks of profiles 1, 3 and 2 (107-128, 107-154, 91-128 h) + profile 2's C1d-RPT + the final LAT, KPI and bundles 1.5 h = 345-457 h, 14.4-19.0 days. The pre-launch review's figures (345-450 h) differ mainly in SURV and SURV3, which it took without the delay-line factor. The Windows Update pause read on 2026-10-16 ends 2026-11-05 13:38 UTC, inside that span: the updates are installed and the pause renewed before the launch. *[Correction 2026-10-18: this was not done. The user decided on 2026-10-16 not to install the updates before the pause ends; the resume mode and the C1p checkpoints cover a reboot (D86, "Launch").]*
- *Pools in memory and on disk.* 40.2 M stored frames per profile: 8 150 flights per Eb/N0 (1 296 of them 38-frame roll-in flights, the rest 20 frames) × 6 Eb/N0 × 36 configurations, at 416 B per frame on profile 1, 456 on profile 3 and 376 on profile 2: the 308 B measured on the D85 smoke's pools (class probabilities 11, link features 23, antenna gains 3 and the idle record 24 singles, plus the scalars; that smoke predates the channel record), the antennas' channel records, 9 × n_rx singles (branch_record.m, pool_cell.m; D85's cues), and one gain per antenna. About 16.7 GB in memory on profile 1, 18.4 GB on profile 3, 15.1 GB on profile 2; on disk (-v7.3) 1.0-1.26 times that (the smoke's file held 1.26 times its in-memory pools, whose short entries carry more header): about 17-21 GB on profile 1, 15-23 GB across the profiles; the K tables about 0.7 GB each, two in C2 (they do not hold the channel records).
- *Peak memory.* At the review's base (a5f0687) the side-by-side stages exceed the 31.2 GB of RAM: the next profile's C1p (six workers of about 2 GB, the client gathering the pools) beside this profile's C2-EDGE, each holding the pools and its K tables, about 38-42 GB; profile 2's B1 (spectrograms 7.0 GB and splits 7.1 GB in memory) beside profile 3's C1p, about 35-42 GB for minutes (the review's 32-35 and 34-40 GB at 12.3 GB of pools, with C2's pools and the C1p client's 3-5 GB scaled to the sizes above). The 52 GB page file (83 GB commit limit) prevents an out-of-memory stop; paging slows the stages. *[Correction 2026-10-18: the page file is system-managed and grows. On 2026-10-17 at 23:45 it was 98 GB (three times the RAM) and the commit limit 127 GiB (136.3 GB), a fixed ceiling from then on (docs/runs/analysis/overnight_audit.txt:108). Profile 3's OOD still stopped out of memory at 22:02 that day, with four large MATLAB jobs at once (PROJECT_LOG, 2026-10-17 22:02).]* The review's memory item (ops-6) lowers these.
- *Disk.* Profile 1 writes about 42-48 GB: the dataset 2.17 GB, spectrograms 5.58, splits 5.60 and the detector 0.91 (14.3 GB, measured on the first reading), the second test set about 0.67 of the first three (8.9 GB), the pools 17-21 GB, the clean and check pools about 1.9-2.4 GB (111 888 against C1p's 980 784 simulations), agents and results under 1 GB; the three profiles about 126-143 GB (profile 3 44-50 GB, profile 2 40-45 GB, by their pools). disk_guard.m stops below 150 GB free (D61's note), so a launch needs about 295 GB free; drive C: held 2.6 TB free on 2026-10-16.

**Stated limits:** above 120 m; below 15 m (the 22 dB path-loss level reads as "near ground / below roofline", Cui et al.); low altitude beyond about 0.6 km has no 2-2.7 GHz measurement and is carried by physics; LoS probability against altitude (ITU-R P.1410, Al-Hourani) is not in the library; the airframe-shadowing statistics hold at 1.4-2.9° elevation (Sun) *(restated 2026-10-16, D86 item 1: airframe shadowing is flown at Sun et al.'s burial; the GCS elevation itself, up to about 23°, lies outside 1.4-2.9°, and the pairing takes the event's deepest burial with the station abeam, as their U-turns passed it)*; the tracker's error against the angular rate is not measured; two-ray nulls deeper than 14 dB; hover is a frozen channel with yaw 0, without the LoS fades of more than 10 dB within milliseconds that wind gives a hovering drone (Lin 2026); a jammer during an overhead pass (its level plus the drop exceeds 30 dB) is not simulated.

**Tests:** on the real receiver the spatial coherence of the clean link is our own signal's residual after the channel estimate: 0.30 at 12 dB and 0.80 at 24 dB over five flights, 0.04-0.07 with the ideal receiver, at 160 Hz and at hover alike. test_receiver_measurements therefore compares five clean flights with a 4 dB jammer on the same flights (0.30 against 0.54; the test asks for a gap of at least 0.15) instead of a fixed bound.

**Checks before launch (2026-10-04):** unit tests 27/27; the reduced chain check (run_stage smoke: A0 A4v A5 A6 B1 B2 B2F B3 B4 B4s C1p C1c C1d C2 C2e SURV SURV3 OHP EDGE KPI RPT, 4.3 h, from an empty data/) ran through: five splits, altitudes 17-119 m in PP.cov, hover flights at speed 0 with heading rate 0, jamming+path_loss and tone_jamming+path_loss at the high level (23 dB with 18 dB of path loss) capped at 30 dB, non-zero survivability options, the edge-map files written. Its data are in archive/smoke_20261004/ (the earlier smoke data in archive/smoke_20261001/), so C1p starts from nothing. V12b (informational) shows an error floor: a 0.8 ray 480 ns late leaves BER 5e-2 at 10 dB with the ideal receiver (80 ns: +0.49 dB), so a specular ray that strong at that delay is beyond the flat-fading model; open.

**Gap review before launch, decision layer and pools (2026-10-04; the user approved every recommendation of the review):**
- *Follower jammer and the signalling delay* (link_env.m; the same rule in the oracle of rollout_policy.m, oracle_action.m, and in v3d_engine.m): the hop in use and a requested hop are kept apart. A hop travels with its request through the signalling delay; until it reaches the link the old, re-acquired channel stays jammed, and no second hop is requested while one is on its way. A channel_switch configuration that is kept asks for a re-hop when the frame just received failed its CRC (what the UAV sees of the channel in use, frame by frame), at the switch cost. Before, the re-hop moved the follower's clock at the decision, so the D frames still sent on the jammed channel counted as clean (a held channel switch gave 50/75/83/86% good cycles at follower delays 0/2/4/5 instead of 0/50/67/71%), and the true state triggered it. Now a held channel switch gets fdelay good and D + 1 jammed cycles per re-acquisition, none at fdelay 0, and a 5-cycle run by hopping alone only from fdelay 5. The monitor's degraded flag is not a trigger: its window still holds frames of the channel before the hop, so it would ask for hops on old evidence and bring back runs longer than fdelay. v6 keeps the old rule: its follower and comb numbers are optimistic and not comparable with v7.
- *Hold and escalation from the arrival* (policy_monitor.m, the same monitor in training and evaluation): while the configuration requested has not reached the link, the cycles since the change stay at 0, so neither the hold nor the escalation counts; the first frame received with it restarts the BER and CRC windows and the degraded count. Coded configurations are degraded with two lost packets in the window (as before with a full window).
- *False alarms in the deployment decision* (train_dqn.m, choose_deployed.m, false_change_rate.m): the gate and the selection count false alarms at the Eb/N0 of KPI 6, from the KPI 1 threshold up (all six points when it is NaN), one 30-cycle episode per clean flight (C2e's clean set too, instead of 8). When the DQN fails its gate, rule + escalation is deployed on its own monitor and drop threshold (rule_sel: the best recovery among the grid's settings whose one-sided 95% false-alarm bound is ≤ 5%); when no setting meets the bound, the policy with the lowest bound is deployed, and unless that bound is within 5% the edge map commits no point (F among the limits of SYSTEM). A bound not measured (no clean validation flights) is not met. KPI 5 keeps the same-monitor comparison. Beside the share of episodes: false changes per cycle and per hour of flight at 20 ms, with the 95% upper bound (bootstrap over flights, at least Clopper-Pearson over all cycles), and the mean time between them.
- *Latin hypercube over direction and correlation* (pool_geometries.m): the test, second test and check designs also stratify the first interferer's broadside angle over [−90, 90]° and the receive correlation over 0.3-0.9 (2 + 10 per sixth of the angle range and 4 + 20 per third of the correlation range at every Eb/N0), the value inside a stratum the flight's own draw; the other interferers keep theirs. Every Eb/N0 column gets its share of directions close to the GCS and of correlated flights.
- *Hover at every level* (build_policy_pools.m): the edge-speed split flies 24 hover flights per Eb/N0 at every level of the single threats and for the clean link, and 12 at 161 km/h at the nominal level (36 geometries, the first 24 at hover). A hover band of a (threat, level) over four Eb/N0 points has 96 flights and is COMMITTED with at most 3 failures. *[Amended 30.10.2026 by D117 (registered under decide-after-30min, Adi silent since 30.10.2026 19:37; profile 3 only): the edge-speed split flies 24 geometries per Eb/N0 at 161 km/h, 48 in all with the first 24 at hover; profile 1's split stays as registered.]*
- *Off-grid check flights* (build_check_pools.m, stage C1c, data/check_pools.mat): test only, 36 flights per point, seeds of their own family (pool_seed.m family 2, block 10; permutation streams 7 200 000 + 100 × Eb/N0 index), the detector of the pools. Every single threat at the nominal level at 1.5/4.5/7.5/10.5/13.5 dB; the midpoint of every two neighbouring levels at 9 dB (jammers 7/13/19.5/26.5 dB, path loss 8/12/16/20 dB, spoofer −1.5/1.5/7.5/21 dB, WLAN −7.5/−2.5/6/21 dB, open connector 27.25/29.75/32.25/34.75 dB, shadowing 10/13.75/17.75/22.5 dB); the clean link at every check Eb/N0 as the restoration reference. Rule (edge_map.m, check_point_cut.m): an "up to" edge in distance or level that spans a check point NOT COMMITTED stops before it. An edge that spans no check point (levels at another Eb/N0 than 9 dB, distance at another level than the nominal) holds at the grid points it lists only, and the report says so. A check point's FA is that of the claim from the grid's clean flights; its own 36 clean flights only must not be NOT COMMITTED.
- *Unknown threat* (experiment_unknown_threat.m, C2u): every leave-one-out agent is kept (data/unknown_threat_agents.mat) and read on the test and second test flights (72 per point), every episode saved. Evidence only: no rate is committed for an emitter outside the modelled classes.
- *Jammer on every channel* (link_env.m spec.comb; evaluate_policies.m, edge_map.m): it also takes frequency diversity's second carrier (the configuration acts without it, at its spectrum cost), and recoverable there means some configuration without channel switch and frequency diversity restores the link. An edge-map row of its own, level basis assumed: no source jams every channel. A follower delay of 0 is a single-channel follower on the new channel at the hop itself, not Liu et al.'s comb.
- *Detector identity* (detector_id.m, check_det_id.m): PP.det_id hashes the detector's training time, the unknown-threat score and its window, the fusion and the unknown-threat threshold. Pools of another detector are never reused; the agent stores it with the pools' creation time; C2e, C2g, C2u and EDGE stop when the agent and the pools (and the check pools) differ; C2u stamps the pools' identity on its leave-one-out agents only after that check.
- *Outside the commitment* (user decision): attacks aware of our protocol (on the training, the pilots or the frame timing) and a counterfeit that sends our full frame timed to ours (false-preamble capture, La Pan et al. 2012) are not modelled; the spoofer of the envelope is a counterfeit on our channel, modulation and pulse shape that does not reproduce our training or frame timing. Resisting them needs keyed or randomised training (La Pan et al. 2013), future work. Approved v7 Steps 2 and 6 are excluded on this basis.
- *Cost per profile, at 0.26 s per simulation [0.19-0.31]:* hover at every level +125 280 simulations, +9.0 h [6.6-10.8] in C1p; check flights +64 800, +4.7 h [3.4-5.6] in C1c; both before the delay-spread factor of the channel. The other changes need no simulation.
- *Tests:* test_follower_rehop_delay, test_oracle_timing (the oracle's predicted configuration is the link's on the arrival frame, at D = 0/1/2/7), test_comb_jammer, test_escalation_after_arrival, test_coded_degraded_after_arrival, test_policy_own_monitor, test_choose_deployed (a bound not measured is not met), test_false_change_rate, test_check_point_cut, test_detector_id (C2u and C2g check the agent); test_lhs_nested (direction, correlation, the hover layout, the check design), test_altitude (the new overrides) and test_seed_streams_disjoint (family 2) extended.

**Gap review before launch, the spatial coherence (2026-10-04):** the feature coh (link_features.m, same name and position: the detector's input size is unchanged) is the mean over the antenna pairs of |R_ij|/√(R_ii R_jj) of the quiet-slot covariance after the receive filter: interference + noise, none of our signal, the covariance the real receiver whitens with before synchronization (extract_closed_loop_frames.m; the filter runs over the whole run as in the receiver). It came from the residual covariance after the channel estimate, which holds what the estimate leaves of our own signal: with the real receiver the clean link rose with Eb/N0 toward a jammer's and the feature was not monotone in JSR (12 flights: a 0 dB jammer 0.65, a 4 dB jammer 0.54, the clean link 0.30 at 12 dB and 0.80 at 24 dB).
- *Now* (three flights of fixed geometry, −3/0/6/12/18/24 dB): the clean link 0.158 at every Eb/N0, the floor of matched-filtered white noise over the 32-symbol slot (0.152 ± 0.045 per frame); the barrage jammer 0.48/0.63/0.85/0.94/0.97/0.98 at 0 dB, 0.68-0.98 at 4 dB, 0.95-0.98 at 16 dB, as its ratio to the noise (JSR + 3 dB + Eb/N0) sets it, 0.46-0.94 at 0 dB and 0.91-0.94 at 16 dB with drawn directions, K and correlation (an antenna in a fade of the jammer's channel lowers its pairs); a tone and the spoofer at 0 dB as the jammer (the receive filter keeps their in-band power); the WLAN at 0 dB 0.30-0.76, the noise bursts and the sweeper at 4 dB 0.38-0.76 and 0.27-0.38 (their share of the slot on the air).
- *A reactive jammer* is silent in the quiet slot: its coherence is the floor, q_react and env_corr carry it. mmse_gain and align keep the residual covariance of the data, where they predict the combiner. Data made before this change (the smoke data archived on 2026-10-04 included) carry the old feature.
- *Tests:* test_receiver_measurements replaces the paired check: the clean link within 0.04 of the floor at every Eb/N0 and within 0.01 across them (the flights' noise is the same at every Eb/N0, so any signal of ours in the slot would move it); the 16 dB jammer above 0.9 at every Eb/N0; the 0 dB jammer 0.2 above the clean link everywhere, rising with Eb/N0 and not above the 16 dB one; the reactive jammer at the floor.

**Gap review before launch, the commitment (2026-10-04; the user approved every recommendation of the review; edge_map.m, edge_verdict.m, edge_commit.m, edge_walk.m, rollout_policy.m):** what COMMITTED means, fixed before any reading.
- *Held recovery and protection:* rollout_policy.m records per episode held: recovered, and restored on every cycle from the start of the 5-cycle run to the end of the episode, but for one run of at most 1 + D cycles in a follower episode of a followable threat (one re-acquisition at signalling delay D; not under a comb jammer, which is on every channel from the onset and has nothing to re-acquire); and the packets lost and the frames from that start. In the edge map REC is held recovery among the recoverable flights and PROT held recovery among all threat flights (an unrecoverable flight a failure), target 0.90, a flight succeeding when all its episodes do. SURV ≥ 0.90 and REC ≥ 0.90 alone left up to 19% of the threat flights unprotected, and any 5-cycle run counted: in v4's last test reading a random policy making 29 changes per episode "recovered" 80.7% of the single-threat and 96.2% of the follower episodes. C2e keeps the proposal's KPI 4 "recovered". Horizon: held for the rest of a 0.6 s episode (at most 0.54 s after the onset).
- *The commitment* (edge_commit.m): a point is COMMITTED when PROT, DET_h, FA, LINK and LINK_T are COMMITTED, the deployed policy met the false-alarm bound on validation (F), and none of its bands is NOT COMMITTED, at every signalling delay apart. FA is that of the claim: the clean flights of every Eb/N0 from the KPI 1 threshold up (KPI 6's range; 4 × 172 = 688 flights, COMMITTED with ≤ 23 alarms, two-sided Clopper-Pearson upper 4.97%; a true 2.2% commits 98% of the time, 3% 75%), or from the point up for a point below the threshold (5 × 172 = 860, ≤ 30 alarms; 1 032, ≤ 37); the point's own 172 flights must not be NOT COMMITTED (≥ 16 alarms make the point NOT COMMITTED). FA is shared by every threat at an Eb/N0, and per point (≤ 2 of 172) a policy with a true 2% would commit a point with probability 0.33 and four points of a walk with 0.012 (1%: 0.75 and 0.32). An intersection, no added error. SYSTEM (DET, SURV and REC, the proposal's relative criterion) and SYSTEM+LINK stay as reported layers, SURV as the physics boundary. The random policy flies every flight beside the deployed one: a point where its PROT is COMMITTED too is not attributable to the decision layer (marked r; a threat that seldom harms the link).
- *Reference of restoration* (link_env.m 'flight'): the same flight's clean link at no_action (the clean cell flies every geometry), its BER floored at 1e-4, and its packet loss, instead of the train-split mean per Eb/N0, which let the flight's channel quality into the verdict; training, validation and C2e keep the mean. *[Correction 2026-10-24: the mean is no longer the train split's. D95 rebuilds `PP.clean` and `PP.clean_fer`, with the monitor's `clean_ref`, from the 300 clean validation flights per Eb/N0 (profile 1 by hand in the software-only stop, profile 3 inside C1c); 'flight' is unchanged.]* LINK_T: the packet loss from the start of the recovery run to the end of the episode, pooled over the point, ≤ max(2× the same flights' clean loss, 1.4%): the per-geometry criterion (2× clean + one packet of 20) let a "restored" link lose about 7.8% at a point whose clean link meets 1.4%. *[2026-10-29: unchanged by D108; the 1.4% floor kept as registered (TGT.linkt_floor).]*
- *Followers:* rows at a re-acquisition 0, 1 and 2 cycles after a hop (0-40 ms; the sources' followers react within 1-50 ms, W03's in the next 1-3 ms slot), the four followable threats, 72 flights, two episodes each; recoverable there means a configuration without channel_switch restores the link (frequency diversity escapes a single-channel follower). Per row the commitment is walked from 2 cycles down to 0 and names the fastest re-acquisition it holds against, in cycles and ms. C2e's 2-5 cycle set stays the KPI protocol (D64).
- *Signalling delay:* the deployed policy flies every single, combined, follower and clean flight at D = 1, 2 and 7 cycles (20, 40 and 140 ms; 7 is about the 141 ms from threat declaration to a restored link that Barajas et al. measured, Table II, the only measured switch), the same onsets and frame draws at every D; the commitment, its walks and the check points have a verdict per D. The receiver alone (always-on MMSE, no request after the onset) is the floor without signalling: its PROT, LINK and LINK_T.
- *DET_h and the severity walk:* DET_h is the detection on the flights whose unmitigated link is not restored (no_action fails the criterion); with fewer than 36 such flights at a point it only must not be NOT COMMITTED. DET over every flight stays KPI 1's reading. The severity walk starts at the nominal level and walks down and up, two fixed-sequence branches of one-sided 2.5% steps (≤ 5% family error), giving a level interval: v5 detected 7 dB shadowing on 64% of its frames, and a walk from the lowest level stopped at its first step for every harmful level above.
- *Intervals* (edge_verdict.m): a rate (DET, DET_h, LINK, LINK_T) takes the hull of the cluster bootstrap and the Clopper-Pearson interval on its effective sample size, the units over the design effect (the bootstrap variance over the binomial variance of independent units), between the flights (the units of a flight fully correlated) and the units; at 1 DET's lower bound stays at most Clopper-Pearson of n flights in n, at 0 LINK's upper bound at least that of no lost frame in all frames. DET with all-or-nothing flights now commits at 70 of 72 (0.9032) and not at 69 (0.8830); the bootstrap alone committed at 69, so a true 0.90 committed 6.2% of the time instead of 2.1%.
- *Bands:* a point commits only if none of its bands is NOT COMMITTED (an intersection, no added error): the speed sixths, the altitude thirds, the five K bands, the first interferer's alignment with the GCS (< 0.5, 0.5-0.9, ≥ 0.9; threats with an interferer), the receive correlation (thirds of 0.3-0.9), and, where PP.cov records them, the bank (0-20, 20-40, 40-58°) and our signal's RMS delay spread (0-100, 100-200, 200-300, 300-1000 ns); a band whose field the pools do not record is skipped and the report says so. A band reads PROT, DET_h and LINK_T of the threat flights and FA and LINK of the clean flights in it, pooled over the point and every point above it while they are COMMITTED from 15 dB down (the region of the distance walk; a point outside that run pools its own flights). Hover and 161 km/h keep verdicts of their own: the points' flights are moving flights. A band's own commitment (the band tables, hover and 161 km/h included) takes the claim's FA (the KPI 6 pool) and the band's own clean flights not NOT COMMITTED: the 96 hover clean flights of a band would commit FA only with no alarm (0 gives 3.8%, 1 gives 5.7%). Hover is labelled "hover in calm air (attitude within ±1°, Lin 2026)", a frozen channel (Stated limits); per-point verdicts are for moving flights.
- *Fixed before any reading:* the K floor −5 dB (the K ≥ −3 dB reading informational); LINK 1.4%; 72 flights per point (36 for a combination); a KPI 1 threshold of NaN means all six Eb/N0 points (as evaluate_policies.m); every target, walk start and direction, layer and band above. Hashes (git blob) before profile 1's C2e reads the test: edge_verdict.m 00a25c17b4ac, edge_map.m 3cf120b05207, edge_commit.m da362caa783b, edge_walk.m 1e70f145727e; a later change is logged here with the outputs of both versions. edge_map.m changed before any reading: D76 (5c3b0dae42ca, DET on unharmed flights), D79 (1d327b63f43c, LINK_T without the decode gap) and the K-band fix of de6a5c5 (e718e49fdc7d: the check points' Eb/N0 overwrote the K band edges of the K walk); e718e49fdc7d is the version that reads the test. *[Later change: D108 (2026-10-29); both versions printed; blob hashes logged before profile 3's C2e.]*
- *Unknown threats:* the edge map flies the leave-one-out agent of every threat (data/unknown_threat_agents.mat, C2u) on the test and second test flights of that threat, the detector output withheld after the onset (the rule with escalation when it is the deployed policy): REC and PROT per point with intervals, evidence only, no verdict. No rate is committed for an emitter outside the detector's ten classes.
- *The map as a whole:* the commitment is the walk regions (Eb/N0 from 15 dB down, levels around the nominal one, follower re-acquisition from 2 cycles, the speed and altitude bands around the nominal ones), each "up to" claim at ≤ 5% family error; a COMMITTED point outside every walk region is reported, not committed. Per point the error is one-sided ≤ 2.5% (≤ 2.1% at a true 0.90 and 72 flights); the map as a whole is not jointly controlled (common random numbers group the errors by Eb/N0 column), and the report gives the count of COMMITTED points. D70's envelope is renamed "predicted on validation"; the edge map tabulates its agreement with the commitment.
- *Seeds:* the edge map's frame draws 200 000 + 1000 × base set + batch, and 40 000, 90 000 and 95 000 + batch for the clean flights of C1c, the check flights and their clean link, the same at every D (FA and the check verdicts at D = 2 and 7 differ from D = 1 by the delay alone): disjoint for batches below 1 000, all below the first flight stream.
- *Cost:* no simulation. About 1.1 million episodes per profile in the edge map (C2e runs about 2 000 per second), plus the verdicts and bands: under an hour.
- *Tests:* test_held_recovery (a comb episode gets no allowance), test_flight_reference, test_edge_walk, test_edge_commit (the claim's FA and the point's own); test_edge_verdict (70 and 69 of 72 all-or-nothing flights, one wrong frame in 30 of 72 flights, 23 and 24 alarms of 688, 0 and 1 of 96) and test_seed_streams_disjoint (the edge map's frame-draw seeds, none of them by delay) extended.

**Limits of the commitment (stated, not modelled):**
- *Return link:* every configuration that changes the GCS transmitter assumes the UAV's request reaches the GCS over the telemetry return link within one 20 ms cycle, both ends change on the same frame, and the request is authentic. The return link, its loss and forged requests are outside the scope; the verdicts at D = 2 and 7 cycles are the sensitivity, and without the return link only receiver-side adaptive combining remains (the floor).
- *Spoofing:* COMMITTED means the counterfeit is detected and the GCS's own command stream keeps arriving within 2× its clean link; it does not mean counterfeit commands are rejected, which needs message authentication (Mekdad et al.; e.g. MAVLink 2 signing), outside the RF scope. Attacks aware of our protocol are outside the commitment as stated above (future work: keyed or randomised training, La Pan et al. 2013).
- *Moving jammer:* interferers are emitters at fixed ground positions; their channels change only through the UAV's own motion (Doppler up to 358 Hz at 161 km/h). A jammer on a moving platform is outside the commitment.
- *Shadowing in turns:* single-threat commitments assume no antenna is shadowed by the airframe; shadowing in a turn is measured only in the combination cells with airframe_shadowing.
- *Threat model:* one emitter per class, at most three at once, only the 14 listed combinations, at equal level index; the noise-burst timing assumed (no source); threats stationary after the onset apart from the follower's re-acquisition; profile 3 is never tested with more emitters than it can null; jamming means a jammer occupying our channel up to about 8 MHz wide; a hop lands at least 3 MHz beyond the jammer and about 22 MHz from a WLAN centre, on a channel found free, and the search is not modelled.
- *Rate reduction:* ×4 direct-sequence spreading inside the same 0.69 ms frame: 250 payload bits + CRC per 20 ms cycle.
- *Recovery cost:* a recovered link may carry 250 bits per cycle (rate reduction), about 500 bits per cycle with a 40 ms decoding delay (FEC), or two channels (frequency diversity); the commitment covers BER and packet loss, not throughput or latency.
- *Episode timescale:* one 0.38 s flight segment (20 frames) replayed over 30 cycles; pointing, shadowing, tracker and altitude are fixed per flight; changes over seconds are covered across flights by the bands.
- *Relay:* an illustrative what-if (60°, 3 dB, the relay link clean); not part of any commitment.
- *Approved v7 steps:* Steps 2 and 6 (protocol-aware attacks, adaptive adversaries) are excluded (above); the adaptive behaviour inside the commitment is the follower's re-acquisition and the reactive jammer. Step 3's roll-driven airframe shadowing is excluded (shadowing in turns, above).
- *v6* (user decision: not patched): its follower and comb numbers are biased upward (the re-hop moved the follower's clock at the decision, so the frames still sent on the jammed channel counted as clean, and it fired from the true state), its REC counts any 5-cycle run against the train-split mean, and it has no edge map; v6 numbers are not comparable with v7's commitment.

**Adoption:** before any v7 reading; stage order A0 A4v A5 A6 B1 B2 B2F B3 B3a B4 OOD B4s C1p C1c C1d C2 C2e C2g C2u GAL SURV SURV3 OHP GCSA EDGE KPI DASH WEAK RPT, then LAT KPI DASH WEAK RPT on the idle machine.

## D75 — v7 gap fixes on the link side: delay spread, attitude, GCS direction, FEC packets, power step, sweeper timing
**Why (2026-10-04, with the user):** the ranked gap review of v7 before launch (link side: items 1, 3-6, 9, 10, 21, 24, 25) found single-tap channels where the sources measure 64-302 ns of delay spread, banked turns and wind without an RF effect, the GCS fixed at the array's broadside, an FEC codeword over a whole run, a power step no named radio has, and a sweeper timing without a source.
