A review of the fixes found a receiver that missed its own check on the delay line, a sweeper class that shrank in the dataset, an in-band cap referenced to the mean antenna, a CRC read one cycle early on coded packets, a pitch beyond the measured tilts, and a radio limit taken for a modulated signal. The user's decisions (2026-10-04): implement the items; the hover wobble model; the space-time MMSE combiner if V12c fails; protocol-aware attacks are not implemented.
**Decision:**
- *Delay spread (item 1).* A tapped delay line on our signal and on every interferer: taps 250 ns apart up to 4.5 µs, an exponential profile matched to the RMS delay spread at the channel's K, the LoS on tap 0, every tap with its own sinusoids and its spatial signature centred on its source. V12c (validate_phy.m: real receiver, 64-1000 ns, clean link at K −5/2/10 dB under MRC, 16 and 30 dB noise jammers under MMSE at ρ 0.3 and 0.9, limits 0.5 and 3 dB) fails on the flat channel, so the line runs on every flight: RMS delay spreads drawn per flight, log-normal (Rodriguez-Pineiro et al., environment II: our signal on the directional OLoS fit (−6.52, 0.32) below 25 m and the omni fit (−6.63, 0.16) above; every interferer its own omni draw), clipped at 1 µs (their Fig. 7a), recorded in the dataset (ds_ns, ds_int) and in PP.cov. Receiver on the line (rx_taps.m): MRC over the antennas and the channel's lags with a linear MMSE equalizer; MMSE with space-time taps half a symbol apart (±2 RMS spreads at the clip, 9 per antenna), and per frame the space-time or the antennas-alone output, whichever reproduces the frame's pilots better under the decision-directed weights (they never use the pilots): on a nearly flat interferer the space-time degrees of freedom cost more estimation noise than they null. The first pass shrinks a lag below the noise of its smoothed pilot estimate, the received power (a lag's correlation over the pilots also picks up every other lag's unknown symbols): a noise-only level cost +4.9 dB at 1 µs and K −5 dB and +4.4 dB at 64 ns and K 2 dB in V12c, so it was not taken. V12c now reports two verdicts: the channel model (the flat channel fails, the link runs the line: PASS) and the receiver on the line (FAIL. The 16 dB jammer passes at ρ 0.9 up to 372 ns (+0.00 to +1.24 dB; the space-time receiver alone +3.86 to +6.90 dB) and at ρ 0.3 up to 138 ns (+0.51 and +1.11 dB; alone +3.80 and +5.23 dB); it fails at ρ 0.3 from 234 ns (+6.94 to +12.02 dB) and at ρ 0.9 at 1 µs (+6.56 dB). The 30 dB jammer fails at every spread (+9.1 dB and more; on the flat channel the receiver itself reaches only BER 3.5e-2 at 15 dB). The clean link stays within 0.5 dB up to 1 µs (rows of the earlier run: this pass leaves the MRC receiver unchanged)). Informational: the 16 dB jammer at 234 ns and 358 Hz (161 km/h), +5.94 dB at ρ 0.3 and +2.36 dB at ρ 0.9, against +6.94 and +0.61 dB at 160 Hz.
- *Banked turns (item 3).* The bank of a coordinated turn, atand(|ω| v / g) up to 57.9° (Gross et al.), tilts the UAV's dipoles and turns their polarization against the GCS antenna (uav_attitude_db.m: Badi et al.'s 20 log10(cos φ) with the GCS ahead, the tilted pattern abeam); on our signal only, so the jammer-to-signal ratio rises by the same, up to the 30 dB cap. Overhead pass flown banked: per speed band the largest bank at its top speed, the worst over the GCS's bearing, against the wings-level cruise gain: up to 26.9 dB at 120 m and 0.28 km (20.8 dB wings level), beyond the 22 dB top path-loss level at 75-120 m and 0.28-0.56 km (results/overhead_pass.txt; EDGE reads the worst band).
- *GCS direction (item 4).* Drawn per flight over the cone angle (gcs_aoa.m), no longer at the array's broadside; recorded with its alignment to every interferer.
- *Interferer LoS Doppler and centred diffuse fading (items 5, 25).* Every interferer's LoS has the Doppler shift of its direction, fd sin(aoa), on the channel clock; the diffuse part of every channel is centred on its own source's steering vector.
- *FEC packets (item 6).* One codeword per 1000 + 32-bit packet over two frames (rate 1/2, K = 7, punctured evenly to 2 × 1032 bits, interleaved over the two frames, erasures for symbols more than 6 dB above the frames' median). BER and packet error are ground truth on both frames; the receiver's CRC result exists only from the second frame, when the packet is decoded, one cycle (20 ms) later than an uncoded packet: the first frame's CRC is NaN, the monitor counts a coded packet once at its second frame, and the clean coded link's packet loss is read from the frames that carry a result (pool field crc). The cycles to recover do not count that cycle. One packet of slack in the packet-loss criterion (packet_share.m). D46's survivability map flies 57 frames per run: with FEC the last frame has no pair, so 56 frames and 28 packets count (slack 2/56).
- *Power step (item 9).* The GCS is an AD9361-class radio: 0.25 dB steps, up to 7.5 dBm at 2.4 GHz for a 1 MHz CW tone (AD9361 data sheet, Rev. F). ETSI EN 300 328's 10 dBm in any 1 MHz caps our RRC signal at 10.20 dBm e.i.r.p. (95.46% of its power in its central 1 MHz, eirp_cap_dbm.m), stricter than 100 mW. power_control raises the radio by its largest step under the cap: +4.0 dB to 10.0 dBm on the 12 dBi antenna. A CW limit bounds our waveform's peak, so its mean stays the peak-to-average ratio below (4.7 dB for our RRC QPSK, papr_db.m): on the omni the radio's limit gives +8.75 dB (the analysis had +13.5 dB); the system antenna is cap-limited either way.
- *Sweeper timing (item 10).* Liu et al.: 1 GHz/s over a 20 MHz band with a 4 MHz instantaneous band, so our 1.25 MHz channel is jammed 5.25 ms per sweep; the sweep period is drawn per flight in 20-83.5 ms (the 20 MHz band up to the whole 2.4 GHz band at 1 GHz/s) and its phase on the channel clock (jam_timing.m); the burst phase of the noise bursts likewise. A frame with less than 10% of its air time in the window is a clean frame (threat_active.m): 13% of the sweeper's frames count, so its dataset cells fly 46 sub-runs instead of 6 (sweep_hit_share.m) and its labelled frames match the other threats' (the other frames are clean frames, weighted by the class-weighted loss). Frequency diversity loses a frame only while both carriers are in the sweep: never at the 25 MHz spacing.
- *Assumption, frequency diversity:* the second carrier is 25 MHz away, 802.11's adjacent-channel separation where it specifies ≥ 35 dB rejection (IEEE 802.11-2007, 18.4.8.3); no source gives a UAV command link's channel plan.
- *Hover in wind (item 21, approved model).* Below 8 m/s the UAV tilts to hold its position: roll and pitch uniform within Polle et al.'s 30 s means (roll −17.5..19.3°, pitch −11.0..14.9°, DJI Air 3S); its pitch wobbles at 5-25 Hz with an amplitude up to 10° and the antennas 0.4 m from the centre (Banagar & Dhillon's assumed parameters, not measurements: decorrelation within 6.7-12.3 ms at 2.4 GHz); the amplitude is limited so that the pitch with its wobble stays within Polle et al.'s largest calibrated tilts (−17.7° forward, +21.1° backward).
- *Airframe loss (item 24).* Per flight and antenna, N(3.03, 2.53²) dB truncated to 0.016-10.96 dB (Badi et al. 2019, Table 1), on our signal only, its mean taken out (the common part is in the 10 dB margin). The 30 dB in-band cap is referenced to our signal on the weakest antenna (the 'Body' block into the threat block), so no antenna sees an emitter more than 30 dB over our signal (weakest antenna up to 7 dB below the mean).
- *Seeds:* new purpose offsets of seed_stream.m: delay spread 17, GCS direction 37, jammer timing 43, airframe loss 59, hover attitude 61 (all distinct, below 64; test_seed_streams_disjoint).
- *Records:* the dataset and PP.cov carry the GCS direction and alignment, bank, roll, pitch, wobble amplitude, the antenna gain at the attitude, the airframe-loss spread, the jammer timing and the delay spreads; MODEL_TAG 'v7-D75' (with D74's decision-side changes): A0, A5 and C1p must be regenerated.
- *With D74's decision side:* the quiet-slot coherence counts, as the receiver's whitening does, from the slot's first sample whose receive-filter memory lies in the frame (22 of its 32 symbols; its thermal floor rises accordingly, test_receiver_measurements reads it from the same samples); the check flights (build_check_pools.m) record the same per-flight draws as PP.cov, the alignment toward the flight's own GCS direction.
- *Corrections to earlier entries:* D73's cap of "about 11 dBm" for a 1.25 MHz signal is 10.20 dBm (the densest 1 MHz of the RRC spectrum); its nRF24L01+ GCS (0/−6/−12/−18 dBm, +6 dB step, then +5.0 dB under the cap) is an AD9361-class radio with 0.25 dB steps (+4.0 dB); D73's "against a 30 dB jammer at 222-358 Hz three antennas cannot hold the null" overstates the null's part: at 160 Hz, 15 dB and ρ 0.3 the real receiver's BER is 3.6e-2 against 1.6e-2 for known-symbol MMSE weights at the same timing, and about half of its errors are frames whose timing the jammer pulls off (synchronization and frequency estimation under the jammer); the rest is the null's own limit.
**Cost:** a flight with the delay line costs 1.28-1.88× the flat one (MMSE 1.88×), against the plan's +10-40%: C1p about 65-96 h per profile instead of 51 h; A5 flies 1.6× the sub-runs (the sweeper's 46 per cell) at the same per-flight factor; the full validate_phy takes about 3.5 h more (V12c).
**Open for the user:** whether the remaining V12c failures may stand as NOT COMMITTED cells: every configuration that relies on the spatial null against a 30 dB jammer, and against a 16 dB jammer at low receive correlation from 234 ns of delay spread (the median of environment II), with the spreads drawn per flight. The alternative is further receiver work (a per-window choice or a longer training for the space-time weights) before launch.
**Adoption:** before any v7 reading; A0, A4v, A5 and C1p rerun on this model.

## D76 — v7 detector: a fair reading beside KPI 1, WLAN gaps judged by their window, branch persistence, quiet-slot shape, partial antenna faults
**Why (2026-10-04, with the user, after the v6 detector diagnosis):** v6's fused test reading was accuracy 92.69%, macro-F1 92.21%, and 94.09% / 93.41% on the frames whose fusion window is full: sub-runs of 20 frames truncate the 12-cycle window (the first frame decides on itself). 419 of the 1427 clean test frames were WLAN frames without a packet inside a benign run (110 read as benign_interference, the same action); the antenna fault lost 28% of its frames to none and shadowing; benign interference read as none on a degraded link in 67 frames; the tone and the spoofer were confused in 12. The user approved seven items and wider antenna-fault levels, every one backed by a source (2026-10-04).
**Decision:**
- *D1, beside KPI 1 (KPI 1 unchanged: macro-F1 per Eb/N0 from the validation threshold, on the fused decisions).* eval_detector.m adds the steady-state reading (frames whose fusion window is full, rank ≥ N in their sub-run, with its bootstrap interval) and the onset latency per class on the test sub-runs (cycles to the first correct fused class and to the confirmed alarm: a hostile fused class on m of the last n cycles, decision_config.m; benign interference raises none). In the closed loop rollout_policy.m records per episode the cycles from the onset to the monitor's first class of the threat (t_cls) and to its first confirmed alarm (t_conf); evaluate_policies.m tabulates them per threat for the deployed policy (KP.onset). measure_all_kpis.m prints both beside KPI 1; export_report_numbers.m carries them (metrics.steady, metrics.onset, KP.onset).
- *D2, WLAN gaps.* The per-frame label stays as the proposal defines it (a frame without a packet is clean, mitigation 3). The fused decision is judged on its window (fusion_target.m): a clean frame whose window holds a benign_interference frame of its run has the target benign_interference, in the fusion's training and cross-validation (select_fusion.m), in the KPI 1 threshold on validation and in the fused readings (eval_detector.m; detector_block.m for eval_unseen_snr.m and eval_unseen_severity.m).
- *D3, branch imbalance over the window (temporal_evidence.m).* off_w, the mean per-cycle offset of the weakest antenna (lowest window mean) from the median of the others (Willgert, US 8,548,029, eq. 5, col. 11), and rat_w, its standard deviation over |off_w| (capped at 10): a loss in the RF path is |mean| > 3 dB with a ratio below 3, a pattern mismatch a mean ≤ 3 dB with a ratio above 3 (Table 1, col. 12); a sustained imbalance above 5 dB marks a degraded antenna connection (Heath, US 10,404,368, Table 2, cols. 41-42); the window mean is Lee's local average. A CUSUM over minutes (Xie et al.) cannot be validated in 0.6 s episodes and is not added: a field capability.
- *D4, the weakest antenna's K-factor.* k_w, the moment-method K = sqrt(Ga² − Gv²) / (Ga − sqrt(Ga² − Gv²)) of its per-cycle power (Aoki & Honda 2025, eq. 2, p. 12; in the library), clipped to −20..30 dB: inside airframe shadowing K falls from about 15 to −16 dB (Sun et al., Part IV, p. 5), a loss in series with the antenna keeps it. Read only over cycles in which every antenna's gain is above the noise of its 32-symbol channel estimate (est_margin > 0 dB, a new per-frame measurement: the lowest over the antennas of |h|² / (R_aa / 32) − 1), at least 3 of them; kv_w is the share of such cycles.
- *D5, the rule (rule_based_policy.m).* A degraded link whose class is none while the quiet slot shows interference (q_iot at or above q_thr) takes channel_switch (with spatial_diversity when the interferer is separable) instead of the generic diversity: low delivery at a high signal level is jamming (Xu et al. 2005, pp. 8-9); the slot's power is the AGC level of a jamming monitor (Ndili & Enge 1998, p. 5). q_thr comes from the clean validation frames as Kazim et al. set the AGC threshold from interference-free samples (eq. 1.3, p. 2, Th = μ − 3σ − 2 dB for a gain drop): μ + 3σ + 2 dB of q_iot (select_fusion.m, data/fusion.mat; default 3 dB, decision_config.m q_alarm_db); it reaches the rule through PP.q_thr (fusion_model.m: the pools, the check pools, the latency stage, the console). Benign interference on a degraded link already left the channel. The DQN sees the same evidence without a change to its training: q_iot of each of the last 4 cycles, occ_w, the degradation and the new window measurements are in its state.
- *D6, WLAN timing, from what the receiver has.* Per frame (extract_closed_loop_frames.m): duty, the share of the frame's 32-sample residual blocks on the reference antenna more than 3 dB over thermal (busy over observed time, Cheema & Salous 2019, eq. 1, p. 5; Airshark F3, p. 7), and edges, the busy/idle changes between them (burst length and envelope ripple, Grimaldi et al. 2019, p. 4; inter-pulse timing, Airshark, p. 5); over the window duty_w, their mean (the occupancy estimate beside occ_w of the quiet slot).
- *D7, the quiet slot's shape.* q_sfm, the spectral flatness exp(mean ln S) / mean S of the slot's periodogram averaged over the antennas, and q_par, the peak-to-average power of its envelope on the antenna with the most slot power: a tone is one spectral line and present in the slot (Shahriar et al. 2015, eq. 19, p. 7), a counterfeit of our modulation fills its band. Measured at 12 dB with 16 dB interferers (20 frames): tone 0.004-0.04 / 0.4-1.5 dB, spoofer 0.013-0.077 / 2.7-4.2 dB, barrage jammer about 0.5 / 7 dB, thermal noise 0.80-0.88 / 5-10 dB.
- *Antenna-fault levels.* From a partial loss to an open contact: decision levels 5 / 11.5 / 18 / 24.5 / 31 dB (decision_config.m), dataset levels eight even steps 5-31 dB (dataset_levels.m), the unseen-severity reading above them at 36 dB (an open contact of 0.01 pF). The backed range: a sustained diversity imbalance above 5 dB (Heath, Table 2), a steady RF-path loss above 3 dB (Willgert, Table 1); real connector faults are small losses or intermittent (Enquebecq et al., p. 6: fretting raises the loss from 0.15 to 0.37 dB; Smith et al. 2008, pp. 1-2). init_params.m: fault_atten_db 18 dB, the nominal level. The edge map marks every antenna-fault level as assumed.
- *EDGE.* DET stays the proposal's criterion; the commitment uses DET_h (checked: edge_commit.m and commit_rows read the harmed flights' verdict, D74). edge_map.m adds DET_u, the detection on the unharmed flights, beside it (per-point table, summary line, EDGE_NUM.det_split with the flight counts); no verdict rule changes.
**Consumers:** link_features.m 18 → 23 features (run_dataset_sweep.m records the new measurements; the normalisation, the detector's feature input and the unknown-threat model take their size from the data); temporal_evidence.m takes the window's link features (C + 4 → C + 9 columns) in fuse_classes.m and policy_monitor.m (window mem.wf); the agent's state holds the nine window measurements per cycle (policy_state_size.m: nC + 14 + 9 rows per cycle); the console, the 3D view and the latency stage run policy_decide.m with the pools' or fusion.mat's q_thr; export_gui_bundle.m carries data/fusion.mat.
**Stated, not added:** the distribution of fault depths between 5 and 31 dB has no source, so the levels are spread evenly over the backed range, and 26-36 dB stays the gap-capacitance estimate; the spectral flatness measure itself is the standard geometric-over-arithmetic mean, which no source of the library defines; Lopez-Benitez's occupancy correction (eq. 9) needs the detection probability of an interferer of unknown level, so it is not applied (the false-alarm share of a 32-sample block 3 dB over thermal is about 1e-7); a K-factor from at most 12 cycles is coarse (Aoki & Honda note the error of few samples); the caps (rat_w 10, k_w −20..30 dB, est_margin floor −30 dB) are numerical; the receiver-side measurements (extract_closed_loop_frames.m) are not timed by the latency stage, as before; edge_map.m's hash changes from D74's (the DET_u output only).
**Tests:** test_temporal_evidence (the window's link features), test_branch_imbalance (an 18 dB fault: offset −18 dB, ratio below 0.3, K above 8 dB; a shadow: K below 5 dB; a ±4 dB pattern effect: |offset| below 3 dB, ratio above 3; no K below the estimate's noise; duty_w; one antenna), test_fusion_target, test_rule_quiet_slot (the rule, and the decision cycle with PP.q_thr), test_slot_and_burst_measurements (the real link: tone, spoofer, WLAN, noise bursts, clean), test_features and test_state_size extended.
**Adoption:** before any v7 reading; A5, A6, B1, B2, B2F, B3 and every later stage rerun (new features and fusion inputs; the decision layer's state and the antenna-fault cells change).

## D77 — v7 receiver on the delay line: space-time sync, signal-free loaded covariance, frame-wide passes; receive front end
**Why (2026-10-04/05, with the user):** D75 left the receiver on the delay line failing V12c: a 30 dB jammer at every spread (its training-based sync broke: the frequency off by more than 500 Hz in 79-88% of frames, while known symbols at the true timing reach BER 4e-3) and a 16 dB jammer at ρ 0.3 from 234 ns. Diagnosis: 27 space-time dimensions estimated from 32-64 samples (SMI loss mean (K − N + 2)/(K + 1), Reed-Mallett-Brennan; Besson 2021, eq. 20-21) with our own signal inside the covariance (MPDR penalty, Besson eq. 22-23), and a first pass whose windows took their weights from the earlier windows' decisions. The user approved three source-backed fixes and, as a scope addition, a receive front end (AGC + ADC).
**Decision:**
- *Sync with space-time taps (R2).* Frequency first (Morelli & D'Amico, eq. 24 and 31; coarse then fine, Bertolucci et al., Fig. 3): at every arrival the training's 5-tap snapshots per antenna (±1 symbol, half a symbol apart), whitened by their own covariance loaded with the noise floor, are correlated with the training on the 512-point FFT grid (1.95 kHz bins over ±121 kHz), coherence a = g'(R + σ²I)^-1 g / K² (JASS, Marti, Arquint & Studer 2024, eq. 10 and Alg. 1: with the training projected out of R the statistic is a / (1 − a) by Sherman-Morrison, the same maximum; the loaded inverse in place of the projection, Besson 2021, eq. 81). The space-time taps sit on the arrival of the largest a; the arrival itself is the peak of the antennas' coherence (JASS's statistic) at that frequency within SPS + 2 samples: the space-time statistic is flat over about ±1 symbol and leans 3 samples early under a jammer, while the antennas' peak is within 0.5-1.2 samples of the true arrival in 90% of the dev frames. The residual frequency comes from the first pass, not from the pilot steps (under a jammer they leaned 150-400 Hz on the dev frames). The flat channel and the MRC on the delay line keep their sync (bit-identical with the front end off).
- *Noise floor.* The median eigenvalue of the quiet slot's 27-dimensional space-time covariance (9 taps per antenna): −1 to +4 dB from the thermal level under the 16-30 dB dispersive jammers of the dev frames; over 5 taps per antenna the jammer fills more than half of the dimensions and the estimate is up to 10 dB high (30 dB, 234 ns, 12 dB: BER 0.10 against 0.014).
- *Signal-free loaded covariance (R1).* Our signal's space-time channel by LS on the symbols around every snapshot (±4 symbols: the taps' ±2 and two raised-cosine side lobes); the interference + noise covariance from the residuals (the snapshots less our signal), never from the snapshots themselves; MVDR weights (R + ISI + μI)^-1 h₀, μ the noise floor (LSMI for non-stationary jammers: Gershman, Nickel & Böhme 1996, Sec. 3.2; Besson eq. 68 and 80).
- *First pass and passes (R3).* The first pass takes our channel from the training and, window by window (32 data symbols, rows within 64), the covariance of the residuals of the rows whose symbols are known or decided (with the training and the quiet slot while fewer than 54 rows): a decision error enters only a covariance row, never the weights' channel. Each window's phase from its pilots and decisions, the residual frequency from the slope of the window phases over the frame; twice. Then 4 passes over the whole frame: the channel by LS on all known and decided symbols (Marti, Kölle & Studer 2023, eq. 15; their regularized start, Sec. V-C, p. 7), the covariance of the residuals within 32 symbols of every 64-symbol window (the jammer's channel ages). The last pass leaves out of its weights the rows that carry a pilot and returns the pilots' error under them (the first pass and the earlier passes use the pilots); the frame keeps that output or the antennas-alone one, whichever reproduces its pilots better (D75).
- *Sync-failure flag.* Receiver output 6, rows 6-7, in extract_closed_loop_frames F.sync_coh and F.sync_fail: the 5-tap space-time coherence of the training at the frame's arrival and frequency (every real-receiver configuration, 0..1) and the flag sync_coh < p.sync_coh_min = 0.60: the training was not found (reported; D78 keeps it out of the decoder's erasures). The threshold is the largest on a 0.05 grid that flags at most 1% of the correctly synchronized frames (frequency within 500 Hz of the genie run without the jammer) over dev flights (11 configurations, seeds 7001-7050, 4840 frames; never validate_phy's or the tests' flights): 0.95% of them flagged; it flags 27 of the 110 frames off by more than 500 Hz and 49 of the 140 lost frames (BER > 0.2). The other lost frames found their training (coherence near 0.7) and fail later in the frame.
- *Receive front end (scope addition, adc_frontend.m; a fast attack added in D79).* Per antenna an AGC and an ADC of p.adc_bits = 12 bits per rail, before synchronization and combining (0 or Inf: off). AD9361 data sheet Rev. F: 12-bit Σ-Δ ADCs (p. 33); Table 1: 1 dB gain step, 73 dB gain range at 2.3 GHz, RSSI ±2 dB; no AGC settling time and no receiver maximum input level. So the gain is held over the frame, set from the larger mean power of the quiet slot and the first 16 short-training symbols at the latest arrival (the short training serves AGC settling in 802.11), on the 1 dB grid; full scale p.adc_backoff_db = 12 dB (to 13 dB) above the rail RMS: the 1 − 10⁻⁴ quantile of the composite's rails on the dev frames is 11.1-12.1 dB (a Gaussian's 11.8 dB), so 2-9·10⁻⁵ of the rails clip; SQNR 63-64 dB. Resolution under jamming: one bit per 6.02 dB of jammer (Marti et al. 2024, Prop. 2, eq. 18-19), 8 bits for a 25 dB jammer (Castaneda et al. 2021), up to 6 dB of anti-jam per A/D bit (Knight, Cahn & Nair 2007): with 12 bits under a 30 dB jammer the quantization noise stays about 23 dB below the thermal floor.
- *Absolute level at the UAV.* The 802.11 receiver maximum input level at 2.4 GHz is −20 dBm (Yucek & Kim 2011, 802.11-11/1077r0; Hart 2022, 802.11-21/1563r3); the AD9361 data sheet gives IIP3 −14 dBm at maximum gain and no maximum input. At the closest committed point (18 dB, 0.20 km, power step on: 10 dBm e.i.r.p.) our signal arrives at −74 dBm in free space and the 30 dB-capped jammer at −44 dBm, 24 dB below −20 dBm. Over the flight geometry our signal is largest at −49 dBm (15 m altitude, 5.8 m from the mast, dipole −3.3 dB); with the cap a jammer could reach −19 dBm there, 1 dB above the 802.11 level, about 57 dB of Eb/N0, far outside the simulated grid: front-end compression is not modelled and that close pass is a stated limit.
- *Pools.* MODEL_TAG 'v7-D77' (build_policy_pools.m, with D76's detector measurements): pools built on the D75 receiver are not reused.
**Results (validate_phy, front end on):** V1-V7 gaps +0.01 to +0.14 dB (unchanged to 0.01 dB); V8b MMSE at 8 and 10 dB 3.9e-5 / 5.0e-6 → 4.6e-5 / 1.0e-5 (the clipped rails; 92 against 78 and 20 against 10 errors); V9, V10, V12 unchanged; V11 losses +0.95 / +1.01 / +4.77 dB (+0.94 / +1.01 / +4.77). V12c, shift against the flat receiver (before → after): 16 dB ρ 0.3: +0.51/+1.11/+6.94/+7.34/+8.06/+12.02 → −0.98/−0.84/−0.63/−0.44/−0.22/+1.53 dB at 64/138/234/302/372/1000 ns (PASS); 16 dB ρ 0.9: +0.00/+0.33/+0.61/+0.92/+1.24/+6.56 → −1.14/−1.10/−1.06/−0.92/−0.79/+0.09 (PASS); 30 dB ρ 0.3: +13.76/+15.00/+15.00/+15.00/+15.00/+15.00 → −0.17/+0.00/+0.00/+1.37/+2.56/+14.21 (FAIL at 1 µs); 30 dB ρ 0.9: +9.14/+11.79/+15.00/+15.00/+15.00/+15.00 → −3.27/−2.76/−1.95/−1.06/−0.18/+3.00 (PASS at the limit); clean rows within 0.01 dB of before (K −5: +0.25 to −0.21; K 2: −0.17 to −0.26; K 10: −0.10 to −0.16 dB; the MRC receiver is unchanged); 358 Hz rows (234 ns, informational): +5.94 → −0.04 dB at ρ 0.3, +2.36 → −0.77 dB at ρ 0.9. The flat receiver itself stays at BER 3.5e-2 (15 dB) under a 30 dB jammer: a passing 30 dB row is not a usable link (7.8e-3 at 234 ns and 15 dB). Run time per 20-frame flight against the D75 receiver: clean MRC 0.95×, jamming MMSE 0.90×, jamming + tone MMSE 0.93× (front end 1-7% of it).
**Quiet-slot support:** after the receive filter's memory and the taps' span the 32-symbol slot leaves 72 snapshots at every sample, about 22 independent ones (18 symbols, 1.25 per symbol over the RRC band), against 2R = 22-24 for a jammer rank of 11-12 of 27: enough for the noise floor (a median) and for the first pass's fallback, not for the null on its own; the null comes from the training's and the decided rows' residuals (68 + at least 128 rows per window). Not changed; a slot of 48 symbols (+16 µs, 2.3% of the 688-symbol frame) would hold 2R up to R = 16 by itself.
**Not adopted:** loading 10 dB above the noise floor (the approved starting point): worse on the dev frames than loading at the floor (30 dB, 234 ns, ρ 0.3, 12 dB: 4.6e-2 against 1.3e-2; 16 dB, 1 µs: 8.0e-3 against 1.2e-3): a dispersive jammer's weaker eigenvalues lie between the floor and 10 dB above it, where Besson's condition λ_R ≫ Kμ fails. A first pass with the training's covariance alone over the whole frame: 7.4e-2 against 1.8e-2 at 30 dB with known timing and frequency (the jammer's channel ages across the frame). A channel drift term or local channel estimates in the passes: worse on static channels, at most 10% better at K −5 dB and 358 Hz.
**Open:** 30 dB at ρ 0.3 and 1 µs fails (+14.2 dB, BER 0.14 at 15 dB) and stays NOT COMMITTED; longer spans are worse on dev frames (15 dB: 9 taps 0.12, 13 taps 0.22, 17 taps 0.21): the three antennas' degrees of freedom, not the span; 30 dB at ρ 0.9 and 1 µs sits at the limit; our own channel at K −5 dB and 358 Hz under a 16 dB jammer keeps a floor near 2.5e-2 at 12 dB (dev, not a V12c row; D75 receiver 3.8e-2); the flag misses frames that find their training and fail later (D78's pilot-error erasure rule catches most of them).
**Adoption:** before any v7 reading; A0, A4v, A5 and C1p rerun on this model.

## D78 — v7 coded packets: codeword over four frames, soft values with the receiver's block reliability, pilot-error erasures
**Why (2026-10-05, C1, approved by the user: the codeword over four frames with erasures):** D75's packet (1000 + 32 bits, rate-1/2 K = 7 code [171 133], 2064 coded bits) sat on two frames: a lost frame erases N − k of its codeword, at the limit e ≤ N − k of the erasures GRAND-EDGE can solve for by Gaussian elimination on the parity checks (Ercan et al. 2023, Sec. III-B), which a K = 7 convolutional decoder does not reach: one lost frame lost the packet. The decoder took hard decisions (±1, erased symbols 0) from the channel's bit errors; D77's sync flag caught 49 of 140 lost dev frames.
**Decision:**
- *Four frames, one packet per frame pair (fec_packets.m, p.fec_frames = 4).* Packet k ends on frame pair k (frames 2k − 1, 2k) and starts on pair k − 1: its interleaved codeword (D75's seeded permutation of the 2064 punctured bits) is cut into four quarters of 516 bits, the q-th on the q-th of frames 2k − 3 .. 2k. Every frame carries 516 bits of the packet that ends on its pair and 516 of the next one, on two fixed sets of 516 bit positions (a seeded permutation of the frame's 1032). The packet rate (one per two frames, goodput ×1/2) and the 1032-bit packet are kept; every channel bit carries one coded bit; a lost frame erases a quarter of each of the two codewords it carries. A run's pairs are cyclic (its first packet takes its first pair from the run's last), as link_env.m reads a geometry's frames cyclically (F_SUB even, asserted). Packet k's ground truth (ber, fer) sits on both frames of pair k and its CRC on frame 2k (NaN on 2k − 1): D75's per-frame layout, obs.pkt (pair keys) unchanged. A block of four frames carrying two packets, both decoded on the fourth, is not adopted: the first packet would wait 40 ms more at the transmitter and the monitor would read two results on one frame.
- *Soft values (beyond the approved item, on the dev evidence below).* Every channel bit enters the Viterbi decoder with the magnitude of its log-likelihood ratio from the combiner output (extract_closed_loop_frames.m, bit_reliability), the sign the receiver's decision: per 32-symbol block the decision-directed gain g and error power es (as the BER estimate), LLR = 2√2 Im / Re(g* z) / es for the first / second bit of the Gray-mapped π/4 QPSK symbol. Each symbol thus carries its block's noise variance, the jammer state information of Baldi et al. (2013, Sec. V-VI, soft-decision decoding with and without JSI); a jammed block's bits enter with a small weight.
- *Erasures.* A frame whose pilot error exceeds p.erase_pilot_mse = 0.80 enters with every bit at zero metric, as 802.11 enters its punctured bits (IEEE 802.11-2007, 17.3.5.5, a dummy "zero" metric); lost frames converted to bit erasures by interleaving (Ercan et al. 2023, Sec. II). Receiver output 6 gains row 8, F.pilot_err: the mean squared error of the frame's pilots under its final weights, the unit pilot as reference; the space-time path the pilots of the output it keeps under the last pass's weights, which exclude the pilot rows (the first pass and the earlier passes use them; D77), the antennas-alone paths MVDR weights from the decisions only (pilot_mse), the MRC equalizer on the delay line the pilots under its last pass, whose LS includes them. F.erase = pilot_err > p.erase_pilot_mse. Threshold: the smallest on a 0.05 grid flagging at most 1% of the dev frames with BER ≤ 0.2 (dev set A: link seeds 7201-7260, the six points below, 7200 frames): 0.62% (41 of 6643); it flags 465 of the 557 lost frames (BER > 0.2), the sync flag 203 of them. The sync flag is not in the rule: with it 2.2% of the good frames are flagged, 5.7% on the clean link at 0 dB (sync coherence 0.59-0.79 there), and the packet loss rises (below).
- *Monitor (policy_monitor.m; the window and the slack revised in D79).* A coded packet counts once, on the second frame of its pair, and only when all four frames of its codeword were received coded (keys k − 1, k − 1, k, k); after a switch to the code the first packet counts on the fourth coded frame or later. A frame the decoder cannot repair costs the two packets that share it (dev set B: 73-91% of the lost packets have a lost neighbour, 54-81% with D75's packets), so the coded link's packet loss is read over Q + 2 = 6 frames (three packets) and degrades at Q/2 + 1 = 3 lost packets (D75: 2 lost over 5 frames); the slack of restored_plr is one loss event, one packet uncoded and Q/2 = 2 packets coded (packet_share.m: 4 of 20 frames). The pools record PP.fec_frames (decision_config C.fec_frames = 4 otherwise).
- *Delay.* A packet is decoded on its fourth frame, three cycles (60 ms) after its first, against one cycle (20 ms) with two frames; its codeword spans four cycles (80 ms). The cycles to recover do not count it, as before (revised in D79: the first three frames after the link turns coded count as not restored). For scale only: Barajas et al. 2026 measured an average recovery of 141 ms for hardware-assisted interface failover on a ROS 2 robot testbed.
- *Pools.* MODEL_TAG 'v7-D78' (build_policy_pools.m).
**Measured (dev set B: link seeds 7301-7360, 60 flights × 20 frames per point, 600 packets per decoder; never validate_phy's or the tests' flights; nominal Doppler; every decoder on the same received frames).** Packet loss [%]; lost frames: BER > 0.2; flagged: pilot error > 0.80.

| point | uncoded frames with errors | lost frames | lost flagged | good flagged | D75 (2 frames, hard) | D78 (4 frames, soft, pilot erasures) | 2 frames, soft | 4 frames, soft, no erasures | 4 frames, soft, sync or pilot erasures | 4 frames, hard, pilot erasures |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 16 dB jammer, spatial_diversity, 6 dB | 76.3% | 176 | 159 | 10 / 1024 | 38.5 | 27.2 | 27.8 | 27.3 | 28.2 | 39.8 |
| same, 12 dB | 34.2% | 42 | 39 | 4 / 1158 | 13.2 | 6.5 | 8.8 | 6.8 | 6.5 | 12.5 |
| 30 dB jammer, 234 ns, ρ 0.3, MMSE, 12 dB | 46.2% | 135 | 120 | 9 / 1065 | 30.5 | 20.5 | 22.3 | 21.2 | 21.2 | 30.3 |
| same, 15 dB | 38.1% | 121 | 103 | 11 / 1079 | 26.5 | 18.2 | 19.0 | 18.0 | 18.3 | 25.8 |
| clean, MRC, 0 dB | 98.7% | 30 | 20 | 8 / 1170 | 34.2 | 11.7 | 13.2 | 11.7 | 17.2 | 37.0 |
| clean, MRC, 3 dB | 78.8% | 3 | 2 | 5 / 1197 | 9.3 | 1.7 | 2.0 | 1.2 | 2.5 | 6.7 |

The 16 dB jammer flies the delay line, directions, K, correlation and attitude drawn per flight; the 30 dB jammer K 10 dB, its channel spread 234 ns, ours flat. Over dev set B the erasure flag catches 443 of the 507 lost frames (87%) and flags 47 of 6693 good ones (0.70%). The coded rule alone on the steady coded link (every cyclic window of every flight, packet loss only): D75's rule on D75's packets fires on 31 / 7.6 / 24 / 19 / 27 / 4.7% of the windows of the six points, D78's on D78's packets on 15.5 / 2.7 / 9.3 / 7.3 / 2.8 / 0.3%.
**Reading:** the gain is the soft values': hard decisions lose 2.6-4.7× the packets of soft ones on the clean link at 0-3 dB, whose good frames carry about 0.5-7% bit errors that a hard-decision Viterbi does not repair, and 1.4-1.5× under the jammers; four frames add 0.5-2.0 points over two with soft values; the pilot erasures move the loss by −0.7 to +0.5 points against none (the block reliability already weighs a lost frame's bits down), kept for frames whose decisions look reliable while their pilots fail; the sync flag as an erasure costs 5.5 points on the clean link at 0 dB. Under the jammers 18-27% of the packets stay lost at the lower points: beyond the lost frames, the good ones carry bit errors too (34-76% of the uncoded frames have errors).
**Open:** the jammed points' residual loss (a lower code rate or a stronger code is a goodput and latency trade not taken here); the equalizer path's pilot error comes from weights whose LS includes the pilots; the threshold rests on six dev points.
**Tests:** test_fec_packets (every channel bit carries one coded bit, 516 bits per frame per packet, the cyclic frames; one erased frame of four decodes, unflagged it does not, with a low reliability it does; two flagged frames of a pair do not, across the run's wrap; burst erasure; CRC timing; two frames still selectable), test_packet_loss_once (counting after four coded frames, the slack, 3 lost of 6 frames), test_coded_degraded_after_arrival, test_sync_flag (pilot error and erasure on a clean and an empty link; the clean run coded).
**Adoption:** before any v7 reading; A5, C1p and every later stage rerun on the new pools.

## D79 — v7 review of the receiver and the coded link: coded loss over five packets, a loss event as the slack, the decode gap, guards, a fast-attack AGC
**Why (2026-10-05, adversarial review of the merged v7 code, D77-D78):** the coded loss window of D78 held three packets (only every second frame carries a CRC result), so its rule "3 lost of 6 frames" was 3 of 3: a coded link losing 30-60% of its packets fired on 13-17% of the cycles (dev set B) while link_env counted it not restored. The slack of restored_plr allowed two packets of a coded run whether they were one event or two. After the link turned coded the pools let its first frames deliver fully coded packets whose codewords began before the switch. The space-time receiver had no failure path for a Cholesky factor or an MVDR normalization; extract_closed_loop_frames hid a model with fewer than eight rows of output 6; the closed-loop onset latency counted one cycle more than the detector's; D77's AGC, held over the frame, was not measured under gated interferers. Text: the sync flag is reported, not an erasure (D78); the space-time pilot error is not held out from every pass.
**Decision:**
- *Coded loss window (policy_monitor.m).* A coded link's packet loss is read over 2 C.win = 10 frames, the C.win = 5 packets an uncoded link reads, and degrades when the window's lost packets exceed C.ratio_ok x the clean coded loss over its packets plus one loss event of Q/2 = 2 packets (the slack below): 3 lost of 5 while the clean coded loss stays below 10%, 4 of 5 at the clean link's 0 dB (11.7%). Dev firing rates on the steady coded link (every cyclic window of every flight, decoded as the link does; dev_monitor_window.m), share of cycles [%]:

| point | packet loss | 3 of 6 frames (D78) | 3 of 10 frames | 4 of 10 frames | flights losing 30-60%: 3 of 6 | the same: 3 of 10 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 16 dB jammer, spatial_diversity, 6 dB | 27.2 / 29.8 | 15.5 / 19.0 | **26.2 / 28.7** | 19.0 / 22.3 | 16.7 / 11.7 (12 / 6 flights) | **43.3 / 26.7** |
| same, 12 dB | 6.5 / 10.5 | 2.7 / 3.2 | **4.0 / 6.8** | 2.5 / 3.2 | 15.0 / 13.3 (4 / 9) | **35.0 / 34.4** |
| 30 dB jammer, 234 ns, ρ 0.3, MMSE, 12 dB | 20.5 / 30.0 | 9.3 / 15.8 | **19.0 / 28.0** | 11.0 / 17.5 | 12.7 / 12.1 (15 / 14) | **36.0 / 40.7** |
| same, 15 dB | 18.2 / 23.0 | 7.3 / 8.5 | **14.8 / 20.7** | 7.0 / 10.2 | 14.6 / 7.5 (13 / 12) | **44.6 / 45.8** |
| clean, MRC, 0 dB | 11.7 / 11.5 | 2.8 / 5.0 | 9.8 / 9.8 | **3.8 / 5.7** | 6.3 / 6.7 (8 / 9) | 38.8 / 32.2 |
| clean, MRC, 3 dB | 1.7 / 1.5 | 0.3 / 0 | **0.7 / 0** | 0.3 / 0 | 20 / 0 (1 / 1) | 40 / 0 |

Set B / set A; in bold the new rule at the point (3 of 5 packets where the clean coded loss is below 10%, 4 of 5 at 0 dB).

  Under the jammers the new rule fires 1.5-2x as often as D78's, and on the flights losing 30-60% of their packets 35-45% of the cycles against 13-17%; a window that degrades stays degraded over the following cycles while the lost packets are in it, so the escalation (3 degraded cycles in a row) follows. On the clean coded link at 0 dB the rule needs 4 of 5 (3.8% of the cycles on set B, 5.7% on A; 3 of 5 would fire on 9.8%); at 3 dB 0.7% / 0%.
- *Slack of restored_plr (packet_share.m).* One loss event of the run, counted in packets: uncoded one frame; coded the run's longest run of consecutive lost packets (cyclic, as the run's pairs are), at most Q/2 = 2, at least one, of its packets. The same rule for 20-frame pools and 57-frame map runs (map_survivability_boundary.m now passes each cell's loss pattern, experiment_survivability_options.m each geometry's). Smoke check on the dev flights (10 packets per flight, 360 flights per set, clean reference 0 at the jammed points and the same flight uncoded at the clean ones): flights that pass with one packet / two packets (D78) / one event: set B 33/37/36, 51/55/55, 37/39/39, 37/43/42, 60/60/60, 60/60/60 (the six points of the D78 table); set A 32/38/38, 45/50/49, 28/34/33, 32/40/37, 60/60/60, 60/60/60. Against D78's rule 2 (B) and 5 (A) flights flip to not restored, against the one-packet rule 14 (B) and 20 (A) flights stay restored: two lost packets that share a frame are one event.
- *Decode gap (link_env.m, rollout_policy.m).* When a configuration with fec_interleave reaches the link after one without, the first coded packet is decoded on the fourth coded frame: the three frames before (60 ms) deliver no packet and are neither restored nor restored_plr (info.fec_gap); T_rec counts them, the held run cannot start in them, and the packet loss after recovery (LINK_T) leaves them out (no packet is due on them). A change between two coded configurations keeps the code running. evaluate_policies.m and edge_map.m read both through rollout_policy.m. The reward is unchanged.
- *Receiver guards (build_threat_model.m).* The space-time covariance of the synchronization statistic (st_sync, st_coh) is loaded through st_load: the noise floor, or 1e-3 of the mean eigenvalue when the floor does not reach 1e-9 of it (a noise-free or empty quiet slot), so the Cholesky factor exists; codegen-safe (no two-output chol). The MVDR normalizations of st_first and st_dd take a window with |w'h0| <= 1e-12 to zero output (its pilots then count their full power in the pilot error, as pilot_mse does), so no NaN reaches output 6 row 8. Bit-identical otherwise: V12c with the gain held reproduces D77's table to the last digit.
- *Model check (extract_closed_loop_frames.m).* Output 6 must hold 8 values per frame; a model built before row 8 stops the run instead of reading zeros.
- *Onset latency.* The closed-loop t_cls / t_conf count from the onset frame as eval_detector.m does: the decision of cycle t reads frame t - 1, so 0 is a class or alarm on the onset frame itself; the printouts state it.
- *Fast attack of the AGC (adc_frontend.m, p.adc_attack_db = 6).* D77's gain, set per frame from the quiet slot and the first 16 short-training symbols and held (802.11 converges its AGC on the short training, IEEE 802.11-2007, 17.3.2.1), let an interferer that starts after those windows clip for the rest of the frame by up to its level less the 12 dB of backoff. Measured on dev flights (dev_agc_clip.m: seeds 7401-7415, 20 frames, the pools' geometry draws, 12 dB; the same flights with the front end off, held and with the attack; share of the rails clipped and frames lost, BER > 0.2, under spatial_diversity's MMSE):

| threat, level | rails clipped, held | frames with > 1% clipped, held | rails clipped, attack | lost frames: no front end / held / attack [%] |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| jamming 16 dB | 1.2e-4 | 0 | 7.8e-5 | 2.8 / 2.5 / 2.5 |
| jamming 30 dB | 1.4e-4 | 0 | 8.5e-5 | 21.1 / 22.8 / 22.5 |
| noise_burst 16 dB | 4.0e-2 | 28% | 1.8e-3 | 0.7 / 2.8 / 1.1 |
| noise_burst 30 dB | 4.9e-2 | 28% | 2.1e-3 | 2.1 / 11.9 / 6.0 |
| sweeping_jammer 16 dB | 5.4e-3 | 1.8% | 1.3e-4 | 0.4 / 1.1 / 0.4 |
| sweeping_jammer 30 dB | 7.2e-3 | 1.8% | 1.6e-4 | 2.8 / 3.5 / 2.5 |
| reactive_jamming 16 dB | 7.6e-3 | 12% | 9.1e-4 | 0.4 / 0.7 / 0.4 |
| reactive_jamming 30 dB | 1.2e-2 | 13% | 1.1e-3 | 9.5 / 14.7 / 11.9 |
| benign_interference 12 dB | 3.4e-2 | 12% | 7.9e-4 | 0.7 / 4.6 / 1.1 |
| benign_interference 30 dB | 5.3e-2 | 12% | 1.2e-3 | 7.0 / 13.3 / 7.4 |

  Under MRC (no_action) the held gain's clipping did not raise the loss (the links under these interferers are lost either way; a clipped burst is bounded like a limiter: noise bursts 59 → 52% and 99 → 92% lost frames), but under MMSE it bent the interferer out of the space the null removes and multiplied the lost frames by 1.2-6.6. So the AGC steps its gain down within the frame: after the training's start, a block of 16 samples whose mean rail power exceeds the level the gain was set for by more than 6 dB sets the gain to that block's level on the 1 dB grid, from 16 samples after the block on; only down within a frame, the next frame starts from its own windows. Behaviour: the AD9361 has a fast AGC mode (data sheet Rev. F, p. 33, without its timing); the CC2500 adjusts its gain whenever required unless frozen on a sync word, averaging 16 channel-filter samples and waiting 16 after a change (its reset values, data sheet SWRS040C, p. 76, AGCCTRL0). Assumed: those 16 + 16 samples at our sample rate (8 µs), and the 6 dB threshold (half the backoff: below it at most 2Q(2) = 4.6% of a Gaussian composite's rails clip; a 16-sample block of a steady composite exceeds its mean by 6 dB with a probability far below 1e-6, so a continuous jammer does not trigger it). On the 5720 dumped frames of the receiver's dev cases (dev_cases.m) the attack steps the gain in 12 frames (9 on the clean link at 6 dB, K 2 dB, where our signal fades more than 6 dB within the frame after a faded short training; 7-8 dB steps), the rest bit-identical. A residual remains at 30 dB (noise bursts 6.0 against 2.1%, reactive jamming 11.9 against 9.5%): the 32 samples before every step still clip. The clipped share of the remaining frames: 0.1-0.2% of the rails under the gated interferers.
- *Detector identity.* detector_id.m hashes the quiet-slot alarm threshold FZ.q_thr too: pools and agents built before this change carry another det_id.
- *Pools.* MODEL_TAG 'v7-D79' (build_policy_pools.m): the fast attack changes the received frames under gated interferers.
- *Dev scripts (code/dev/).* The thresholds and tables of D77-D79 run from the repository (outputs in tempdir/uav_gcs_dev or a given folder, never data/ or results/): dev_dump.m (the receiver's dev flights, dev_cases.m), dev_rx_script.m / dev_eval_rx.m (the Rx block over dumped frames), dev_sync_threshold.m (sync_coh_min 0.60), dev_adc_backoff.m (adc_backoff_db 12), dev_noise_floor.m (the 9-tap floor against 5 taps), dev_fec_flights.m (dev sets A and B), dev_erasure_threshold.m (erase_pilot_mse 0.80), dev_fec_table.m (D78's table, with dev_fec_packets_2f.m, D75's packet), dev_monitor_window.m (the table above), dev_agc_clip.m. bit_reliability.m is its own file (the decoder's soft values for the dev scripts and the tests).
**V12c on the final code (validate_phy('V12c'): the D77 receiver with D78's output 6, the guards and the fast attack; results/phy_validation_v12c.txt):**

Shift of the delay line against the flat channel [dB] at 64 / 138 / 234 / 302 / 372 / 1000 ns (the K 10 dB rows reach 767 ns):

| case | shift [dB] | limit | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| clean K −5 dB, MRC | +0.25 / +0.01 / −0.21 / −0.16 / −0.15 / +0.00 | 0.5 | PASS |
| clean K 2 dB, MRC | −0.19 / −0.24 / −0.24 / −0.25 / −0.26 / −0.17 | 0.5 | PASS |
| clean K 10 dB, MRC | −0.15 / −0.16 / −0.16 / −0.15 / −0.14 / −0.10 | 0.5 | PASS |
| jam 16 dB, ρ 0.3, MMSE | −0.98 / −0.84 / −0.63 / −0.44 / −0.22 / +1.53 | 3 | PASS |
| jam 16 dB, ρ 0.9, MMSE | −1.14 / −1.10 / −1.06 / −0.92 / −0.79 / +0.09 | 3 | PASS |
| jam 30 dB, ρ 0.3, MMSE | −0.16 / +0.00 / +0.00 / +1.38 / +2.56 / +14.22 | 3 | FAIL at 1 µs |
| jam 30 dB, ρ 0.9, MMSE | −3.27 / −2.76 / −1.95 / −1.06 / −0.18 / +3.00 | 3 | PASS (at the limit) |
| jam 16 dB, ρ 0.3 / 0.9, 358 Hz, 234 ns (informational) | −0.04 / −0.77 | – | – |

Channel model: the flat channel fails, the link runs the delay line → PASS; receiver on the delay line: FAIL (30 dB, ρ 0.3, 1 µs), as in D77. The same rows with the gain held (the guards alone) reproduce D77's table to the last digit; the fast attack moves only the flat reference curves under the jammers (a few frames step: flat BER within 4% of held) and the 30 dB ρ 0.3 shifts by 0.01 dB. Run as two processes of validate_phy('V12c', rows), rows [1 2 3 8 9 4] and [5 6 7] (the seeds are 4000 + row, as in a single run), merged in table order.

**Tests:** test_packet_loss_once (the event slack: one packet, an adjacent pair, two separate losses, the cap, the wrap, 56 frames, Q = 2; ten-frame window: two lost of five not degraded, three degraded eight frames later), test_coded_degraded_after_arrival, test_fec_restoration_start (the gap after the arrival at delays 0 and 1, none between coded configurations; T_rec 3, LINK_T without the gap), test_bit_reliability (first bit on Im, second on Re of conj(g) z, against pskmod / pskdemod), test_det_layers (DET_u, the third output), test_sync_frequency_sign (+-30 kHz found with its sign on the space-time path), test_detector_id (q_thr), test_adc_frontend (the fast attack: a +20 dB block steps the gain 32 samples later to 12-13 dB above it, a steady frame unchanged).
**Adoption:** before any v7 reading; A5, C1p, C2 and every later stage rerun (the front end, the monitor's coded observation, the restoration counts and the detector identity changed). validate_phy's checks on the final code (results/phy_validation.txt): V1-V7, V9, V10 and V12 unchanged, V8a to three digits; under the 10 dB jammer (MMSE, ideal receiver) the attack steps the gain in a few frames: V8b 2.32e-2 / 7.62e-3 / 1.84e-3 / 3.22e-4 / 4.55e-5 / 9.99e-6 (held: 7.64e-3, 3.25e-4, 4.60e-5 at 2, 6, 8 dB), V8c and V8d fewer errors at 4-10 dB (V8c 742 → 697 at 6 dB, 30 → 15 at 10 dB); V11 losses +0.95 / +1.01 / +4.76 dB (held +4.77).

## D80 — KPI 7: the unknown-threat score within the decision-cycle budget, a fast isolation-forest scorer
**Why (2026-10-06, v6 latency measured on an idle machine):** KPI 7 (decision latency per 20 ms cycle, median < 10 ms and p95 < 20 ms; the low latency class of Oli & Mahalal, IEEE Access 2025, Table 8, see D49) failed in profile 1 at a median of 87.8 ms (p95 98.0 ms) and passed in profile 2 at 2.94 ms. The detector alone took 85.8 ms: the leave-one-threat-out selection of eval_ood_detection.m picked last_or_if in profile 1, and its isolation-forest part, isanomaly, visits every node of every tree in an interpreted loop whatever the number of frames (100 trees of up to 16383 nodes), about 85 ms for one frame. The selection compared AUROC only, so a score the deployed cycle cannot afford could win it.
**Decision:**
- *Fast isolation-forest scorer (iforest_pack.m, iforest_score.m).* The trained trees are exported once through the toolbox's code-generation export (toStruct) into flat arrays (split variable, split value, children, the leaf's c(n)) and kept in the unknown-threat model (M.forest_pack, fit_ood_model.m; eval_ood_detection.m and demo_gui.m add it to a detector or bundle trained before). The scorer walks all trees at once, one vector step per tree level, and returns isanomaly's 2^(−E[h(x)]/c(ψ)) with the same rules: x < split to the left, x ≥ split to the right, a missing value stops the walk without the leaf's c(n), c(n) = 2H(n−1) − 2(n−1)/n at the leaf (Liu, Ting & Zhou 2012), the predictors that were constant at training dropped. On the validation split of the smoke data (924 frames, the trained forest) it is identical to isanomaly (largest absolute difference 0), also with missing values and one frame at a time; one frame 41.5 → 0.51 ms. ood_score_set.m uses the packed trees when present, isanomaly otherwise.
- *Latency-aware selection (ood_latency.m, kpi7_admit.m, ood_select.m).* Before the folds, eval_ood_detection.m times every candidate on the production detector the way measure_latency.m times the detector: detect_scores.m on one validation frame per call (one forward pass with the score), each device in its own passes (GPU when present, then CPU), 10 warm-up calls, GPU work synchronized before the clock is read, median and p95 over 200 calls (30 in the smoke run). A candidate runs on the device with its lower p95 (the device rule of measure_latency.m). Budget: the detector with its score may take at most half of each KPI 7 target, median ≤ 5 ms and p95 ≤ 10 ms. The other half covers the rest of the cycle: spectrogram, link features and DQN policy measured 1.33 + 0.05 + 0.60 = 2.0 ms median and 1.66 + 0.06 + 0.82 = 2.5 ms p95 in v6 (profile 1), so the rest keeps a margin of 2.5× on the median and 4× on the p95. A candidate over the budget is excluded from the selection and from the nested estimate (the pair chosen on the other held-out threats is chosen among the admissible pairs; the method is otherwise unchanged); its AUROC stays in the table, and the report lists every candidate's cost, device and verdict (results/ood_detection.txt; LC in ood_detection.mat). When no candidate fits, the cheapest is kept with a warning.
- *pre_features.m* takes the device of detect_scores.m: it used the GPU whenever one was present, so the CPU timing of last_pre ran its gradient pass on the GPU.
- *Reports.* measure_latency.m records the detector's score (LAT.score); measure_all_kpis.m lists the admissible candidates and their costs beside KPI 2 and the score beside KPI 7.

**Measured costs (smoke detector on this machine, idle; median / p95 ms per frame, GPU | CPU):** last 1.26 / 2.07 | 2.16 / 2.52; ensemble 3.60 / 4.52 | 4.26 / 4.81; raw 1.72 / 1.99 | 2.11 / 2.37; last_or_raw 1.43 / 2.38 | 2.01 / 2.55; last_or_if 1.75 / 2.93 | 2.52 / 3.48 (with isanomaly 42.9 / 71.4 | 43.6 / 46.5); last_pre 10.0 / 51.6 | 4.40 / 21.6 at the selected step 0 (its second forward pass alone) and 31.6 / 39.5 | 30.0 / 33.9 at a step of 0.002. last_pre is excluded, every other candidate fits; profile 1's last_or_if stays a candidate at 1.75 ms.
**Tests:** test_iforest_fast (the packed scorer against isanomaly: a constant predictor, a missing value, one frame and 201; ood_score_set with and without the packed trees), test_ood_latency_budget (the edges of the budget; the selection never picks an excluded pair, neither for the production score nor for the nested estimate).
**Adoption:** OOD and every later stage rerun in each profile (the selection can change, and LAT, the pools and the GUI bundle read it). The isolation-forest scores, and so every candidate's AUROC, are unchanged.

## D81 — Temporal fusion: the current frame's own evidence beside the window
**Why (2026-10-07, v7 B2F on the validation split):** since D75 a sweeping-jammer frame is labelled by its own hit (threat_active.m): 14.1% of the frames of the sweeper's validation sub-runs carry the class, in 804 bursts of mean length 1.08 frames, 95% of them a single frame. The fusion input of fuse_classes.m held only window quantities (temporal_evidence.m: the window's mean log-probabilities and the nine persistence measurements), so with N > 1 the hit frame and its clean neighbours share nearly the same evidence: out-of-fold sweeping_jammer F1 fell from 83.4% at N = 1 to 46.3 / 41.8 / 30.7 / 27.4% at N = 3 / 5 / 8 / 12, and the selection fell to N = 1 (macro-F1 81.3%, the per-frame reading 81.1%). With the current frame's log-probabilities added to the same input the out-of-fold macro-F1 is 84.0 / 84.6 / 85.3 / 85.8% at N = 3 / 5 / 8 / 12; at N = 12 sweeping_jammer 89.2% and none 94.0% (89.5% without), and no class loses more than 0.5 points against the window alone (same folds by sub-run, lambda 1e-4, validation split only). Judging the sweeper's clean neighbours by the hit in their window instead (as fusion_target.m does for WLAN gaps) gives 85.5% at N = 12 with none at 89.6%: not taken.
**Decision:**
- *Fusion input (fuse_classes.m).* 'windows' appends log(max(p, 1e-6)) of the frame's own C detector probabilities after temporal_evidence.m's C + 9 columns: C + 9 + C columns. temporal_evidence.m is unchanged, so the agent's state (the nine window measurements per cycle, policy_state_size.m) keeps its size.
- *Decision cycle (policy_monitor.m).* The fusion is applied to the same row: temporal_evidence.m of the window, then the log-probabilities of its newest cycle (the current frame); M.te stays the window measurements only. Every other reading goes through 'windows' (select_fusion.m, eval_detector.m, fused_class.m for detector_block.m and edge_map.m) or through policy_monitor.m (the closed loop, measure_latency.m, the console and the 3D view); export_gui_bundle.m carries data/fusion.mat.
- *Guard.* fuse_classes('apply') stops when the input's columns differ from the fitted model's (a data/fusion.mat from before this entry).
- *Pools re-keyed, not simulated again (rekey_pools.m).* A pool holds the per-frame detector outputs only (pool_cell.m; build_policy_pools.m and build_check_pools.m pass the detector without the fusion), so the new fusion changes only PP.fuse, PP.fuse_N, PP.q_thr and PP.det_id of data/policy_pools.mat and CK's of data/check_pools.mat; each file is copied to <name>_before_rekey_<time>.mat first. It stops, changing nothing, when the pools come from another detector network, unknown-threat score or threshold (det_id recomputed with the pools' own fusion), hold a field not known to be independent of the fusion, or a frame field that is not a per-frame output. The clean-link pools (CT) hold no detector keys and are left as they are.
**Tests:** test_fusion (the new layout; a model of another layout stops), test_fusion_current_frame (isolated hits among clean neighbours at N = 12: the window alone finds fewer than half of them, with the current frame's columns more than 90% of hits and of neighbours; the decision cycle gives the same probabilities as 'windows' cycle by cycle, and M.te keeps its size), test_rekey_pools (only the four keys change, the backup, a file without keys untouched, the three stops).
**Adoption:** B2F, B3, B4 and B4s rerun; then rekey_pools from the repository root (C1p's frames are unchanged and are not simulated again); then C1c and every later stage. The check pools are re-keyed after the policy pools and stay newer than them; the clean-link pools are older than the re-keyed policy pools, so C1c rebuilds them by its own freshness rule.

## D82 — The test after the fusion fix: a second detector test set, new seeds for B4 and B4s, stricter alarm confirmations, KPI 6 over every independent clean flight
**Why (2026-10-07, before profile 1's C1p ends):** D81 changed the detector's decision after B3, B4 and B4s had read their test frames in profiles 1 and 3 (the first reading, 2026-10-06/07). Proposal mitigations 9 and 17: the test set is read once, and a change adopted after a reading is measured on new test runs. Rerunning B3 on the same test split would be a second reading of frames that already informed the decision to change. The review of the full-scale chain (2026-10-07) also found: (1) in v6 no DQN run met the false-alarm bound on validation (5%, one-sided 95%), and with fa_bound_met false the edge map commits no point (D74); (2) KPI 6 asks for at least 600 independent clean episodes above the KPI 1 threshold, and C1c gave 100 per Eb/N0, so a threshold at 6 dB or higher left KPI 6 NOT MET by construction (400 episodes or fewer), not by measurement.
**Decision:**
- *Second detector test set (B3n, build_fresh_test in run_stage.m).* run_dataset_sweep.m, extract_spectrograms.m and prepare_data.m run with FRESH_TEST: the dataset's own recipe (every threat, every level, every Eb/N0, the balanced clean class, the sweeper's extra sub-runs, each sub-run at its own speed), 2 sub-runs per cell (the test split has 1), every one in the test fold, seeds 3,500,000 + run id and their own speed stream (rng 2029), z-scored with the training statistics -> data/splits_fresh.mat (checked: same classes, features, normalization, test fold only). No decision of the chain reads it before B3.
- *B3 reads both (eval_detector.m).* First the test split (outputs with the suffix _split: eval_detector_metrics_split.mat, confusion_matrix_split.png, accuracy_vs_snr_split.png, detector_predictions_split.mat), then the second test set under the standard names, which KPI 1, the edge map, the weak-point analysis and the console read. The KPI 1 threshold is chosen on the validation split in both. measure_all_kpis.m states which set KPI 1 was read on and gives the test split's reading beside it.
- *B4 and B4s on new seeds.* Blocks 4,500,000 + k (rng 4244) and 5,500,000 + k (rng 4345) in place of 4,000,000 + k and 5,000,000 + k: the same recipe and the same points, frames never read.
- *Stricter confirmations in the monitor grid (train_dqn.m).* class_drop 4/5 and 5/5 beside 3/3 and 2/2 (k-out-of-n detection: Norouzi 2006, Barajas et al. 2026; D48): a later alarm for fewer false ones, chosen on validation with the false-switch penalty, γ and the seed under the same bound; the rule fallback's monitor is chosen from the same grid. The alarm memory holds 8 cycles; policy_monitor.m stops on a longer confirmation. The grid doubles C2 (36 runs). [Correction 2026-10-18: γ is not chosen here. It was last chosen on validation in D64's grid and is one fixed value, 0.5, since D65; see D90. D84 doubled the grid again with two drop thresholds (d84_dec.md): `train_dqn.m` on 9b5c9f1 trains 72 runs (4 monitors × 2 drop thresholds × 3 penalties × 3 seeds).]
- *More independent clean flights (build_clean_test_pools.m, pool_seed.m).* 300 geometries per Eb/N0 in each clean set (validation, block 4, and test, block 15), 100 per geometry family 1, 3 and 5, seeds disjoint from every other block, the dataset and the B4/B4s ranges (test_seed_streams_disjoint).
- *KPI 6 over every independent clean flight (evaluate_policies.m).* The test pools' clean episodes (one per geometry) and those of data/clean_test_pools.mat, from the KPI 1 threshold up; the edge map already counts both (and the second test pools' 84 flights). Each source is also reported alone (KP.far_testpools, KP.far_cleanpools). With the test pools' 12 geometries per Eb/N0 that is 312 per Eb/N0, at least 600 (624) whenever two Eb/N0 points are above the threshold; the edge map has 396 clean flights per point.
**Disclosure:** the first reading (before D81) is kept in archive/prefix_D81_<time>/ of each profile (results/ and data/fusion.mat) and is reported beside the second; the B3 reading on the test split after D81 is a second reading of those frames and is labelled so.
**Tests:** test_monitor_m_of_n (4/5 confirms on the fifth cycle of 1 1 0 1 1, 5/5 on the eighth of 1 1 0 1 1 1 1 1; 3/9 stops), test_seed_streams_disjoint (families 3 and 5 of blocks 4 and 15, the 3.5/4.5/5.5 million ranges).
**Adoption:** profiles 1 and 3: B2F, B3n, B3, B4 and B4s after D81; profile 1 re-keys its pools (D81) and runs C1c, SURV and SURV3 before the other stages, profile 3's C1p starts after profile 1's SURV3 (the parallel turn); profile 2 runs every stage with both entries.

## D83 — The received level against a fixed noise floor, path loss as a step, a connector ahead of the receiver, shadowing from the attitude; a drop alarm and a lost branch in the decision layer
**Why (2026-10-08, with the user, after a validation-only diagnosis of the weak classes of the v7 reading; the user stopped the v7 runs and approved a rerun of every stage of every profile from A0):**
- *Distance was simulated by the noise.* Eb/N0 was set by the AWGN block's SNR against a fixed received signal, so a far clean link had more noise and the same signal, while a path loss lowered the signal under the same noise. The absolute level then told them apart where a receiver cannot: at equal SNR, path loss 10 dB at 15 dB Eb/N0 against the clean link at 3 dB had an RSSI of −14.6 against −3.2 dB (validation split), and the mean antenna gain and the fixed-dB spectrogram carried the same difference. A real receiver's floor is fixed (kTB times its noise figure, link_distance_km.m) and the field scales with distance.
- *A constant attenuation from the first frame is a longer range.* The path_loss class (82.3% recall, out of fold) was learnt from that absolute level; physically a loss present all along is the clean link farther away. The threat the sources name is a sudden loss: an obstruction, terrain or a building between the UAV and the GCS (up to 21.9 dB, Cui et al. 2019, D72). Moving away is no threat: free-space loss changes by d(20 log10 d)/dt = 8.69 v / d dB/s, at most 1.39 dB/s at 161 km/h and 0.28 km (the distance of 15 dB, link_distance_km.m).
- *An open connector attenuated our signal only.* build_threat_model.m scaled our signal before the interferers were added, so the faulty antenna kept a full-strength copy of the jammer: a clean jammer reference for MMSE in jamming+antenna_fault, noise_burst+antenna_fault and noise_burst+spoofing+antenna_fault, whose recovery was overestimated. A connector sits between its antenna and the receiver: it attenuates everything the antenna receives.
- *Airframe shadowing was a constant on a random antenna.* Sun et al. (Part IV, IEEE TVT 66(9), 2017) tie it to the manoeuvre: an event needs a roll above 5° and the GCS azimuth more than 30° off the heading (Sec. III, p. 7645); the hidden antenna's K-factor falls to about −16 dB (Fig. 12, p. 7647); the smallest roll of the measured events is 16.1° (Table II, p. 7648). v7 already draws the flight's heading rate, bank, wind attitude and GCS direction (flight_draws.m).
- *Decision layer.* A lost branch (fault or shadowing) leaves white noise on the antennas, where MVDR is MRC (R = σ²I gives w ∝ h; Winters, Salz & Gitlin 1994) and only pays its estimation loss (Besson 2021, eq. 20-21): the rule's spatial_diversity for a fault had no physical gain without an interferer. Once the fusion window has passed a path-loss onset nothing named the loss: the monitor had no alarm on the drop itself.

**Decision:**
1. *Fixed thermal noise (thermal_nv.m, set_link_snr.m, build_threat_model.m).* The AWGN block adds a fixed variance per sample and antenna (Variance mode), N0 of Eb/N0 = 0 dB at unit symbol energy (1 / bits per symbol). A gain block 'Level' between the threat block and the AWGN scales everything the antennas receive, our signal and every interferer, to the run's Eb/N0 (our amplitude 10^(Eb/N0 / 20)); every caller sets it through set_link_snr.m (dataset, pools, detector blocks, maps, gallery, latency, console and GUI, validate_phy, dev scripts, tests). JSR, INR and SIR keep the sources' definition, the interferer's level over our received signal (the in-band cap of D74 unchanged), so distance moves our signal and the interferers together. The receiver measurements take the fixed floor as their calibration constant (iot, q_iot, duty: extract_closed_loop_frames.m and iot_db.m no longer read the AWGN setting); RSSI and the antenna gains are absolute, as a receiver that knows its AGC gain index reports them. The detector image (spec_image.m) is in dB over the thermal floor of an STFT bin (nv times the window's energy) on [−25, 55] dB: noise at 0 dB (0.3% of its bins below −25 dB), our signal about 2 dB over it at 0 dB Eb/N0 and 17-18 dB at 15 dB, a capped noise-like interferer near 47 dB (a tone's single bin can reach 61 dB at the top level and Eb/N0, saturating a few pixels; the earlier [−40, 40] absolute scale saturated it by about 20 dB). Checked in absolute terms and unchanged by the scale: the AGC and the 12-bit ADC (adc_frontend.m sets the gain from the received power on the 1 dB grid, so a field n whole dB stronger gives the same quantized samples n dB stronger, with or without the fast attack, test below; the levels of the link span about 0 to 50 dB over the floor, inside the AD9361's 0-73 dB gain range at 2.3 GHz in 1 dB steps, data sheet Rev. F, p. 3), est_margin, mmse_gain, align, the slot's coherence and shape, SINR, the BER estimates, clean_ber_ref.m (keyed on the receiver's own Eb/N0 estimate) and the monitor's Eb/N0 estimate (SINR + interference over thermal = our signal over the floor); the receiver's numerical guards are relative or far below every level. BER against theory is scale invariant: validate_phy on this code below.
2. *Path loss as a step (build_threat_model.m, threat_active.m, fusion_target.m, run_dataset_sweep.m, detector_block.m, eval_unseen_snr.m, eval_unseen_severity.m).* The loss starts at an onset frame drawn per run in p.pl_onset_frames (drawn after every other draw, so the other threats' streams are unchanged); frames before it carry no threat (output 2 = 0). The detector's sub-runs (A5, B3n, B4, B4s: p.pl_onset_det, frames 2-11 of 20) put the step inside the run; a frame is path_loss while the onset lies in its link-feature window of 10 frames (threat_active.m with the frame positions: var_rssi, dlog_ber and plr see the step there), and the fused decision's target is path_loss while the onset lies in its fusion window too (fusion_target.m); later frames are clean frames, the same link as a longer range, and the decision layer's drop alarm carries the loss (item 5). The path-loss cells fly N_SUB F_SUB / 10 sub-runs (10 labelled frames of 20). The pools' path-loss cells keep the loss from the first frame (p.pl_onset_frames [1 1]): their episodes put the onset themselves (clean cycles before it, link_env.m), and the edge map reads DET on a path-loss flight after the same flight's clean link (item 5).
3. *Antenna fault ahead of the receiver (build_threat_model.m).* The open or lossy connector scales the faulty antenna's whole received field, our signal and every interferer, after the interferers are summed (and before the receive chain's gain error and the thermal noise). A matched attenuator at ambient temperature T0 passes kT0B / L of the noise at its input and adds (1 − 1/L) kT0B of its own, so the receiver's floor stays (physics derivation).
4. *Shadowing from the attitude (shadow_hidden.m, build_threat_model.m, flight_draws.m, link_seed.m).* Per frame, with the GCS direction turning at the heading rate: no antenna is hidden unless |roll| > 5° and the GCS lies more than 30° off the heading (Sun et al., Sec. III); then the end of the array farther from the GCS along the fuselage is hidden (the aft end when the GCS is ahead of the beam, the front end behind it), losing the level's depth and its line of sight (K −16 dB). The depth stays the severity axis (6-25 dB, D73); no attitude reaches the detector (RF inputs only). The draws are not conditioned: a flight whose attitude hides no antenna carries no threat (output 2 = 0, clean labels in the dataset). The dataset flies its shadowing cells N_SUB over the share of flights with a hidden antenna (shadow_hit_share.m, Monte Carlo over flight_draws.m: 74.2% at init_params), and the pools keep their shared geometries, so a shadowing flight keeps the clean cell's flight of the same geometry as its restoration reference; an episode whose threat is never on the air in its geometry is not counted as a threat episode (link_env.m K.on; policy_run_set.m, dqn_eval_batches.m: KPI 4/5, the edge map, the DQN selection). Fixed geometries (survivability map, gallery, console) fly a bank of 16.1°, the smallest roll of Sun et al.'s events, with the GCS abeam (p.shadow_view_roll_deg, p.roll_deg); a fixed bank flies its coordinated turn, heading rate g tan(bank) / v (the speed floored at p.turn_v_floor as heading_rate.m), and is a tilt without a turn at hover.
5. *Decision layer.* (a) rule_based_policy.m: antenna_fault and airframe_shadowing take power_control, and spatial_diversity+power_control where the receiver's predicted MMSE gain reaches C.rule_mmse_db (an interferer it can null). (b) policy_monitor.m, 'class_drop' (every setting of the train_dqn.m grid): the drop itself raises the alarm (drop_alarm: our signal over thermal noise, the 3-cycle median, at least PP.drop_db below the median of the last 10 cycles without an alarm; a reference that does not follow an alarm, so a step stays an alarm after the detector's window has passed it); the rule answers a drop alarm whose class has no mapping (none, unknown) with power_control, and rate_reduce+power_control when the link is also degraded (the rule's proposal on the next confirmed cycles), and leaves an alarm with interference in the quiet slot to the interference rule. The false-alarm guard: choose_drop_threshold.m picks drop_db on the train pools against every clean frame (now a candidate false alarm: the midpoint of the clean frames' 99th percentile and the path-loss frames' 5th, else the largest margin), above the moving-away floor (1.39 dB/s over the 12 cycles between the reference's oldest cycle and the newest: 0.33 dB; it stops below it, and train_dqn.m's grid drops a value at or below it), and the m-of-n confirmation and the drop setting are chosen on validation under the false-alarm bound (train_dqn.m, D82). (c) Beside KPI 1, never in its place: the branch level, antenna fault and airframe shadowing as one branch lost (branch_level.m; eval_detector.m metrics.branch, measure_all_kpis.m, export_report_numbers.m). The edge map's DET keeps each of the two as its own class and reads the branch level beside it (DET_b, no target, edge_map.m det_classes); a path-loss flight is read after the same flight's clean link at no_action (its onset), detected by the fused class or the drop alarm (det_frames, the check points too). (d) The comments of temporal_evidence.m (drop_w), policy_decide.m (escalation at no_action), apply_countermeasure.m and build_threat_model.m (MMSE "tracks a faulty branch") no longer describe an intermittent fault or a gain MMSE does not have.
6. *Consistency.* MODEL_TAG 'v7-D83' (build_policy_pools.m): pools of the earlier model are not reused. The GUI's spectrogram view uses the same floor and scale; README's assumptions state the received level, the step and the attitude.

**Stated, not backed by a source:** the far-end rule of item 4 is a geometric assumption (no source gives the hidden antenna of a line array on a small UAV; Sun et al. saw both underside antennas shadowed, with up to 16 dB between them), and their criteria do not name the side of the bank, so neither does the model; the step of item 2 happens between two cycles (a knife-edge transition at the UAV's speed takes a fraction of a second; no measured onset time of a blockage at 2.4 GHz is in the library); the onset range 2-11 of the detector's sub-runs is a construction (a clean frame before the step, the step inside the 10-frame feature window); the attenuator's noise of item 3 and the moving-away rate are physics derivations; a fixed bank as a coordinated turn is physics (the dataset's own turns come from heading_rate.m and bank_angle.m).
**Open:** the pools' windowed link features (var_rssi, dlog_ber, plr) and detector outputs are computed within each pool run, so at an episode's onset they do not see the clean frames before it: in the closed loop the detector meets a path-loss step through the fusion window only, and the drop alarm carries it. train_dqn.m's drop grid stays one value, 2 dB below the train-pool threshold (CFG.drop_steps); with the drop now raising the alarm on its own, CFG.drop_steps = [0 2] would let validation choose between the train-pool value and the lower one if the bound fails at the lower one (it doubles C2).

**Tests (code/tests/test_core.m, 83 tests, all pass pass):** test_fixed_noise_floor (the AWGN variance is thermal_nv; the quiet slot holds the same floor at 0 and 12 dB while RSSI rises by our signal; a jammer's slot power and IoT rise with our signal; the image's noise at its floor; the AGC and ADC scale exactly with the field), test_fault_scales_interferer (jamming+antenna_fault: the jammer is 20 dB and more lower on the faulty antenna, near the thermal floor there), test_path_loss_onset (no threat before the onset frame, our level down by the loss after it, the floor unchanged; onsets drawn inside the range; labels while the onset is in the window; fused targets), test_shadow_attitude (shadow_hidden's criteria and ends; the dataset share; the fixed views' bank and its coordinated turn), test_receiver_measurements (the GCS abeam, ahead of and behind the beam hides the aft, aft and front end; level flight hides none, output 2 = 0), test_threat_on_air (K.on and the threat episodes), test_rule_lost_branch_and_drop (rule mapping), test_drop_alarm (a 4 dB step raises the drop alarm within 3 cycles and power_control within 5; a 1.39 dB/s ramp over 3 s raises nothing). Every other test now sets the level through set_link_snr.m (test_delay_line compares the received power over the Level gain).

**validate_phy on this code (2026-10-11; results/phy_validation.txt, phy_validation_v12c.txt; four processes: 'flat' and V12c rows [1 2 3 8 9], [4 5], [6 7], the seeds 4000 + row as in a single run):**

Channel model: the flat channel fails, the link runs the delay line → PASS; receiver on the delay line: FAIL (30 dB, ρ 0.3, 1 µs), as in D77 and D79. The new model is the earlier one scaled per run (the received field g(signal + interferers) + thermal noise of variance nv is g times the earlier field + noise of variance nv / g², the earlier noise), so every check without a jammer is unchanged to the last digit; under a jammer the AGC's 1 dB gain grid now sits at another point of the composite's level, which moves the clipped rails and the jammed rows by a few hundredths to 0.2 dB (V11 under the 10 dB jammer +0.20 dB, the 16 dB V12c rows by up to 0.14 dB, every verdict unchanged).

**Smoke chain:** run_stage('smoke', 'A0', 'A5', 'A6', 'B1', 'B2', 'B2F', 'B3n', 'B3', 'B3a', 'B4', 'OOD', 'B4s', 'C1p', 'C1c', 'C1d', 'C2', 'C2e', 'C2g', 'C2u', 'GAL', 'SURV', 'SURV3', 'OHP', 'GCSA', 'EDGE', 'LAT', 'KPI', 'DASH', 'WEAK', 'RPT') on this code from an empty data/ (a scratch copy, 2026-10-11): exit 0, all 30 stages, 5.2 h (C1p 191 min, SURV3 45, SURV 19, C1c 18, A5 14; validate_phy ran beside it for its first 2 h); A4v ran on its own (above) instead of inside the chain, its V12c taking about 3.5 h. A5: the attitude hides an antenna in 74.2% of the flights (397 of the shadowing cells' 504 frames labelled), path loss 240 labelled frames of 504 (10 per sub-run); B3 prints the branch level beside KPI 1 for both test sets; C1d's threshold 5.0 dB on the smoke pools (clean 99th percentile 4.7 dB, path-loss 5th 5.2 dB; floor 0.24 dB at the smoke grid's 12 dB, 0.40 km); the edge map writes DET_b; KPI, DASH and RPT carry the branch level. The only warning is the DQN gate on the smoke problem (as before). Smoke numbers are not results.

**Adoption:** every stage of every profile rerun from A0 (the user's approval, 2026-10-08): the received frames, the dataset's labels, the detector image and the link features, the pools (MODEL_TAG), the rule, the monitor and the evaluation's threat episodes all change.

## D84 — The pre-launch review fixed: the reactive trigger, shadowing where the airframe is in the line of sight, the cap over the run, labels, calibration and LoS Doppler; unknown threats chosen on validation; the monitor without our own gain; shadowing pools on hiding flights; the receiver on the delay line
