**Why (2026-10-11 to 10-13; the pre-launch review of v7-merge a66ebb9, findings L- link, T- threats, S- sources, D- detector, DL- decision layer, E- evaluation; notes docs/d84_link.md, d84_det.md, d84_dec.md, d84_shadow.md, d84_rx.md):** every finding was a physics, labelling or selection defect found before any v7 reading: the reactive jammer listened to our signal as the UAV receives it (after fading and a path-loss step), airframe shadowing hid an antenna on 74% of the flights at any depth, the in-band cap read the run's first frame, a combination was "off" when its last gated component was, the path-loss label window held frames with no clean frame in their link window, the receiver compared its levels with its floor perfectly, our LoS Doppler was drawn apart from the GCS direction, the unknown-threat pair was chosen on test frames, the monitor read its own configuration's gain as a drop, the shadowing pools flew 5-20 hiding flights of 72 per point, and the MRC receiver on the delay line floored on long channels.

**Decision (group A, the link, the threats and the dataset; d84_link.md):**
1. *Reactive jammer* (build_threat_model.m): triggered by an energy detector on our transmitted frame (the Tx output at the frame's arrival), on from the start plus 3.75 µs (the shortest jamming-initialization delay of Wilhelm et al., Fig. 4a, digitised) to the frame's end (Xu et al. 2005, Sec. 2).
2. *Airframe shadowing* (shadow_hidden.m, shadow_roll_band.m, flight_draws.m): an antenna is hidden only with the GCS above the belly's horizon (cz < 0), more than 30° off the heading, at a roll inside the level's band from Sun et al.'s C-band fit of the median loss against the event's largest roll (Table II: A 12.4 dB, n 0.25 dB/°, σ 4.4 dB, rolls 16.1-58.3°, ±1.96σ); none below 8 m/s (Khawaja et al.'s slowest flying UAV); fixed views bank at the roll whose fit equals the level. Flights hiding an antenna: 5.6-24.7% by level (D83: 74%). The dataset's shadowing cells fly hiding flights only, one per fold, chosen from the draws before simulation.
3. *In-band cap over the run* (sig_weak_db.m, 'Cap' block): an emitter's power is fixed for the run at the level that stays within 30 dB of our signal on every antenna over p.cap_frames frames (Liu et al.; the attitude, airframe loss, shadowing and step included).
4. *Combinations and labels* (threat_active.m, run_dataset_sweep.m, benign_hit_share.m): per frame every component's on-air share (act_comp, output 3) and their union; the path-loss label while the step lies inside the link window (pos − t0 < TW − 1, 9 frames), 13 sub-runs per cell; the WLAN's labelled share measured from its schedule (0.574), 10 sub-runs; extra sub-runs rotate over every fold; the received Eb/N0 of every frame (ebno_rx, received_ebno.m) carried through dataset, splits, detector blocks and pools.
5. *Noise bursts* as Xu et al.'s random jammer (bursts and sleeps uniform, one-symbol slots, duty per flight in 0.2-0.775 between Xu et al.'s and Marti et al.'s); *delay spread* by three height bands of Table VII with a 2250 ns clip (half the measured 4.5 µs profile range: a physics bound); *calibration error* ±2 dB per antenna and flight (AD9361 Rev. F, Table 1; stream 'cal') on every absolute level the receiver reports; *our LoS Doppler* fd cos(el) sin(direction + heading turn) from the geometry.
6. *Sources* (A11-A13): the path-loss top re-anchored on Simunek et al. 2011 (knife-edge, measured 33-42 dB behind the last building; the levels to 22 dB kept as obstructions below it), the altitude 15-120 m, Heath's imbalance indicator worded as it reads (5 dB), the receive-correlation floor on Badi et al., the sweep periods and digitised values marked; citations outside the library removed (test_citations_in_library). A14: shadowing and obstruction scale the LoS and diffuse parts alike (stated).

**Decision (group B, detector and unknown threats; d84_det.md):** (1) the unknown-threat (candidate, window) pair is chosen on validation only (leave-one-threat-out on half the validation sub-runs, the nested estimate for KPI 2) and read on the second test set; (2) the windowed score is the monitor's statistic (ood_window.m, the last W cycles of the frame's own sub-run, partial windows at the start); (3) its threshold is the 5% quantile of that windowed statistic on validation; (4) KPI 1 bins every frame by its received Eb/N0 (kpi_ebno.m), frames received below the grid are reported beside it (never dropped), and a prevalence-normalized macro-F1 (equal priors, Bayes' rule) is printed beside KPI 1; (5) the augmentation masks fill with the image's thermal level (E[ln X] = −γ for an exponential bin); (6) the image scale stated, the architecture comparison on the second test set; (7) the machine's load logged beside the candidates' latency.

**Decision (group C, decision layer and evaluation; d84_dec.md):** (1) the monitor's drop windows take out the known Eb/N0 gain of the configuration each frame was received with (config_gain_db.m: power_control's step by Friis, rate_reduce's 10 log10 4 = 6.02 dB), and the answer to a loss of our signal is the smallest link-budget configuration that covers the compensated drop; (2) validation chooses the drop threshold between the train-pool value and 2 dB below it; (3) under 'class_drop' a path-loss component counts in DET by the drop alarm alone (det_frames.m); (4) the clean pools keep their seeds and draws; (5) SURV counts flights with the threat on the air; (6) the unknown and unseen-combination experiments keep K.on; (7) escalation only on a confirmed alarm; (8)-(9) the clean pools carry the detector and link identity, every KPI input its staleness (result_status.m); (10) the overhead pass read as a ramp (monitor_window_drop.m); (11) KPI 6 over every independent clean flight (396 per Eb/N0 at the full run); (12) KPI 4 and KPI 7 with intervals; (13) main.m runs every stage; (14) the hover label from the attitude hover flights fly. *rate_reduce in the receiver (L-F3):* the measurement route, not a despreading receiver: the simulated field is the despread link (our signal up by the processing gain against the noise and every interferer not spread like it), and the level measurements a despreading receiver takes before despreading (rssi, the detector image, gain_ant) are taken on the chip-rate field; the counterfeit spoofer, not spread like our signal, loses the gain too.

**Decision (shadowing pools; d84_shadow.md):** a cell holding airframe shadowing flies geometries of its own in every pool split, each the first candidate in its stratum whose own draws hide an antenna at the cell's level (hides_antenna.m); levels at the same rolls share flights (four groups: 8 | 12 | 15.5/20 | 25 dB); no flight below the level's speed floor (tan(bank) = ωv/g with ω at most 28.7°/s, a derivation) or 8 m/s; the clean link flies the same flights as their reference; seeds of their own (shadow_pool_seed.m, 10 000 001-61 199 999; B4/B4s hiding_flight.m inside their ranges); every shadowing reading carries the share of hiding flights beside it (shadow_note.m). Result: 72 of 72 flights per point on the air (before 5-20).

**Decision (the receiver on the delay line; d84_rx.md):** the clean K −5 dB row of V12c failed from 1 µs (+6.19 dB, +11.44 at the clip) after the LoS Doppler fix; diagnosed on dev flights (seeds 7601-7730) as the MRC receiver's, not the Doppler's nor the channel's (a genie linear receiver reaches 22.8 dB median SINR there). Fix (build_threat_model.m Rx script, rx_taps.m; MRC on the line only): the training's energy searched over the line's delays, the frequency where its sum over delays peaks (Morelli & D'Amico 2007), lags moved by whole symbols to the most training energy, a robust joint-LS first pass with per-block phase and gain, the residual frequency from the pilots' slope, and a 21-tap equalizer. Dev flights at 15 dB: K −5 dB, 1 µs 3.7e-4 → 4.0e-7 (8 → 0 frames lost); the clip 6.9e-3 → 3.4e-6 (208 → 0). The flat and MMSE receivers are unchanged.

**Integration (this merge):** *the chip-rate field exactly* (task L-F3 above). test_rate_reduce_chip_level failed on every branch: under a 10 dB jammer the detector image under rate_reduce read 0.93 dB below no_action (tolerance 0.5 dB), because the referral scaled the receiver's own decision-directed estimate of our signal, which a jammed receiver gets wrong. The model now exposes our own received component when p.proc_gain_db > 0: the threat block's output 4 (after the path-loss step, a faulty connector and the chain errors), logged as Thr_sig, times the Level gain and the antenna's calibration error, is exactly our signal's part of the received samples; extract_closed_loop_frames.m subtracts (1 − 10^(−g/20)) of it from every sample before rssi and the image, so the level measurements see the chip-rate field a despreading receiver sees (a receiver-side measurement emulation, never a decision input; models without the output keep the decision-directed fit). The one approximation left: the AGC and the 12-bit ADC act on the despread field (the AGC sets its gain on the training, up to the processing gain lower than a chip-rate front end would; with the gain taken out again and the quantization noise far below the thermal floor, the levels differ only where the ADC clips). Measured on the test's flights (10 dB jammer, 9 frames): on the same antenna the chip-rate field equals no_action's to −57 to −62 dB of its power (the ADC's quantization), its image and rssi within 0.001 dB; the 0.93 dB the test read before (0.65 dB with the exact referral) came from the reference antenna, the one of the best despread SINR, which under the jammer differs in 3 of 9 frames once the processing gain makes the despread estimates work: a receiver's choice, not a referral error. test_rate_reduce_chip_level compares the levels on the frames with the same reference antenna, tolerance 0.05 dB (was 0.5).

**Stated, not backed by a source:** the C-band fit for 2.4 GHz and the 1.96σ band; no shadowing below 8 m/s; the reactive detector's threshold and zero detection time; the noise-burst slot and duty law; the delay-spread clip; the calibration error uniform and independent per antenna; A14; the shadowing readings hold conditioned on a hiding flight (the share stated beside); the per-split strata weigh the speeds equally.

**Open:** a 33-42 dB obstruction (Simunek et al.) is beyond the levels; the MRC link under noise bursts and at 358 Hz with K −5 dB, 1 µs keeps a small floor (per-lag tracking would be next); V12c's jam 30 dB, ρ 0.3 row fails at 1 µs (as since D77); SURV3's shadowing combinations fly unselected flights; the 25 dB level has no flight below 44.1 km/h.

**Tests and validate_phy:** each group's tests in its note (D84's suite reached 122 tests); validate_phy('flat') every check PASS; V12c: the clean rows PASS at every spread up to the clip after the receiver fix; the receiver on the line FAILs only at 30 dB, ρ 0.3, 1 µs, as before.

**Adoption:** the link, the dataset's labels and cells, the pools and every later stage rerun from A0.

## D85 — The receiver's idle time, branch and step cues, per-cycle labels, the onset of a lost branch; KPI 1 as the proposal words it; a larger second test set
**Why (2026-10-12 to 10-15; notes docs/d85_obs.md and docs/d85_onset.md):** a validation-only study of the weak detector classes on fresh D84 data (6 828 sub-runs, never a test set) read WLAN 62.7% [57.1, 68.2], sweeping jammer 89.5%, path loss 65.3% (4 and 7 dB steps 5% and 24%), antenna fault 52.3% [43.6, 60.3] and airframe shadowing 61.1% [54.3, 68.4], every other class 96.6-99.8%. Three causes the receiver can measure: it saw 0.69 ms of every 20 ms cycle (a WLAN's 0.27-3.2 ms packets and a sweep's 5.25 ms dwell fall mostly into the idle 19.3 ms; Wollenberg et al., Liu et al.), the fusion read no fading statistic of a lost branch and no step of our level, and the labels judged the frame alone. A fault and shadowing both lose one branch; in the steady state they differ only by the hidden antenna's lost line of sight (K about −16 dB, Sun et al. Fig. 12), noisy over a few cycles, while their onsets differ physically: an open connector steps within a frame, shadowing grows as the bank rolls in.

**Decision (obs):**
1. *Idle-time RSSI per antenna* (build_threat_model.m records the on-share of the WLAN's and the noise bursts' schedules in every idle bin with no draw moved, bit-identical frames; idle_rssi.m measures every emitter's mean power per antenna and bin from the run's own block values, idle_context.m; idle_summary.m keeps 24 values per idle time). The RSSI is the CC2420's that Grimaldi et al. read: 18.5 kHz, 128 µs average, 1 dB steps (Sec. II-B, III-A, Table III), the AD9361's ±2 dB calibration, its spread Gamma(M,1)/M with M = BT = 160. Busy share by Cheema & Salous's duty rule (eq. 1); burst, gap, contrast and ripple features (Grimaldi et al.; Airshark).
2. *Window cues* (window_cues.m, 42 columns beside temporal_evidence.m's): 16 branch cues (the weakest antenna's K by the moment method, Aoki & Honda 2025 eq. 2, its spread, correlation and offset trend against the others; Willgert, Heath), 5 step cues (the best split of the combined level and of the Eb/N0 estimate: t statistic with the pooled spread floored at 0.5 dB, size, age; Xie et al. 2021), 21 idle cues. **The antenna-index cue stays out:** D83's far-end rule is a stated assumption (no source says which antenna the airframe hides), so a cue on the antenna's place would learn the model's own assumption (it would add shadowing 69.2 → 78.4% on the study data; one column of window_cues.m should a measured installation fix it).
3. *Per-cycle labels* (threat_active.m, fusion_target.m, det_frames.m, link_env.m K.on): with the idle time observed, a gated threat (WLAN, sweeper, a combination holding one) counts in a cycle while it is on the air anywhere in it (act > 0 or act_idle > 0; proposal mitigation 3 over the decision cycle); the run's first frame keeps its own share; the cell sizes follow (benign_hit_share.m, sweep_hit_share.m per cycle).
4. *Fault against shadowing under an emitter of opportunity* (emitter_idle.m, emitter_reading.m; beside KPI 1, never in it): a connector attenuates everything its antenna receives, the airframe only the GCS's direction, so an emitter in the idle time separates them; A5 adds for fault and shadowing sub-runs the idle records with a WLAN of the block's packet law at a duty of 4.6-11.5% (Cheema & Salous, Table 6) at INR 0, 10, 20 dB; B3 prints the two-class reading per condition. Its packets inside our frames are not simulated; no source measures such emitters at UAV altitude.

**Decision (onset):**
5. *Roll-in and the shadowing ramp* (turn_attitude.m, roll_rate.m, shadow_frame.m): a flight whose shadowing onset the data place rolls in linearly at a rate drawn between Allen & Lin's measured roll-in (30° in 1.6 s, 18.75°/s) and the largest rate Gross et al. measured over a window as long as the roll-in (45.4°/s over 0.5 s, 35.5°/s over 1 s, linear between: a derivation); its heading follows as a coordinated turn; during the roll-in the hidden antenna loses f(x) = (3x − x³)/2 of the level, the rising half of Sun et al.'s event shape (Sec. IV-C, eq. 8), x the roll's progress from the airframe's entry into the line of sight to the bank, its K moving to −16 dB by the same share; the event counts while the loss reaches 5 dB (Sun et al.'s third criterion). The 'Att' block holds 10 values (roll-in start, rate, the event's start roll). A held bank is D84 exactly.
6. *Antenna fault onset*: the connector opens at a drawn frame (af_on, the threat stream's last draw), a step within one frame.
7. *Onsets in the data*: the dataset, B3n, B4 and B4s place both onsets in frames 2-11 (frames before them are 'none'); the two cells fly round(N_SUB F_SUB / 14.5) sub-runs; the pools' shadowing flights roll in so that the airframe enters the line of sight just before frame 11 of a 38-frame run, episodes reading their onset cycle there (event_rows.m, link_env.m; C.event_frame 11, C.event_run 38).
8. *Onset-shape cue and latched diagnosis* (onset_shape.m): os_rate and os_glr (the best step against the best linear ramp of the lowest branch's offset, Gaussian likelihood ratio) join temporal_evidence.m (C + 11 columns; the agent's state grows by two); per episode the monitor latches the cause 3 cycles after a branch falls 5 dB (Heath's indicator, Sun et al.'s criterion): shadowing when os_glr exceeds FM.os_thr, chosen on validation (select_fusion.m), else a fault; while latched, a fused fault or shadowing class reads the latched cause; it never raises an alarm.

**Decision (integration and measurement, this merge):** the fusion row is temporal_evidence.m's C + 11 columns, window_cues.m's 42 and the frame's C (75 at C = 11; fuse_classes.m's column guard stops a fusion of another layout); the idle-time model follows the roll-in's attitude (heading and roll at every bin, turn_attitude.m) and the connector's drawn onset (the idle time after the onset frame attenuated; test_idle_rssi_quiet_slot checks the block's own onset draw); the emitter of opportunity's seed offset moved from 19 to 3 (the roll-in's stream holds 19); det_frames.m reads the idle records with the onset's joins and latch; MODEL_TAG 'v7-D85'. *Measurement beside KPI 1, never replacing it:* analyze_weak_points.m breaks every class down by altitude, bank, delay spread, INR (benign and jammers: the frame's level over our signal plus our per-sample SNR, as set_link_snr.m sets it), the path-loss step and the onset inside the fusion window or after it; compare_weak_points.m tabulates any profiles' weak_points.json side by side; measure_all_kpis.m prints the edge map's DET_h pooled from the KPI 1 threshold up (detection on the flights the threat harms) and the latched cause per event (the share of events whose first latched cause is right, Clopper-Pearson 95%, and the median cycles from the onset to it); Mahalanobis++ (Mueller & Hein 2025, arXiv:2505.18032: the last layer's features l2-normalized before the class means and tied covariance) is one more unknown-threat candidate, last_l2, chosen only if validation picks it. *KPI 1 as the proposal words it* ("at least 90% macro-F1 above an SNR threshold, with the degradation curve below it and a confusion matrix for every threat"): its threshold is the lowest Eb/N0 from which macro-F1 stays ≥ 90% at every higher point, chosen on validation, and its status reads macro-F1 only; the stricter every-class reading (every class F1 ≥ 90% above its own validation threshold) is printed beside it with the per-class F1 and the confusion matrix, never hidden; train_dqn.m's EBNO_THR, evaluate_policies.m, the edge map and KPI 6 follow kpi1_threshold_db. *A larger second test set:* B3n flies 4 sub-runs per (class, level, Eb/N0) cell instead of 2 (the gated threats', path loss's and the lost branches' compensated counts scale alike; seeds 3 500 000 + run id, disjoint, test_seed_streams_disjoint), so KPI 1's test intervals narrow by about √2; cost about +1 h of A5-type simulation per profile.

**Library audit (2026-10-15):** the m-of-n alarm confirmation cites Norouzi 2006 (k-out-of-N detector) and Barajas et al. 2026, not Richards' book (never in the library; train_dqn.m, D48's and D82's wording corrected in place; on 2026-10-16 also D59's and D61's: the MMSE combiner's weights and the M − 1 nulls cite Winters, Salz & Gitlin 1994); the window mean of temporal_evidence.m is what it is, a mean over the window's cycles: it is Lee's local average power (20-40 wavelengths, as D72 quoted) only when the window spans that distance (10 cycles at 29 km/h cover about 13 wavelengths at 2.4 GHz, at 161 km/h about 72), and **at hover the fading barely changes between cycles, so the window's means average little of it** (a stated limitation); the path-loss severity covers the sources' range without changing the training levels: B4s reads 33 and 42 dB as 'above' levels (Simunek et al. 2011 measured 33-42 dB behind the last building, Figs. 6 and 11) and the survivability map's path-loss axis runs to 42 dB; sources in the library now cited (docs/LITERATURE.md): Abdullayeva & Valikhanli 2025 (a fused CNN + feature detector above either alone), Brown, Cai & DasGupta 2001 (Clopper-Pearson for the commitment claims: its coverage never falls below nominal), Wang et al. 2025 (about 40 dB of a CW jammer cancelled with two antennas, measured), Yanmaz et al. 2013 (a measured three-antenna UAV array: profile 1), Fang et al. 2024 (TGbn receiver text: −20 dBm maximum input).

**Measured gains (validation-style data, never a test set):**
- *obs* (weak_v2, 6 828 sub-runs, the D84 detector retrained there; the integrated code; N and λ by 5-fold sub-run CV on folds 5-6; fold 8 never fitted on), recall before → integrated with per-cycle labels: WLAN 62.7 → 89.5% [86.1, 93.1], sweeping jammer 89.5 → 97.8%, path loss 65.3 → 84.7% [81.4, 87.6], antenna fault 52.3 → 62.5% [54.0, 70.2], shadowing 61.1 → 69.7% [63.3, 75.7]; macro-F1 85.45 → 91.18%; none 96.6 → 96.7%. Fault against shadowing with an emitter of opportunity (fold 5 fit, folds 6 and 8 read): no emitter 77.2% / 72.7%, INR 0 dB 93.1 / 84.4%, 10 dB 98.2 / 97.4%, 20 dB 98.5 / 98.1%.
- *onset* (432 sub-runs of this branch's model, seeds from 6 100 001; the fusion without the per-frame network, sub-run CV): CV macro-F1 of the three classes 70.88 → 72.42%; the deployed reading (cue and latch) against none: antenna fault 61.6% [55.9, 66.9] → 68.8% [61.9, 75.4], shadowing 57.5% [53.6, 61.2] → 72.5% [68.0, 76.5]; after the window fault 63.2 → 68.5%, shadowing 59.7 → 81.2%; clean frames read as a lost branch 4.90 → 4.08%; the latch's cause per event at threshold −1: fault 83.9%, shadowing 88.8%.

**Stated, not backed by a source:** the RSSI's rate, averaging and step are the CC2420's (the AD9361 sheet gives only its accuracy); the RSSI spread law; the per-cycle label with no minimum time on the air; the emitter of opportunity (duty of ground 2.4 GHz channels, INR around Cheema & Salous's busy threshold, not inside our frames); tying Sun et al.'s event shape to the roll-in, the K-factor following it, the linear roll-in and its uniform rate; the rate bound linear between the two measured windows; the onset statistic's neutral linear ramp, 3-cycle delay, 20-cycle history and 5-cycle reset; only flights whose shadowing onset the data place roll in (shadowing that begins inside a held turn is not flown); the fault's onset at a frame boundary.

**Open:** only shadowing flights roll in (the roll-in's common gain change, under 2.5 dB at 30°, appears only around shadowing events); a shadowing event beginning inside a held turn has no onset model; the latch's threshold rests on fewer events than sub-runs; faults at 5 dB stay within the airframe's own spread and at 31 dB near the floor; the per-frame CNN now learns per-cycle labels whose in-frame evidence is absent for about three of four sweeper frames (the fused decision carries them through the idle cues); the WLAN at −12 dB (about 61%) and the 4 dB step (42%) stay where they were.

**Cost:** the idle model about +10% on every stage that simulates frames (0.16 s per 20-frame sub-run against 1.3-1.8 s); 24 singles per frame of storage; the fusion row 31 → 75 columns; B3n doubles.

**Tests, validate_phy and smoke (this code, one MATLAB process, a scratch copy):** code/tests/test_core.m, 139 tests, 26.0 min: 138 passed on the first run; test_rate_reduce_chip_level, rewritten as in D84's integration item, then passes alone. validate_phy('flat') every check PASS, 98 min (V1-V7 gaps +0.03 / +0.01 / +0.08 / +0.06 / +0.13 / +0.14 / +0.07 dB; V8a, V8b, V9 and V10 (16.24 against 16.23 dB) as D84; V11 +0.91 / +0.98 / +4.92 dB; V12 +0.49 dB; params_id 1989c0d4…). The source parse check found no problem. The smoke chain from A0 to RPT runs once, after the pre-launch review's fixes and the cue branch are merged; it has not run on this code.

**Adoption:** every stage of every profile reruns from A0 (MODEL_TAG 'v7-D85'): the threat block's records, the onsets, the labels, the cells, the fusion and the agent's state change.

**Note (2026-10-16, D86):** KPI 1, its validation threshold and B4/B4s read the monitor's class, the fused class with the cause of a lost branch latched from its onset shape (D86 item 10), not the fused argmax; the hidden antenna's K stays its own until the event counts (D86 item 6); the emitter of O4 is shadowed by the airframe (D86 item 2); every turning flight of every class rolls in (D86 item 3).

## D86 — The pre-launch review: airframe shadowing at Sun et al.'s burial, every class rolls in, the frame rule for the per-frame network, the monitor's class in KPI 1, checkpointed pools
**Why (2026-10-16; notes docs/d86_link.md, d86_det.md, d86_dec.md, d86_ops.md):** a review of the v7 code before the launch (D81-D85 and the cues C1/C2) raised 38 findings; 34 were confirmed. Four groups fixed them on branches from a5f0687 (link, det, dec, ops), each with a note, its measured numbers and a full-suite run (146, 148, 148 and 146 passed on the branches); the branches are merged on v7-merge. Where a verifier gave a corrected fix, that fix was applied. Every number below is a measurement on code or a count of the layout, none a result: no test split or second test set was read.

**Link (d86_link.md):**
1. *Airframe shadowing is paired with Sun et al.'s fit by the burial, not the roll* (physics-1, MAJOR). Their fit (Part IV, Table II) comes from events at 1.4-2.9 deg elevation, where the roll sets how deep the airframe lies in the line of sight; D85 checked the roll band on the bank alone and gave the full depth however far the GCS sat above the belly's horizon (our GCS lies up to about 23 deg below the UAV). The verifiers found 24-41% of the counted frames (96% at 25 dB) below the smallest burial Sun et al.'s geometry gives at the band's lowest roll. `shadow_equiv_roll.m`: the burial of an attitude and the equivalent roll req = burial + 2.9 deg (`p.shadow_el_deg`, the most severe of their elevations); an event is flown at the level when the equivalent roll of its deepest burial (the turn's bank, the GCS abeam) lies in the level's band, one check per event; within it the depth follows Sun et al.'s shape (Sec. IV-C, eq. 8) on the burial's progress. Fixed views (survivability map, gallery, console) bank by the fit's roll less 2.9 deg (6-12 dB 13.2 deg, 15.5 dB 25.6, 20 dB 43.6, 25 dB 55.0; D85: 16.1, 28.5, 46.5, 57.9); the map's shadowing views pass the beam at mid-run (Sun et al.'s U-turns passed it), so the 25 dB cell holds 24.6-25.0 dB over its run (it eased to 20.8 dB with the GCS abeam at the first frame). Speed floors from the bank the airframe can fly (`shadow_speed_floor.m`, `shadow_alt_floor.m`): 6-20 dB 28.8 km/h, 25 dB 39.2 km/h (D85: 44.1); the pools' Latin hypercubes draw the altitude first and cut the speed strata at its floor (`pool_geometries.m`). Where a level can be flown at 15 dB Eb/N0 the groups leave out up to 5.2% (16.1 deg) and 19.4% (32.0 deg, 25 dB) of the speed-altitude plane, the slow and high corner. The share stated beside every shadowing reading (`shadow_note.m`) is counted on unstratified candidates after each design (`SH.count`, 60 / 36 flights); the weighting by the altitude's reach was measured and not adopted (a Latin hypercube's kept/tried is the harmonic mean of its strata). Moved (4000 flights, held bank, 20 frames; D84 in brackets): hit share 6 dB 3.8% (5.6), 12 dB 17.2 (19.8), 20 dB 21.6 (24.7), 25 dB 11.9% (14.3); the A5 recipe keeps 3.1% (6 dB) to 19.5% (20 dB) of the flights tried; frames at the whole depth below Sun et al.'s smallest burial 0% (39%, 70% and 100% at 12, 15.5 and 25 dB before); the onset is a ramp (median loss 5.1-5.2 dB at the onset frame). *D74's stated limit, restated:* airframe shadowing is flown at Sun et al.'s burial; the GCS elevation itself (up to about 23 deg) lies outside their 1.4-2.9 deg, and they publish no heading differences, so the pairing takes the event's deepest burial with the station abeam, as their U-turns passed it. Tests: `test_shadow_burial`, `test_shadow_reach`, updated `test_shadow_attitude`, `test_shadow_onset_ramp`, `test_shadow_selection`, `test_cell_geometries`.
2. *The emitter of opportunity is shadowed by the airframe* (physics-2). O4 assumed the airframe hides an antenna from the GCS's direction only; by the model's own rule an emitter above the belly's horizon on the banked side is hidden from the same antenna. `emitter_idle.m`: the azimuth is uniform over the full circle; `emitter_elevation.m`: the farthest emitter that reaches the condition's INR at the licence-exempt density cap (ETSI EN 300 328 v2.2.2, 4.3.2.3: 10 dBm/MHz), free space at 2.4 GHz, floor -108.0 dBm (5 dB noise figure), a 10 m mast: 11.16, 3.53 and 1.12 km at INR 0, 10 and 20 dB, 0.56, 1.79 and 5.65 deg below a UAV at 120 m; `shadow_frame.m` at the emitter's direction in every idle bin. On the A5 recipe's kept flights the emitter is hidden on the GCS's hidden antenna on 11.7% (6 dB) to 18.2% (20 dB) of the draws and on some antenna on 24.3-34.9%, which the O4 table assumed to be 0%. `test_idle_emitter_attenuation` rewritten.
3. *Every turning flight of every class rolls in* (data-5). Only shadowing flights rolled in, so the antenna gain's common change and the heading-rate onset of a turn entry occurred before shadowing events only, and no test set held turn entries without shadowing. In the detector's sub-runs (`p.roll_in_frames`: A5, the second test set, B4, B4s) every turning flight rolls in from its own 'roll' stream; a non-shadowing flight's start is uniform over the times that put some of the roll-in inside the run, its labels unchanged. The pools keep their placement (their other cells hold the bank: a validation decision for the pools' owner). The 2.5 dB of the roll-in's common change in D85's notes was wrong: over 3000 clean flights p50 0.8, p90 4.6, p99 7.1, at most 11.9 dB; inside a 20-frame sub-run p90 1.0, p99 1.8 dB; 98.3% of the turning clean flights have a frame inside the roll-in. `test_roll_in_every_class`. *The false-alarm reading on the clean sub-runs' roll-in* (the review's corrected fix) is in B3 (`eval_detector.m`, `roll_in_false_alarms.m`; `class_run` and `roll_in` are carried from A5 to the splits): beside KPI 1, the share of the test frames of the clean sub-runs inside their roll-in read as any threat, as path_loss and as airframe_shadowing, next to the same shares over every 'none' frame (`metrics.roll_in_false_alarms`); `test_roll_in_false_alarms`.
4. *The interferers' Doppler phase turns with the heading* (physics-3): `los_phase.m` integrates fd sin(aoa + heading change) (closed form for a steady turn, 2001-point trapezoid over a roll-in, 1.5e-5 rad measured); the threat block, `idle_rssi.m` and `emitter_idle.m` take it. The old error was at most 2 fd sin(dpsi / 2): about 47 Hz over a 20-frame sub-run, 92 Hz over a 38-frame pool run, 139 Hz over the map's 57 frames at 358 Hz. `test_los_phase_turns`.
5. *Noise bursts are one of the two measured patterns per flight* (physics-4): half the flights Marti et al. 2023's J4 (Sec. VII-A, Fig. 5(d): one-symbol bursts at 20%), the rest Xu et al. 2005's random jammer with the duty uniform in 0.5-0.775 (bursts U[0, 31], sleeps at most 31 symbols). Below 50% the sleeps had gone beyond Xu et al.'s measured range, and at 20% 43% of the 32-symbol quiet slots held no jamming (0.08% under J4). No source gives how often each pattern is met: half each (`p.burst_j4`). `test_noise_burst_duty` rewritten.
6. *The hidden antenna keeps its own K until the event counts* (physics-5): its K stays the antenna's own while the loss is below `p.shadow_evt_db` (5 dB) and is -16 dB from the onset on (Sun et al., Sec. III and Fig. 12); the loss itself still ramps. The old K ramp gave the clean-labelled frames before the onset an almost Rayleigh hidden branch (about -10.8 dB at the 6 dB level's threshold for an own K of 15 dB), which the O2 branch-K cue read under the clean label. `test_shadow_k_onset`.

**Detector stages (d86_det.md):**
7. *The per-frame network and the unknown-threat models learn the frame rule* (data-1, MAJOR). The label of D85 O3 is per cycle (a gated threat counts while on the air anywhere in the 20 ms cycle) and went unchanged into the CNN's loss, class weights and best epoch, and into the Mahalanobis models' class means and covariance, the FGSM targets and the leave-one-threat-out sets, though the network sees the 0.69 ms frame and a 10-frame window only: the sweeper is on the air in 55.8% of the cycles but about 13.4% of the frames. A5 stores `label_frame` (`Y_frame` in the spectrograms and splits, `frame_labels.m`); `train_detector.m`, `fit_ood_model.m`, `ood_loto_split.m`, `compare_architectures.m` and the per-frame line of B3 use it; the fusion's targets, the onset threshold, KPI 1 and every fused reading keep the per-cycle labels. The gated cells fly the larger of the two needs, 46 sweeper and 10 WLAN sub-runs per cell (the second test set 31 and 7; the cell sizes D85's study flew); A5 3 936 -> 5 808 sub-runs (+47.6%), B3n 2 688 -> 3 984 (+48.2%). A held-out threat's own sub-runs stay out of its fold's known sets (their frames before the threat's hits are 'none' by the frame rule: about 87% of a sweeper sub-run, 43% of a WLAN's), which removed a conservative bias of the folds of the sweeper, WLAN, path loss, fault and shadowing. `test_frame_rule_labels`, `test_dataset_balance`, `test_ood_loto_split`.
8. *A4v, A5 and B3n never write params.mat* (data-2): a stop in the middle left the last level's parameters, which a resume wrote back as "restored" and the pools, LAT and the gallery would then read. The models are built in memory; `assert_frame1_onsets.m` stops a stage whose params.mat does not put every threat on from the first frame (hooks in five files); the run starts at A0. `test_params_written_by_init_only`.
9. *The extra sub-runs rotate over the Eb/N0 points* (data-3): `FOLD(nk + lv - 1)`; before, the n mod 6 extra sub-runs fell on the same Eb/N0 points in all 8 levels (fault and shadowing in the validation fold: 8 8 16 8 8 16 per point -> 10 11 11 10 11 11; the WLAN 8 16 16 8 16 16 -> 13 13 14 13 13 14). `test_dataset_balance`.
10. *KPI 1, its validation threshold, B4 and B4s read the monitor's class* (detector-04, integration-5): the fused class with the cause latched from the branch's onset shape (`latch_runs.m`, `select_fusion.m` saves `val_latch_oof`, `fused_class.m` returns the monitor's class and the fusion's alone, `metrics.fused_alone` beside KPI 1). D85 measured the latch moving fault and shadowing by 7-22 points. `test_monitor_class_reading`.
11. *The latch threshold is fitted on the events the deployed latch acts on* (detector-06): `onset_shape('events', G, j0, heal)` runs the deployed latch (heal 5) and scores the first diagnosis on the threat's branch; before, the first diagnosis anywhere (D85: the latch fires on 24.3% of clean 40-frame study sub-runs), about 1% of the fault and shadowing validation sub-runs. `test_onset_events_branch`.
12. *The unknown-threat validation halves are stratified* (detector-01, MAJOR): every other sub-run in run order tied the halves to Eb/N0 (for six classes the fit half held only 0/6/12 dB and the selection half 3/9/15 dB). Within each (class, Eb/N0) the sub-runs, ordered by level, go to the two halves in turn from the Eb/N0 index's parity: on A5's layout every (class, Eb/N0) and (class, level) has sub-runs in both halves, at least 4 and 3, and the halves differ by at most one. `test_ood_halves_stratified`.
13. *The features-only baseline trains without masking* (detector-03): `spec_mask.m` fills with the image's noise level, which painted bands on a zero image (`augment = false` for that variant). *KPI 2's summary* (detector-07) gives the deployed statistic (the production score over its window) and the nested FPR95. `test_spec_only_baseline_unmasked`, `test_kpi2_deployed_statistic`.

**Decision layer (d86_dec.md):**
14. *Clean-pool swaps keep the split's event frames* (integration-1, decision-1, BLOCKER): five places replaced a split's runs with C1c's 300 clean geometries and kept D85's event frames, so `link_env('tables')` indexed beyond its end and C2 would have stopped at its first `eval_clean_wide`, C2e and EDGE likewise. `clean_split.m` replaces every per-geometry field of the split; `link_env.m` stops with `link_env:event_frame` on a stale one. `test_clean_split_event_frames`.
15. *Every pool build keeps its finished cells on disk* (ops-4): `pool_checkpoint.m` (cell files with a key, flushed before the rename; C1p, C1c and the check pools). C1p is 73-107 h per profile and a power loss during v6's C1p had kept nothing; a resume loses at most the cells in progress (up to 6, the clean cell 22-32 h of a worker). Peak disk 2-3 times the output (12-15 GB for C1p), inside the floor. Resume check: the interrupted and the uninterrupted build equal bit for bit. `test_pool_checkpoint_resume`.
16. *Memory* (ops-6): the pool builders read the splits' norm alone (`split_norm.m`; 7 GB at full scale), C2 drops the splits 3-5 it never reads (its resident pools 12.44 -> 2.12 GB at the full layout), `build_check_pools.m` clears PP. `test_split_norm_memory`.
17. *The fusion window reads no step at our own configuration change* (detector-02): the window holds the antennas' gains less the power step and the SINR less the configuration's whole known gain (`policy_monitor.m`, `config_gain_db.m`); a release to no_action or a switch from a gain configuration put a 4-10 dB step into the window, the path-loss signature. `test_monitor_release_fusion`.
18. *EDGE reads rolling-in flights from their event frame* (integration-2, decision-2): LINK of the clean pool over `event_rows`, `det_frames.m` joins the clean lead-in; the LINK_T targets and DET of the three combinations with shadowing move. `test_det_frames_rolling_in`.
19. *Drop alarm: the window-limited CUSUM, chosen on validation* (the library audit's planned candidate; Xie et al. 2021, eq. 2-3, Theorem 1; Xie, Moustakides & Xie 2022, eq. 6-7, Remark 4, Lemma 6): `drop_cusum.m` (the three last drops held in the set of the envelope's path-loss levels 6-22 dB, bounded at twice the threshold so the alarm clears on the same evidence in reverse, `drop_ref.m` holding the reference through an excursion). C1d (`choose_drop_threshold.m`) reads both forms through the monitor's confirmation on the independent clean validation geometries, admissible when the one-sided 95% Clopper-Pearson bound of the false alarms is at most 5%; the CUSUM is chosen when it detects more than one point more (or within one with a shorter delay), else the median window, each deployed at the threshold it was compared at (`data/drop_threshold.mat`). Synthetic links at 2.4 dB spread: the CUSUM clears a median 3 cycles after a 6 dB drop ends (32 without the bound) and 1 after a 10 dB one (49); its clean 30-cycle false alarms at threshold 72 fall from 13.3% to 9.2% (confirmed 2/2: 11.9% to 8.5%). `test_drop_cusum_alarm`, `test_drop_cusum_bounded`.

**Operations (d86_ops.md; no stage of the chain changes its numbers):**
20. *The parallel turn cannot be held by a stale file* (ops-7): the owner counts only while a win64 MATLAB session runs under the recorded PID and started before the turn file was written (the process's start time, not the boot time: Fast Startup is on, and a hybrid boot on 2026-10-09 left LastBootUpTime unchanged); a waiting session prints the holder every hour. `test_parallel_turn_owner`.
21. *The machine's load beside KPI 7* (ops-11): `machine_load()` before and after LAT's timed passes, kept in `LAT.load` and printed under KPI 7 (a pass under load holds on an idle machine, a fail is measured again on an idle one). `test_latency_load_logged`.
22. *No stale file survives in a console bundle* (ops-9): `export_gui_bundle.m` deletes optional files that are stale or not produced (profiles/rx3 still held the 145 MB smoke detector). `test_gui_bundle_drops_stale`.
23. *The console's pools and shadowing as evaluated* (integration-3, integration-4): the episode pools store each antenna's gain (the fusion and the latch ran on fixed gains before); airframe shadowing rolls in (`console_scenario.m`, `console_flights.m`: a flight is kept when `hides_antenna.m` finds the airframe entering the line of sight just before frame 11 of 38 and the loss counting by frame 30; 0% of candidates at 0 and 20 km/h, 19-31% from 40 to 161 km/h), with the clean link of the same flight as lead-in. The console says the bank needed in the equivalent-roll space (25 dB: 29.1 deg, not 32.0). `test_console_shadowing_rolls_in`.
24. *The 3D engine resets the event flag* (integration-6, `v3d_engine.m`): `test_v3d_inject_resets_event`.
25. *Runtimes, the disk floor and the pools' memory* (ops-13): re-derived in "Cost and resources, re-derived (2026-10-16)" after D74's cost lines and the note in D61: a profile 145-173 h (about 6-7.2 days), the campaign of three profiles 345-457 h (14.4-19.0 days); the floor is 150 GB (disk_guard.m), a launch needs about 295 GB free; the pools hold 40.2 M stored frames per profile (416 / 456 / 376 B per frame on profiles 1 / 3 / 2, 15-18 GB in memory). README.md and docs/LITERATURE.md follow.
26. *Citations*: the claims "an N-element array nulls N-1 interferers" and the MMSE combiner's weights from the sample covariance (D59, D61, one clause of D85's library audit) cite Winters, Salz & Gitlin 1994 (Sec. I, p. 1740; eqs. 19-23, p. 1745), not Richards' book.

**D85 notes this changes (not rewritten above):** KPI 1, its threshold and B4/B4s read the monitor's class (item 10); the hidden antenna's K is its own until the event counts (item 6, D85 item 5 said "by the same share"); O4's emitter is shadowed by the airframe (item 2); D85's open item that only shadowing flights roll in no longer holds (item 3).

**Stated, not backed by a source:** the event's deepest burial at the bank with the GCS abeam stands for Sun et al.'s largest roll less their elevation (their heading differences are not published); the shape of the loss on the burial's progress over a held turn; the emitter of opportunity at the density cap on a 10 m mast, its loss the level wherever its burial reaches the band; half the noise-burst flights J4; the roll-in's start uniform over the times that overlap the run; the map's shadowing views passing the beam at mid-run.

**Open:** (a) the pools' non-shadowing cells hold their bank (a validation decision of the pools' owner); (b) shadowing frames whose burial lies below the level's band carry no loss, so the class is milder there than physics and the emitter's shadow is a lower bound; the GCS elevation spans up to about 23 deg against Sun et al.'s 1.4-2.9 deg; (c) the drop alarms (median window and CUSUM) start every episode from a fresh monitor whose reference is the median of the first few cycles: about 9% of synthetic clean 30-cycle episodes (2.4 dB per-cycle spread) raise the CUSUM at 72 because that reference was set high; C1d, C2 and KPI 6 read the clean episodes the same way, so their false-alarm readings include it, while a real flight starts the monitor once (a warm-up would delay the detection of onsets at cycle 3-10); (d) the hover flights are not in the clean validation pools; (e) memory: side by side the profiles peak at about 38-42 GB (C2-EDGE beside the next profile's C1p) and 35-42 GB (profile 2's B1 beside profile 3's C1p) against 31.2 GB of RAM; the 83 GB commit limit prevents an out-of-memory stop, and the review's memory item lowers the peaks *[2026-10-18: 83 GB was the limit with the 52 GB page file of 2026-10-16. The page file is system-managed; by 2026-10-17 23:45 it was 98 GB and the limit 127 GiB (docs/runs/analysis/overnight_audit.txt:108). An out-of-memory stop did happen, in profile 3's OOD at 22:02 on 2026-10-17 (PROJECT_LOG)]*; (f) the console's other scenarios still draw a new flight per build; (g) the launcher's items (the bounded wait for zero MATLAB sessions before LAT, archiving profiles/ with data/ and results/, exporting the bundle only after LAT and KPI succeed, RESUME mode) belong to the launcher; (h) MODEL_TAG in build_policy_pools.m is still 'v7-D85', so no data/ of an earlier D85 run may be present at the launch.

**Adoption:** every stage of every profile reruns from A0 (the threat link, the labels, the cell sizes, the folds, the attitudes and the emitter records changed). The merged suite runs at the launch gate; the smoke chain runs once on the merged code.

**Launch (2026-10-16; written 2026-10-18).** Where the numbers come from: the launcher's log `uav-gcs-logs/v7_d86_launch.log`, the gate logs `uav-gcs-logs/v7_d86/gate_<stamp>.log`, `v7_d86/code_commit_launch_20261016.txt` and the profile logs `v7_d86/p1.log` and `p3.log` (all outside the repository). The launcher itself is in `docs/runs/v7_d86_launch_20261017.sh` (a read-only copy, see `docs/runs/README.md`).
- *The launcher* (`v7_d86_launch.sh`, open item g). It takes the code as a commit SHA (SRC), never a branch name.
  - Launch mode, in five steps:
    1. the full `test_core` on a `git archive` export of SRC; N of N must pass;
    2. a dry merge into every profile; conflicts are allowed only in generated files (`params.mat`, `models/*.slx`, `results/`);
    3. the first reading of every profile is moved to `archive/first_reading_<stamp>/`;
    4. SRC is merged into v7-dev, and v7-dev into v7-p3 (a merge, not a cherry-pick); the code of every profile is then checked equal to SRC;
    5. the chains start.
  - Resume mode skips the gate, the archive and the merge. It checks that every profile's code equals SRC. It restarts each chain at its first unfinished stage, read from the `##### STAGE <s> done` markers of the current `##### PART` in the profile's log.
  - A heartbeat file, `v7_d86/run.lock`, is touched at least every 5 min. A resume refuses while that file is younger than 20 min and newer than the last boot.
  - Order: profile 1 runs every stage. Profile 3 runs A0-B4s, then C1p once profile 1 has passed SURV3. Profile 2 runs after profile 1, its C1p after profile 3's SURV3. LAT, KPI, DASH, WEAK and RPT run again on an idle machine at the end.
- *First launch refused by its own gate.* 10:43 on `47c7ac8`: `test_core` 165/166, `test_onset_recipes` failed (v7_d86_launch.log 10:43-11:08; gate_20261016_104305.log). The fix is `c2c8b8b` ("test_onset_recipes reads the sweep's roll-in on its base parameters"); its code differs from `47c7ac8` in that test only.
- *Launched 11:09 on `c2c8b8b`.* Gate 166/166 at 11:34:10 (v7_d86_launch.log; gate_20261016_110932.log). The first reading moved to `archive/first_reading_20261016_110932/` in both profiles. Merged: profile 1 v7-dev `27c2634`, profile 3 v7-p3 `26fffb5` (code_commit_launch_20261016.txt). Both chains started at 11:34:24; profile 3's C1p waits for profile 1's SURV3.
- *Smoke on the merged code.* The full smoke chain A0 to RPT with LAT ran on `47c7ac8` (the same code as `c2c8b8b` outside one test) and passed at 20:00. Every stage ran, C2, C2e and EDGE included; the only warnings were smoke-scale ones (STALE phy, no KPI 1 threshold, the DQN gate failed so the rules were deployed). Smoke figures are not results (run notes 2026-10-16 20:00).
- *Open item (h)* is closed: `47c7ac8` tags the pools `v7-D86`; the run resumed on 2026-10-17 tags them `v7-D89` (D89).
- *Windows Update.* The pause read by the launcher ends 2026-11-05 13:38 UTC (v7_d86_launch.log). The user decided on 2026-10-16 not to install the updates before then. The mitigation is the resume mode and the per-cell C1p checkpoints (item 15).

## D87 — The onset latch chosen on validation; a third detector test set (B3m)
**Why (2026-10-17; note docs/d87_fusion.md).** Profile 1's B3 read the second test set once at 07:38 on 2026-10-17. KPI 1 was 93.11% [92.39, 93.71] above the 6 dB threshold (p1.log:1036, 1067). The fused class alone, without the latched cause, read 94.78% above the same threshold (p1.log:1030). Antenna-fault recall on that set, fused → latched: 81.3% → 66.3% with the onset in the 12-cycle window (3,426 frames) and 76.4% → 64.3% after it (1,048 frames) (p1.log:1041). The latched class recall was 65.9%, F1 74.7%; airframe shadowing F1 77.9% (p1.log:1017, :1021). (The test split, read by the same B3 before the second set, gave 78.5% → 65.5% and 82.5% → 62.2%, p1.log:958.) Validation had already shown it: B2F's out-of-fold macro-F1 was 91.94% for the monitor's class against 93.32% for the fused class (p1.log:718). `select_fusion.m` chose the latch threshold but never whether to latch (D86 items 10-11). This is a deployed component without a validation on/off choice. Antenna fault and airframe shadowing take the same rule action, so the latch changed the class, not the action.

*Profile 3's first reading of the second test set.* Its B3 read the set once at 12:57 on 2026-10-17, before the stop (D88). KPI 1 was 92.20% [91.50, 92.84] above the 3 dB threshold (p3.log:1036, :1067). The fused class alone read 93.92% (p3.log:1030). Fault F1 was 72.5% and shadowing F1 75.8% (p3.log:1017, :1021). Fault recall, fused → latched: 78.6% → 62.4% with the onset in the window, 75.3% → 56.4% after it (p3.log:1041). The B3 of 16:47 on the same set (PROJECT_LOG, 17.10) is therefore profile 3's second reading of it.
**Decision.**
- *The latch is a validation choice* (d87 sec. 1). `select_fusion.m` sets `FM.latch = mf_latch > mf_fused` on the out-of-fold macro-F1, the measure that already chooses N and lambda. Every reader honours the flag: `fused_class.m`, `policy_monitor.m`, `det_frames.m`, `eval_detector.m`, `eval_unseen_snr.m`, `eval_unseen_severity.m`. The flag is part of `FM`, so `detector_id.m` changes with it and pools built with an older fusion must be re-keyed.
- *Remedies for the weak classes, tested and rejected on validation* (d87 sec. 2, nested CV over the validation sub-runs): per-class logit offsets (+0.04 [-0.21, +0.36] on profile 1), class-prior correction (0.00), balanced priors (-1.41 [-1.98, -0.86], clean false alarms 12.04%), a majority of the last m monitor classes (m = 1 chosen in every fold).
- *A third detector test set, B3m* (d87 sec. 3). The second set was read before this choice, so KPI 1 after it is read on new sub-runs. Same recipe as the second set (4 sub-runs per cell, every sub-run in the test fold); seeds 6,500,000 + run id; speeds from `rng(2035)`. Outputs `data/*_fresh2.mat` and `results/*_fresh2*`. `measure_all_kpis.m` reads KPI 1 from it when present.
**Evidence (validation, out of fold).** Profile 1: latch off, 93.32% against 91.94% latched; nested gain +1.38 [+0.60, +2.26]. Profile 3: +2.34 [+1.36, +3.28] (d87 sec. 1). After the redo (D88), B2F chose the latch off on both profiles: profile 1 fused 93.43% against latched 92.06% (p1.log:1496-1498); profile 3 93.01% against 90.66% (p3.log:1278-1280). With the latch off, profile 1's KPI 1 threshold, chosen on validation, moves from 6 dB to 0 dB; profile 3 stays at 3 dB (d87 sec. 1; p1.log:1668, p3.log:1450).
**Adoption.** Merged on v7-merge as `2cbf7e4` (2026-10-17 10:18). The note's mid-run deployment plan (d87 sec. 4: profile 1 at B4, then OOD, B4s, C1p) was not used: the align defect (D88) stopped the run first, and d87 entered the profiles with the redo of D88. B3m ran in isolated worktree copies (`uav-gcs-b3m-p1`, `uav-gcs-b3m-p3`; `docs/runs/v7_d87_b3m_20261017.sh`), so no Simulink build was shared with the running chains. Profile 1: 348.4 min, ended 22:04 (p1_b3m.log:323-325). Profile 3: 395.2 min, ended 22:51 (p3_b3m.log). The readings are in PROJECT_LOG ("Official detector readings").

## D88 — The align field, the predicted MMSE gain on known symbols, the unknown-threat threshold out of the detector identity, fair baselines
**Why (2026-10-17; note docs/d88_blockers.md).** An audit of the running chain (27 of 30 findings confirmed; docs/runs/analysis/audit1_confirmed.json and audit1_plan.md) found a blocker. `run_dataset_sweep.m` wrote both the flight's drawn `align` and the measured `align` into `D.align`, 2N values for N frames. Link feature 13 was therefore scrambled in every dataset (training, validation, every test set), while the pools, B4 and the console read the true value: a mismatch between training and deployment (d88 sec. 1). The audit also found that the predicted MMSE gain was decision-directed and collapsed under strong interference. Under a 23 dB jammer the old `mmse_gain` read +5.3 dB where the predictor on known symbols reads +18.4 dB (d88, reduced C1p check). Both feed the rule's nulling flag and the agent's state, and C1p bakes them into every pool frame, so the fix had to land before C1p.
**Decision.**
- *align* (d88 sec. 1): the draw is renamed `align_gcs`, so the measurement loop is the only writer of `align`. Guards: no MEAS name may also be a field of its own; `assert_frames.m` checks one entry per frame before every save (A5, A6, B1). `repair_align.m` rebuilt `data/dataset.mat` and `data/dataset_fresh.mat` of profiles 1 and 3 from the stored measurements; backups `*_before_align_repair.mat`.
- *The predicted MMSE gain* (d88 sec. 2): `F.mmse_pred`, from the known training and pilot symbols (optimum combining by direct matrix inversion, Winters, Salz & Gitlin 1994, eqs. 19-23). The predictor's limits are stated in d88 sec. 2.
- *The unknown-threat threshold out of the detector identity* (d88 sec. 3): `maha_thr` leaves `detector_id.m`; it is kept as `PP.maha_val` with the retention level, so changing the threshold no longer forces a pool rebuild.
- *Fair architecture comparison* (d88 sec. 4): `compare_architectures.m` tunes the random forest and the kernel SVM on validation and compares every variant fused out of fold. The earlier SVM figure (19.8%, untuned and unscaled) was an unfair baseline.
- *Print-only lines in `eval_detector.m`* (d88 sec. 5).
**Evidence.** d88's branch: full suite 170/170, `validate_phy('flat')` PASS, smoke A0-B3a passed (d88, Verification). Merged as v7-merge `725a2f1` (c2c8b8b + d87 + d88): full suite 172/172 (run notes 2026-10-17 14:26). The redo's B3a on profile 1 (p1.log:1850-1912), selection basis the validation split, fused out of fold: hybrid 93.33%, spectrogram-only CNN 93.26%, link-feature MLP 92.42%, random forest 92.03%, kernel SVM 92.19%. Per frame on validation the random forest leads (83.79% against the hybrid's 81.07%). Fused and read on the second test set, the hybrid stays first: 93.86% against 92.77% for the forest.
**Adoption.**
- The user stopped the run at 13:28 (the launcher's process trees). Profile 1 had finished through B4; its OOD was cut at the eighth of ten held-out threats (p1.log:1301). Profile 3 had finished through B3; its B3a was cut (p3.log:1080-1084).
- The redo started at 14:26 on `725a2f1` (`docs/runs/v7_d87_redo_20261017.sh`; p1.log:1302, p3.log:1084). Per profile: A0, A6, B1, B2, B2F, then the second test set's A6 and B1 from its repaired dataset, logged as B3n. Both profiles finished at 16:14 (v7_d87_redo.log). The best epoch's validation macro-F1 was 0.8131 on profile 1 and 0.8062 on profile 3 (p1.log:1459, p3.log:1244).
- A HOLD file was written at 14:55 for D89. The redo script checks it only between its steps (`hold()` before the fresh step and before the resume; `docs/runs/v7_d87_redo_20261017.sh`). Its single A0-B2F MATLAB call therefore ran on, and B2F ended at 15:53:31 on profile 1 and about 15:53 on profile 3 (p1.log:1473, :1500; p3.log:1255, :1282). The script then waited on the HOLD.
- code_commit.txt's SRC was set to a placeholder ('pending-d89-mmse-fix'), and the HOLD was removed at about 15:54. The fresh step started at 15:54:44 on profile 1 and 15:55:25 on profile 3 (p1.log:1501, p3.log:1283): the second test set's A6 and B1, logged as B3n, 18.0 min on each profile (p1.log:1576, p3.log:1358). The fresh step reads nothing D89 changes.
- The redo ended at 16:14:20 (v7_d87_redo.log). Its own resume on `725a2f1` was refused as designed at 16:14:24, because SRC named the placeholder (v7_d86_launch.log).

## D89 — The MMSE predictor's frequency hypotheses and arrival search, its cost in the cycle, the delay-line clean row
**Why (2026-10-17; note docs/d89_mmse.md).** An independent review of d87 and d88 confirmed 9 findings (docs/runs/analysis/rev88_confirmed.json). Four concern the new predictor, which C1p bakes into the pools (d89 secs. 1-4):
- dec-01: under a 30 dB tone the predictor locked onto a pilot alias, about 19.2 kHz off; profile 3 failed V13 on 9 of 11 rows;
- phy-02: a greedy arrival search;
- phy-03: the predictor's 9-20 ms were not timed inside LAT's decision cycle;
- phy-01: V13 had no clean row on the delay line.
**Decision.** The search tests the frequency hypotheses that the pilot spacing leaves ambiguous, inside the receiver's ±25 ppm window, and searches the arrival jointly (d89 sec. 1). The predictor is timed in the cycle, and its search is made cheap (d89 sec. 2). V13 gains a delay-line clean row and is read as the profiles deploy, with its verdict in the result (d89 secs. 3-4). `MODEL_TAG` becomes `v7-D89`, so no C1p checkpoint built with the d88 predictor is reused.
**Evidence.** V13 12 of 12 rows PASS for n_rx 3 (profile 1's params, params_id add18af2...) and for n_rx 4 (profile 3's, 7ecc49e9...) (d89 sec. 5). The reduced C1p check passes: a 23 dB tone at no_action reads `mmse_pred` +20.5 dB (p10 +4.2 dB), against +14.4 dB (p10 +2.0 dB) at `725a2f1` (d89 sec. 5). Full suite 174/174 (d89 sec. 5).
**Adoption.**
- v7-merge fast-forwarded to d89-mmse's `9b5c9f1`; v7-merge merged into v7-dev (`208602b`) and v7-p3 (`29510e7`).
- The run was resumed at 16:42 on `9b5c9f1` with `docs/runs/v7_d87_resume_20261017.ps1`. code_commit.txt: SRC `9b5c9f1beec4bef1ceadaa18068239c36d82c77a`, p1 v7-dev `208602b`, p3 v7-p3 `29510e7` (v7_d86/code_commit.txt).
- A first resume at 16:41:38 did not start. The launcher counted two running MATLAB sessions as pipeline stages: PIDs 43448 and 11808, the isolated B3m copies started at about 16:15 (v7_d86_launch.log 16:41:38: "MATLAB running pipeline stages (PID 43448 11808); not started").
- The launcher's `stage_pids` was therefore edited to skip command lines that name B3m. The resume at 16:42:05 then passed its code check (v7_d86_launch.log 16:42:05-16:42:07).
- Both chains restarted at B3 with A0 first (v7_d86_launch.log 16:42:13; p1.log:1577, p3.log:1359).
- Pending: after C1p, `PP.model_tag` in profile 1's pools must read `v7-D89` (run notes 2026-10-17 16:42).

## D90 — Choices on validation before C1d and C2: the sweeper's confirmation, the drop threshold on the whole monitor, the C2 reward and selection; fusion candidates behind a switch (branch d90-loop, not merged)
**Why (2026-10-17; note `docs/d90_loop.md` on branch `d90-loop`, commit `2cbe559`).** The remaining confirmed findings of the audit (docs/runs/analysis/audit1_plan.md secs. 1.3-1.6) and of the d87/d88 review (data-01, data-02, data-03, dec-04, dec-05, dec-06) act on C1d, C2, C2e, EDGE and the KPI readings, not on C1p. Every choice is made on validation; no test split or test set was read on the branch (d90_loop.md, "What was read").
**Decision** (d90_loop.md, Summary and secs. 1-6):
1. *SWEEP*: the sweeping jammer gets its own k-of-n confirmation, 2 of 6 (Norouzi 2006), adopted at C1d only if it passes there (sec. 2).
2. *DROP*: each monitor's drop threshold is chosen on the whole monitor; the median window's reference is held through an excursion (Xie et al. 2021, eqs. 2-3) (sec. 3).
   - *[Amended 31.10.2026 by D121 (approved by Adi, 31.10.2026; profile 3 only); profile 1 and P1R stay as run: where the whole-monitor rule finds no admissible threshold, the drop threshold is chosen on the drop path's own budget share of 2.0% (D121 item 4) instead of falling back to the least sensitive grid value; where it finds one, it stands.]*
3. *C2*:
   - the reward counts BER and packet loss (loop-reward-plr);
   - the selection uses held recovery (loop-select-held);
   - sel-06: the DQN is deployed only when it passed the gate and either no rule setting is within the bound or it recovers at least as much as the best admissible rule setting (`choose_deployed.m`);
   - sel-07: every run is scored with and without escalation, so the plain DQN can be deployed;
   - dec-04, sel-02, sel-08: the rule's nulling threshold, its quiet-slot branch and the unknown-threat flag's retention and override are chosen on validation and stored as `dec` in `trained_dqn.mat` (`decision_settings.m`);
   - loop-rule-dwell and loop-esc-none (sec. 4).
   - *[Amended 30.10.2026 by D113 (registered under decide-after-30min, Adi silent since 30.10.2026 19:37; profile 3 only): C2's rule settings gain the `dec.s1_gate` step, placed first among the rule steps of `choose_settings`, decided by `rank_pick` and D113 point 3's (g1)-(g2).]*
4. *KPI reporting*: harmed-episode readings and an offset set of threats that end (sec. 5).
5. *rev88 items*: data-01 (an A6 stamp: a B1 on spectrograms built before the branch stops with `prepare_data:stale`, so A6 must run again first, about 30 min), data-02, data-03, dec-05, dec-06 (sec. 6).
6. *FUSE*: candidates behind `FUSION_REV`, off by default and bit-identical when off. The user decided on 2026-10-17 that the detector stays as it is (sec. 1). (D91 later revisits the fusion on its own branch.)
- *The discount factor* (sel-07 (c)). γ is one fixed value, 0.5 (`train_dqn.m`: `GAMMAS = 0.5`, line 65 on v7-merge, line 86 on d90-loop). It was last chosen on validation in D64's grid (two discount factors, 0.5 chosen). D65 retrained at the D64 selection with γ 0.5 (γ 0.9 had exceeded the false-alarm bound in every D64 run), D66 and D67 kept it, and every run since (v5-v7, D68 on) trains with that one value. So γ is fixed since D65. On d90-loop, γ = 0 (the contextual bandit) is tried as one coordinate step on validation after the grid, at the selected monitor, drop threshold and penalty (sec. 4, sel-07). Earlier wording that has γ "chosen on validation" in v5-v7 is corrected by this entry (ROADMAP; DECISIONS D82, bracketed note).
**Cost** (d90_loop.md sec. 7). C2 grows from 72 to 108 runs (4 monitors × 3 drop thresholds × 3 penalties × 3 seeds), plus up to 9 coordinate runs, and every run is scored twice. At v6's 18-20 min per run that is about 38-42 h per profile, against 22-24 h. `decision_config.m` `C.drop_steps = [-2 0]` brings the grid back to 72 runs, about 26 h, if the timeline needs it; profile 2's C2 is on the campaign's critical path. C1d grows from under 1 min to minutes.
**Evidence and status.**
- The full `test_core` passed on `d91-speed` `1249ef3`, which contains d90: V7TESTS 196/196; the baseline `9b5c9f1` 174/174 (uav-gcs-logs/v7_d91_tests2_summary.log, 2026-10-18 19:49).
- d90's own smoke run and an independent review of the d90 diff are not recorded. d90_loop.md secs. 8 and 9 still hold placeholders.
- Pending: the d90 smoke and review.
**Adoption (not done).** d90-loop is not merged into v7-merge or into any profile. Merge rules (docs/runs/analysis/overnight_audit.txt:27-29, :81-82, :110; run notes 2026-10-18 00:10):
- Nothing of d90 changes a C1p, C1c, SURV or SURV3 value. Those workers still read the code live: `decision_config.m`, which d90 changes, is called by `build_policy_pools.m` (C1p), `build_check_pools.m` (C1c) and `experiment_survivability_options.m` (SURV3), and the C1p cell keys hold no code version.
- Merge into profile 1 only after its C1c is done (the SURV/SURV3 window), and before C1d.
- `data/fusion.mat` stays fixed from C1p to C1c.
- Merge into v7-p3 only while no profile 3 MATLAB runs.
- After the merge, code_commit.txt SRC must name the merged commit, or a later resume refuses.
- A merge into v7-dev also triggers launcher defect (b) of D91 part 4: the running launcher's SRC then refuses profile 2 and runs LAT early on a busy machine. The d90 merge into profile 1 therefore waits until the takeover (D91 part 4) runs the campaign.
- d90_loop.md sec. 10 gives the whole of C1p-SURV3 as the safe window and says no fourth test set is needed. Both statements are superseded: the window by the overnight audit (above), the test set by D91.
**Sources.** As listed in d90_loop.md, "Sources". Hastie, Tibshirani & Friedman (ESL, sec. 7.10, the one-standard-error rule) is cited only on the `FUSION_REV` path and is not in the library (LITERATURE.md).

## D91 — Fault and shadowing below 85% on the third test set: a branch-evidence fusion revision on an enlarged fit set, a fourth test set read once, value-neutral speedups for profiles 3 and 2, a takeover of the launcher, and the C1p slowdown
**Why (2026-10-17/18).**
- *The official KPI 1* (B3m, read once). Antenna fault and airframe shadowing are below the user's 85% bar on both profiles: profile 1 fault F1 83.1%, shadowing 82.7% (p1_b3m.log:236, 240); profile 3 83.6% and 82.0% (p3_b3m.log:236, 240). The earlier second-set readings after the fixes (B3 at 16:47 on 2026-10-17, diagnostic) were higher: profile 1 fault 85.4%, shadowing 86.7% (p1.log:1734, :1738); profile 3 84.9% and 83.8% (p3.log:1516, :1520).
- *The weak-branch analysis* (docs/runs/analysis/weak_branch_recommendation.md; it read validation, the already-read third test set and the code):
  - No label bug: the fault label and attenuation switch on from the same onset; the shadowing label and loss come from the same `shadow_frame` call.
  - Most misses are a lost branch read as 'none'.
  - Most 5 dB fault misses at K < 10 dB are limited by information. The static imbalance between antennas has an SD of 2.9 dB (body loss, Badi 2019, Table 1, plus the ±2 dB calibration error). A window holds at most 12 independent samples against the 36 that Lee 1985 needs for ±1 dB (eq. 31).
  - The recoverable part: shallow faults at K ≥ 10 dB (on the third set the CNN recalls 10.6-48.1% against 88.1-99.5% for a gain-only ideal), hover, and frames after the onset has left the 12-cycle window.
  - True out-of-fold validation: fault 83.4%, shadowing 85.2%. The 84.7 / 83.2 quoted earlier as validation was the first test split.
- *The user's decision* (2026-10-18 00:35, option A): fit a fusion-only revision on validation plus the test sets already read, and read KPI 1 once on a new fourth test set.
**Decision, in five parts.**
1. *A fourth detector test set* (branch `d91-set4`, `0e410cf`; note `docs/d91_set4.md`).
   - Stage B3q builds it and reads nothing. Seeds 6,000,000 + run id; speeds from `rng(2037)`; disjoint from every stream (`test_seed_streams_disjoint`, `test_fourth_test_set`).
   - Stage B3r reads it once. Its guards in `eval_detector.m` (d91_set4.md, "What the branch adds"), all checked before the start marker is written and before any data is loaded:
     - the switch `code/evaluation/kpi1_set.m` must say 'fresh3' (it says 'fresh2' until adoption);
     - `data/splits_fresh3.mat` must exist, and no earlier fresh3 reading or start marker may exist (`fresh3_read`, `fresh3_started`);
     - the input checks: `data/splits.mat`, `data/trained_detector.mat`, `data/fusion.mat` and `params.mat` exist (`fresh3_input`), and the fusion holds `val_latch_oof` (`fresh3_fusion`);
     - `data/fusion.mat` must be the adopted revision (`fresh3_fusion_rev`) and not older than the detector's `trained_at` (`fresh3_fusion_old`);
     - the rehearsal: the third set's reading `results/eval_detector_metrics_fresh2.mat` exists and is not older than `data/fusion.mat` or `trained_at`, so the same path has just run to its end on an already-read set (`fresh3_rehearsal`);
     - the committed record: no `docs/d91_fresh3_read_*.txt` (aborted ones aside) holds this `params.mat`'s `params_id` (`fresh3_recorded`);
     - planned in the deploy commit (d91_fuse.md sec. 7 B3), not yet in the code: `data/fusion.mat` must record a `deployed_pick` equal to `fusion_pick.m`'s (`fresh3_pick`).
   - Built in isolated copies at `31ab0de`: profile 1 done 12:15 (420.0 min), profile 3 done 19:36 (439.5 min) (p1_set4.log:215, p3_set4.log:215).
   - Outputs are in `uav-gcs-logs/set4_out/p1` and `p3` with `sha256_fresh3.txt`. They are unread and stay unread until the go/no-go.
   - *[Amended 30.10.2026 by D110 (registered under decide-after-30min, Adi silent since 30.10.2026 19:37): in profile 3 the adopted revision that B3r's `fresh3_fusion_rev` and `fresh3_pick` require is D110's pick (`FM.head`, the GCC columns and/or `opt.cfar`), or the d91 pick as base B when D110 adopts nothing or its B2F re-check fails. In the same single pass B3r also applies `base_fm` (d91 alone) and prints it as 'd91 alone, descriptive, never the KPI 1 value'. No guard is weakened.]*
   - *[Amended 30.10.2026 by D111 (registered under decide-after-30min, Adi silent since 30.10.2026 19:37): profile 3's fourth set gets one more reader, D111's OOD `read` phase. It runs only if D111 adopted on validation, strictly after B3r's committed record, with its own once-marker. It prints AUROC and FPR95 per held-out threat only; the LOTO detectors' per-class lines and Algorithm 2's known-class check (`eval_ood_detection.m:38-45`) are not computed on set 4. B3r's guards are not weakened.]*
2. *The branch-evidence fusion revision* (branch `d91-fuse`, `2ea990a`; note `docs/d91_fuse.md`).
   - Four column groups computed from the stored per-antenna gains only, behind `FUSION_REV`:
     - iso, the largest step over the antennas (Xie 2021, Sec. III-I);
     - held, a per-branch reference held through a drop, with a one-sided window-limited CUSUM (Lee 1985, Sec. IV; Xie 2021, eqs. 2-3; Xie 2022);
     - glr, a one-sided exponential GLR of a branch against the pooled others, with both means estimated in the window; adapted from Xie 2021, eq. 20 and Xie, Moustakides & Xie 2022, eq. 9, which assume a known pre-change density;
     - post, K and spread after the split (Aoki & Honda 2025, eq. 2).
   - The fit set is enlarged (`FUSION_FIT = 'enlarged'`): validation, the first test split and the second and third test sets, 5 folds by sub-run. N and lambda stay chosen on validation.
   - *Pre-registration* (d91_fuse.md sec. 4).
     - The adoption rule was coded in `code/detection/fusion_adopt.m`, last written 05:15:41 on 2026-10-18.
     - The measurement started 05:48:26. It was committed as `ad3cde8` at 05:48:34.
     - A code check on every 20th sub-run reached a verdict at 05:47:47. That was before the commit, but after the rule and the script were last written.
     - The rule's file and the script are byte-identical to what ran (sha256 in d91_fuse.md sec. 4). The check's numbers are not used.
   - *The rule.* Against the deployed design refitted on the same folds, with paired sub-run bootstrap intervals (1,000 resamples), a variant is adopted only if all of these hold: the fault F1 gain's lower bound is above 0; the macro-F1 gain's lower bounds, over all frames and above the KPI 1 threshold, are above 0; the shadowing F1 upper bound is at least 0; at K ≤ 0 dB, the fault and shadowing F1 upper bounds are at least 0; the clean-frame false alarms' lower bounds are at most 0. The verdict also requires the nested reading to pass.
3. *Value-neutral speedups for profiles 3 and 2* (branch `d91-speed`, `0069d03`; merge candidate `d91-speed-on-v7-p3`, `686fd11`, built onto v7-p3 `29510e7`; note `docs/d91_speed.md`).
   - FEC twins share one simulation: only the ground truth and the CRC are computed again, by `closed_loop_truth.m` and `pool_configs.m`.
   - The longest job goes first, one job per free worker, by `parfeval` FIFO (`pool_lpt.m`, `pool_job.m`) in all five pool stages.
   - Pool values and checkpoint keys stay bit-identical. Nothing changes for profile 1's live run.
4. *A takeover of the d86 launcher* (branch `d91-takeover`, `7d4d0b6`; `ops/v7_d91_takeover.md`, `ops/v7_d91_takeover.sh`).
   - The overnight audit (docs/runs/analysis/overnight_audit.txt) found these launcher defects:
     - (a) the launcher counts profile 3 as ended (its subshell died at 22:02 on 2026-10-17), so profile 2's C1p would not wait for profile 3's SURV3;
     - (b) after any code merge into v7-dev, the in-memory SRC refuses profile 2 and runs LAT early on a busy machine;
     - (c) a profile 1 crash writes 'chain ended', and the fix script then starts profile 3's C1p at once;
     - (d) the launcher's stage list has no B3m, so profile 2's KPI 1 would come from its second set;
     - (e) the zombie MATLAB 32824 is counted as a live session;
     - (f) the campaign's end against the Windows Update pause.
   - The takeover keeps profile 1's chain and reads every dependency from log markers, never from process ids.
5. *The C1p slowdown* (docs/runs/analysis/c1p_rate.txt, c1p_slow_model.py, c1p_slow_timeline.py; run notes 2026-10-18 12:30 and 13:10).
   - Profile 1's C1p ran 86 builds in its first 9 h (9.7 per hour; c1p_rate.txt, 12:20). The first reading's C1p ran about 20 per hour: 740 'Building model' lines in 37 h (`uav-gcs-logs/full_v7p1_first_reading_20261016_110932.log`). At 9.7 per hour the 12:30 estimate was about 200 h for C1p.
   - Not a bug. The cost per simulation is ×1.7 on emitter cells and ×1.18 on clean ones. The idle-time RSSI model (D85) explains about one third of the emitter increase; 0.5-0.7 s per simulation is unexplained (a profile of the emitter path is wanted).
   - The 38-frame roll-in flights add ×1.115, and parfor's default chunking adds more.
   - *The basis of the 155-175 h projection.* `c1p_slow_model.py` counts the simulations of the 93 cells (980,784, ×1.115 for the 38-frame flights) at a per-simulation cost recalibrated from the workers' build timestamps (1.75 s, plus 1.08-1.40 s per emitter), and schedules the cells over 6 workers in their order: 144-161 h. The 155-175 h of the 13:10 notes add parfor's default chunking to that. It is a projection, not a measurement.
   - *Measured state at writing* (2026-10-18 21:33): 170 builds in 18.2 h since 03:20 (about 9.3 per hour, p1.log from :2384), and 5 of 93 cells on disk, the last at 18:44 (`uav-gcs-v7/data/c1p_cells`).
**Evidence (fit-set numbers, out of fold or nested; not test readings).**
- *Verdict* (d91_fuse.md sec. 5; docs/runs/analysis/d91_fuse_m_p1_log.txt:132-134, d91_fuse_m_p3_log.txt:132-134). The rule adopts 'branch evidence' on both profiles, and all 5 outer folds pick it. Profile 1 finished 11:02, profile 3 16:24 on 2026-10-18.

  | Nested gain against the base, points [95%] | Profile 1 | Profile 3 |
  |---|---|---|
  | Fault F1 | +5.14 [+4.16, +6.20] | +5.19 [+4.23, +6.25] |
  | Macro-F1, all frames | +0.64 [+0.52, +0.76] | +0.61 [+0.49, +0.74] |
  | Clean frames read as a threat | -0.29 [-0.43, -0.15] | -0.32 [-0.47, -0.17] |
  | Shadowing F1 | +0.39 [-0.02, +0.80] | +0.30 [-0.13, +0.74] |

- *Profile 1 fault recall, base → branch evidence*, out of fold on the enlarged fit set (d91_fuse_m_p1_log.txt:95-101):

  | Stratum | Base | Branch evidence |
  |---|---|---|
  | 5 dB | 20.9% | 52.0% |
  | 8.7 dB | 56.6% | 79.8% |
  | K ≥ 10 dB | 86.1% | 96.3% |
  | Onset age ≥ 12 cycles | 75.0% | 90.7% |
  | Hover | 77.9% | 87.5% |

  The held reference is the main lever.
- *M3, the shadowing bound with cross-antenna coherence* (d91_fuse.md sec. 5; d91_fuse_m_p1_log.txt:71-77, d91_fuse_m_p3_log.txt:71-77). Bagged trees (100), trained out of fold on the enlarged fit set, tell shadowing frames from clean frames only. They are set at the fusion's clean budget (4.14% on profile 1, 4.31% on profile 3). It is therefore a bound, not a deployable class decision.

  | Coherence trees against the fusion's shadowing recall, points [95%] | Profile 1 | Profile 3 |
  |---|---|---|
  | All shadowing frames (10,845) | +4.7 [+3.9, +5.6] | +7.0 [+6.1, +7.9] |
  | Onset age 0-2 cycles (2,108) | +10.8 [+8.9, +12.8] | +15.7 [+13.4, +18.1] |
  | K < 0 dB (1,989) | +2.7 [-0.3, +5.5] | +7.7 [+4.6, +10.7] |

  d91_fuse.md sec. 5 states the criterion: a shadowing change is justified only if the coherence trees beat the fusion with an interval above 0. Overall and at onset age 0-2 it holds on both profiles. The coherence columns are a candidate for shadowing; no design or deployment exists yet.
- *phi.* The hover factor phi = (1-rho)/(1+rho) in the GLR has no source in the library. Its comparison with phi = 1 was declared before it ran (d91_fuse.md sec. 8, commit `5abf87d`). The profile 1 job started at 20:18 on 2026-10-18 (d91q/fuse/logs/queue.log). Pending: the phi verdict.
- *Wrap check* (d91_fuse.md sec. 10). Pool episodes jump from frame 20 back to frame 1 inside their 30 cycles. The check ran on the d90 smoke pools only, as a mechanism check; its rates are not results.
- *Speed proofs* (d91_speed.md sec. 4).
  - On `70f69f4` (the D91CODE of the proofs; the branch tip is now `0069d03`): A_static, P_parfor and P_order2 PASS; B_old_vs_new, C_order and C_vs_B PASS with the detector applied ("detector 1") (uav-gcs-logs/v7_d91_speed_summary.log).
  - On the 4-antenna merge candidate `686fd11`: B_old_vs_new_nrx4, C_order and C_vs_B_nrx4 PASS with the detector applied (v7_d91_tests4_pnew.log and _porder.log; the old side in _pold.log). The three jobs ended rc=0 by 20:18.
  - The first full-suite runs failed for an environment reason, a Stateflow build path over 260 characters in the long scratch TEMP; they are voided. tests3 was voided too.
  - tests2 in a short path: new `1249ef3` 196/196, old `9b5c9f1` 174/174, focused 8/8 (v7_d91_tests2_summary.log).
  - tests4, the full suite on the merge candidate: the merge job (full `test_core` on `686fd11`) started at 21:09:09 (v7_d91_tests4_queue.log). Pending: the tests4 VERDICT.
  - Estimated gains (the analysis's cost model, not measurements): profile 3's C1p about 169 h → 105-115 h, profile 2's about 135 h → 85-95 h (d91_speed.md sec. 3).
- *Takeover* (run notes 2026-10-18 19:40). Test harness 287/287 at `7d4d0b6`. The round-3 review is FIX-NEEDED: `main()` must parse the whole script before it runs, and the main loop needs a watchdog. A round-4 fix is running.
- *Timeline after the slowdown* (run notes 2026-10-18 13:10):

  | Milestone | Projected |
  |---|---|
  | Profile 1 C1p (155-175 h) ends | 2026-10-24 14:00 to 10-25 10:00 |
  | Profile 1 ends | about 27-28.10 |
  | Profile 3 ends | about 4-6.11 |
