  These dates pass the Windows Update pause's end (2026-11-05 13:38 UTC); the takeover's resume handles reboots. The speedups could save about 3 days on profiles 3 and 2. The earlier projection (profile 1 about 23.10, everything about 2.11) is superseded.

  The timeline script (`c1p_slow_timeline.py`) allows 22-52 h for C1d-RPT per profile. The lower value is the top of D86's 19-22 h, which counted C2 at 12 h; with the 72-run grid C1d-RPT is about 29-34 h (the bracket in the cost note after D74). The upper value covers d90's grid (45-52 h). So the later dates allow for d90's C2, and the earlier dates are short even for the 72-run grid.
**Adoption (not done; the go/no-go with the user is planned for 2026-10-21 evening).** Deployment follows d91_fuse.md sec. 7, the deploy checklist:
- `fusion_pick.m` ties B2F to the measured pick, with the latch off as measured.
- Then the B2F rerun on the enlarged fit set, `kpi1_set.m` = 'fresh3', the copy of `set4_out` into the profile with its hash check, and B3r once.
- Profile 3 before its C1p.
- Profile 1 after its chain, with `rekey_pools` (the new columns read only the stored gains, so no C1p rebuild).
- B3, B3n and B3m become in-sample under an adopted revision; KPI 1 is then read only on the fourth set.
- Profile 2 has no enlarged fit set at its B2F and needs its own fusion decision (d91_fuse.md sec. 7 F, proposed). Pending: the user's approval of profile 2's fusion decision.
- If the user decides no-go, `kpi1_set.m` stays 'fresh2' and the fourth set stays unread.
- d91-speed goes into v7-p3 by a cherry-pick, not a merge, because the branch also carries d90: `git cherry-pick 2cbe559..d91-speed` while profile 3 waits for profile 1's SURV3, under the merge rule of d91_speed.md sec. 7 (7.4-7.5). The tested candidate is `d91-speed-on-v7-p3` `686fd11` (2cbe559..74ca87c on `29510e7`). Profile 2 takes the same cherry-pick before its C1p, on a candidate built for v7-p2 (7.6).
- After a go, d91_fuse.md sec. 7 A merges d91-set4, d91-fuse and d91-speed into a `d91-deploy` branch, tests that branch merged onto the v7-p3 tip (7 C), and merges it into v7-p3 (7 D2). D2 covers a v7-p3 that already holds the cherry-pick. With a no-go, sec. 7 G says nothing is merged. Whether the speedups then go in alone by the cherry-pick is not settled in the notes; it is for the go/no-go.
- The user launches the takeover before profile 1's chain ends; the steps are in `ops/v7_d91_takeover.md`, "Procedure".
**Stated, not backed by a source** (d91_fuse.md sec. 1, "What the sources support"):
- phi's AR(1) form (pending its comparison);
- alpha = 1.87%, profile 1's clean lost-branch rate on validation, also used for profile 3 (it only decides whether an excursion's cycles join the held reference);
- b = |log alpha| applied to a frame share: Xie 2021 (Sec. II-D1) gives it for a false-alarm rate alpha = 1/ARL, so this is an analogy, not the source's quantity;
- the cap W ≤ 2b on the CUSUM;
- the exponential (Rayleigh) power as the likelihood model, an assumption, not a sourced worst case;
- the Clarke coherence-time approximation used in the wrap analysis.

**Open:**
- KPI 2 is below its 0.8 target on both profiles (nested 0.769 and 0.763); the untried options are in PROJECT_LOG, weak-class status.
- Path loss and WLAN stay below 90% on the third set (87.8% and 89.0% on profile 1).
- Shadowing: the adopted revision gains only +0.39 [-0.02, +0.80] (profile 1) and +0.30 [-0.13, +0.74] (profile 3) nested. M3 (above) meets d91_fuse.md sec. 5's criterion for a shadowing change with the coherence columns. That is a candidate, not yet designed or deployed.

## D92 — The lecturer's revised proposal of 2026-10-20 is the official scope; profile 2, the relay path and the altitude axis are dropped; new code for the lecturer's KPIs
**Why (2026-10-20/21, with the user).**
- The lecturer sent a revised proposal on 2026-10-20. The user decided it is the official one, unchanged.
- *Title:* "פיתוח מערכת תקשורת אדפטיבית מבוססת בינה מלאכותית לזיהוי ולהתמודדות עם שיבושי תקשורת (Jamming) בין רחפן לתחנת קרקע" / "Development of an AI-Assisted Adaptive UAV-to-Ground Station Communication System for Jamming Detection and Mitigation".
- *Deliverables:*
  - the simulation: QPSK, Rician, clean link plus narrowband and wideband jamming at several JSR levels;
  - a labelled spectrogram dataset and a CNN (clean / narrowband / wideband);
  - rule-based recovery (channel switch, rate adaptation) from the class and the link metrics;
  - the closed-loop integration;
  - a comparison of three configurations: static link; conventional adaptive on link metrics only; CNN-based adaptive;
  - the results: graphs, confusion matrix, tables.
- *KPIs:*
  - BER model validated against theory;
  - macro-F1 ≥ 85% on an independent test set;
  - false alarms ≤ 5% in clean states;
  - recall ≥ 85% per jamming type;
  - PER reduction ≥ 30% against the static link in recoverable scenarios;
  - recovery time, from detection until the PER stays under a threshold for a fixed window; the onset-to-detection time is measured separately;
  - goodput;
  - the CNN's contribution against the conventional mechanism, as an analysis metric and not a promise.
- *Gantt:* unchanged, except "literature with a focus on Jamming" and "DQN agent - optional".
- *Mapping used in the code:* narrowband = `tone_jamming`, wideband = `jamming` (barrage). The existing policies map: static = `none` / `no_action`, conventional = `blind_esc`, CNN-based = `rule_esc`.
- *Status when decided (2026-10-21 morning):* profile 1's C1p at 44 of 93 cells; profile 3 waits before C1p (`p3_c1p.hold`) for its deployment; the processor boost is off since 2026-10-20 12:40, so stages run up to about 35% slower.
**Decision, in six parts.**
1. *The lecturer's proposal is the scope.* The deliverables and KPIs above are what the project is measured on. The ten threats, three and four UAV antennas, the tracked GCS antenna, the real receiver, the hybrid network with link features, unknown-threat detection, the 36 configurations, the DQN, the GUI, the survivability map, EDGE, C2g, C2u and OHP / GCSA on profile 1 stay as extensions without commitment. Nothing is rerun to remove any of them.
2. *Profile 2 (two antennas) is dropped* (supersedes the profile 2 of D70). `v7_d86/p2.abandon` was written 2026-10-21 06:49: the takeover ends the profile 2 track and the final phase skips it.
3. *Not reported:* the relay path (the SURV3 what-if) and the altitude axis of the envelope.
4. *OHP and GCSA are skipped on profile 3* by a new `profile.json` field, `skip_stages`, set at profile 3's deployment. Profile 1 runs them.
5. *Python is dropped from the tools.*
6. *New code for the lecturer's KPIs.* Worktree `C:\Users\Adi Suliman\uav-gcs-lectkpi`, branch `lect-kpi` off `v7-deploy-d91` (commits `27803c3`, `2562605`, `c8ca607`, `0be0662`):
   - `per_metrics.m`, `per_reduction.m`, `lect_kpis.m`: PER reduction against the static link with a paired geometry-bootstrap interval; recovery time from detection (`t_rec_det`) and onset-to-detection time (`t_conf`); goodput in bit/s at 50 kb/s nominal;
   - `three_state_view.m`: 3-state recall and macro-F1 from the saved confusion matrix, with no new test reading;
   - `profile_skip_stages.m` and `run_stage.m`: the `skip_stages` field.
**Adoption (not done).** The tests are written and have not run (the gate is busy). The branch is to be tested, reviewed and merged into `v7-deploy-d91` before the Plan A stop (end of profile 1's SURV3, about 2026-10-25/26), so that profile 1's C2e and KPI report read the new KPIs. Plan A was approved on 2026-10-21: the fusion revision 'branch evidence, phi = 1', the B3-B4s rerun, the rehearsal, one reading of the fourth test set, the re-key, and the resume from C1d.
**Results audit of 2026-10-21.**
- Confirmed from the primary files: KPI 1 93.58 / 94.19, KPI 2 0.769 / 0.763, KPI 3 7/7, the dataset and the fusion revision.
- 58 wording issues about what a number measures were found in the interim-report draft. Example: the 2.67 / 3.85 ms latency is the detector forward pass plus the score.
- The per-class "Acc" in `eval_detector` is recall.
**Projections (estimates, not measurements).** Profile 1's decision-layer results early November; profile 3 mid November; the final LAT and KPI after that.

**Open:**
- `lect-kpi` is untested and unmerged.
- The 58 wording issues of the interim-report draft are not yet fixed.

## D93 — No Plan A stop for profile 1: it runs its own code to the end; the lecturer's KPIs for profile 1 from isolated runs; profile 3 deploys after profile 1
**Revised by D94 (2026-10-23, about 09:00).** Profile 1 is stopped after all, as a software-only stop. D94 supersedes parts 1-4 and the runbook clause of part 6. Part 5 stands, with a shorter queue on `-q3`.
**Why (2026-10-23 03:10, the user).** "No reason to stop now"; the user will say if a stop is needed. Profile 1's chain was in C1p (82 of 93 cells at 03:07), with no cut file, no `final.hold` and no alert.
**Decision.**
1. *Profile 1 is not stopped.* Plan A (D91, D92: the cut after SURV3, the reboot, the fusion swap, the B3-B4s rerun, the rehearsal, B3r, the re-key and the resume from C1d) is cancelled for now. `p1_cut_after.SURV3` is never written. Profile 1's chain runs its current code to RPT: `uav-gcs-v7`, `v7-dev` `208602b` (code tree `9b5c9f1`), base fusion, latch off, through C1p, C1c, SURV, SURV3, C1d, C2, C2e, C2g, C2u, GAL, OHP, GCSA, EDGE, KPI, DASH, WEAK and RPT.
2. *The fusion revision 'branch evidence, phi = 1' is validated but not deployed in profile 1.* Its verdict (ADOPT on both profiles by the pre-registered rule, D91) stands and is reported as a validated revision. Profile 1's decision-layer results are those of the base fusion. Profile 3 deploys the revision.
3. *The lecturer's KPIs for profile 1 come from isolated runs; profile 1's tree is only read.*
   - PER against the static link (per scenario: narrowband `tone_jamming`, wideband `jamming`), recovery time from detection, goodput, the CNN's contribution, the system false alarms and the three link states: an isolated copy of profile 1's C2e after it finishes (`docs/c2e_lect_protocol.md` on `lect-kpi`, fixed 2026-10-23 before profile 1's C2e; code `lect-p1` `8118281` = `208602b` plus the readers; inputs copied read-only with SHA-256; an exact consistency gate against profile 1's own C2e results, with no tolerance; a reading only after the gate passes; `tools/c2e_lect_isolated.sh`).
   - Three-state macro-F1, recall per jamming type and the detector's false alarms: B3c in a checkout of its own on profile 1's inputs, reading the fourth test set once, with no B3r before it, under the committed declaration `docs/core_cnn_no_b3r.txt` (`e84a5fd`, params_id `add18af2b7532a0bd2f7fbc78f06e526`). The reading makes no choice for the hybrid.
   - BER validation: profile 1's A4v (7/7).
   - CORE_BW: after profile 1's chain, in its own worktree, on copies of profile 1's deployed inputs.
   - Profile 1's own KPI stage (code `208602b`) reports none of these; its eight proposal KPIs stay extensions.
4. *No B3r of the hybrid on profile 1.* Profile 1's fourth test set is read only by B3c. KPI 1 for profile 1 stays the third-set reading (B3m).
5. *Profile 3 waits until profile 1's chain has ended* (`p3_c1p.hold` stays). Reasons: without a reboot the zombies 32824 and 8908 hold about 11 GB of commit and free commit stays at 22-23 GB during profile 1's parallel stages; profile 3's rerun needs 65 GB and its C1p-SURV3 block runs six workers; `uav-gcs-v7-p3` is the only free slot for the lecturer-KPI hand-runs while profile 1 runs. Its deployment then takes the candidate `-q3` (`v7-deploy-d91` with `d91-edgefix` and `lect-kpi`, `skip_stages` OHP and GCSA inside the tested tree), the second deploy queue's six jobs on it (link_env changed, so the smoke runs again), and the B-stage rerun, B3r and the release of the hold as in `d91_deploy.md` D.
6. The hand-runs keep the gate (28 GB) and the wrapper. `STOP_CHECKLIST` is kept for a future stop only; the order of the steps is the runbook `RUNBOOK_NOSTOP.md` (session scratchpad `interim_report/core/`, not in this repo) (triggers T1-T7).
**Consequences.** Profile 1 ends about 2026-10-28/29 (estimate). Profile 3 ends about 2026-11-08/11 (estimate), past the Windows Update pause (2026-11-05). The interim report (2026-11-01) takes the gallery figures, B3c's reading and the C2e-lect lines if they exist by about 2026-10-29; it needs no final results.
**Open.**
- CORE_BW's code base: `lect-kpi` (its decision layer is d90's monitor, applied with profile 1's settings, stated in a committed declaration) or a port onto `lect-p1`.
- `lect-kpi` and `lect-p1` are not pushed.
- A reboot after profile 1's end (zombies, BIOS items) is the user's choice.

## D94 — Software-only Plan A stop of profile 1 (revises D93): cut after SURV3, the deploy before C1d, no reboot; profile 3 after profile 1
**Why (2026-10-23, about 09:00, the user).** If a stop is needed for fixes and improvements, it is to be done. It was approved as a **software-only** stop: no reboot, no BIOS, fan or processor change, and nothing for the user to do.
- The cut file `v7_d86/p1_cut_after.SURV3` was written 2026-10-23 08:56. The takeover accepted it with no alert.
- Profile 1 was in C1p, at 87 of 93 cells on 2026-10-23 morning.

**Decision.**
1. *The cut.* The takeover ends profile 1's chain win64 MATLAB once the current part (`PART 20261017_164205`) shows `##### STAGE SURV3 done`. C1p, C1c, SURV and SURV3 finish first. The chain resumes from C1d through the takeover (`p1_resume.go`).
2. *What profile 1 takes before C1d.* The source is one merged tip: `v7-deploy-d91` `0f437f3`, then `d91-edgefix` `8fdb09e` (tree `ce4cd2ec`), then `lect-kpi` `5b4b970` (tree `ac8d0d95`). Profile 1's candidate on `v7-dev` `208602b` has tree `579f4baa`. It carries:
   - *D90's audit fixes:*
     - the reward with packet loss;
     - the held-recovery selection;
     - the DQN deployment rule;
     - the rule thresholds and the unknown flag, set on validation;
     - the sweeper's confirmation;
     - the drop threshold on the whole monitor (C1d);
     - the harmed/offset readings.

     C2 grows to 108 runs (`C.drop_steps = [-2 0 2]`).
   - *D91's fusion revision* 'branch evidence, phi = 1' (B2F with the enlarged fit). After it come the B-stage rerun on the deployed fusion (B3, B3a, B4, B4s; in-sample labels), the rehearsal, one B3r reading of the fourth test set, and `rekey_pools`.
   - *`d91-edgefix`:* `edge_map` names the leave-one-out agents' file and C2u, the smoke's `dep_*` steps run in chain order, and `test_rekey_unknown_agents` is added.
   - *`lect-kpi`:*
     - `rule_core` and `blind_core` in C2e;
     - `lect_kpis` and the lecturer's lines in KPI;
     - B3c, the three-state CNN;
     - the CORE_BW code;
     - `skip_stages`.
   - *B3c* reads the fourth test set once, after B3r, in profile 1's own tree. The hybrid's comparison is then part of the reading.
3. *Order and gates.*
   - Before the code enters profile 1, the merged suite (258 tests), d91-edgefix's focused tests, the proofs at n_rx 3 and the smoke must all pass. The smoke is required because `link_env.m` changed (`d91_deploy.md` 3.7).
   - The fast-forward of `v7-dev` is the point of no return.
   - Every hand-run goes through the wrapper and the gate. The gate stays at 28 GB, and the floors stay at 35, 45 and 65 GB.
   - Test sets are read once.
4. *No reboot.* The zombie MATLAB processes 32824 and 8908 keep about 11 GB of commit.
   - Expected free commit after the cut is about 64.6 GB. The wrapper reads its integer part, 64, which is below the 65 GB floor of B2F, the B stages, the rehearsal, B3r and B3c.
   - If the measurement confirms this, the user chooses: a reboot, a lower floor for these hand-runs, or plan B. The agent lowers no floor.
5. *Plan B,* until the fast-forward: nothing changes in `uav-gcs-v7`, and profile 1 resumes from C1d on `208602b`. D93's isolated runs then apply again.
6. *Profile 3 after profile 1.* D93 part 5 stands: `p3_c1p.hold` stays, and profile 3 deploys `-q3` (tree `c1081d1f`) after profile 1's chain has ended. Its queue runs `pold pnew porder p3core` on `-q3`. `core` and the smoke come from profile 1's run on the same tip. Profile 2 stays dropped (D92).

**Supersedes.** D94 supersedes D93 parts 1-4 (no stop, the base fusion in profile 1, the isolated runs, no B3r on profile 1) and the runbook clause of part 6.
- The isolated runs (C2e-lect, the isolated B3c) are plan B only.
- The declaration `docs/core_cnn_no_b3r.txt` (`e84a5fd`) no longer describes profile 1 once B3r has read. The code ignores it when B3r's two result files exist, and it stays in the tree.
- The steps are in `STOP_SOFT.md` (session scratchpad `interim_report/core/`, not in this repo). It replaces `RUNBOOK_NOSTOP.md` as the order of the steps.

**Consequences** (estimates, not measurements):
- the cut about 2026-10-24/25;
- the window about 18-39 h if 65 GB is free;
- the resume about 2026-10-25/26;
- C2 38-42 h;
- C2e with the lecturer's lines about 2026-10-28/29;
- KPI and RPT about 2026-10-30/31;
- profile 3 after that, about 2026-11-10/13.

The interim report (2026-11-01) needs no final results.

## D95 — The decision layer's clean reference comes from the 300 validation clean flights per Eb/N0, not from C1p's 8 train flights; profile 1 at the stop, profile 3 inside C1c
**Why (2026-10-24; three independent reviews of the finding, details in `interim_report/core/wf_cleanref.json`, session scratchpad, not in this repo).**
- C1p (`build_policy_pools.m`) builds the clean-link table from the clean cell's own train flights only: `NGEO(1) = 8` simple random flights per Eb/N0 (`:79`; `LHS(1) = false`, `:85`), 20 frames each, a seed of its own per Eb/N0 (`pool_seed.m`). `own` keeps those flights (`:379`) and `:394-398` writes `clean_ref` (`ber`, `fer`, `ber_est`, `plr`, `plr_fec`); `PP.clean` and `PP.clean_fer` are the same means.
- *Readers.* `policy_monitor.m` reads `ber_est` and `plr_fec` through `clean_ber_ref` (the degradation alarm, floored at `C.deg_floor`). `link_env.m` 'mean' reads `PP.clean` (floored at `C.ber_floor`) and `PP.clean_fer` (the reward, `healthy`, `restored`, `restored_plr`, the goodput normalisation). The table therefore reaches the C2 reward and selection, C2e (KPI 4 through restoration, KPI 6 through the monitor), C2g, C2u, EDGE's monitor (its restoration is 'flight'), CORE_BW, and, on the D94 tree, C1d's whole-monitor false-alarm bound (the monitor's `degraded` alarm; C1d on `208602b` reads none). `ber`, `fer` and `plr` of `clean_ref` are only printed.
- *Evidence.* Profile 1's C1p printed the clean BER at 0, 3, 6, 9, 12 and 15 dB as 7.1e-2, 1.9e-2, 1.0e-2, 2.4e-5, 1.2e-5 and 0 (p1.log:7904). The C1c check pools (36 LHS flights) printed 3.0e-2, 9.9e-3, 2.2e-3, 2.0e-3, 5.9e-4 and 1.1e-4 at 1.5, 4.5, 7.5, 9, 10.5 and 13.5 dB (p1.log:11440). C1p falls from 1.0e-2 at 6 dB to 2.4e-5 at 9 dB; the check pools are nearly flat from 7.5 to 9 dB. A heavy-tailed per-flight BER (bank angle up to 57.3° in the train split, attitude and polarisation loss, K −5 to 20 dB per flight) sampled by 8 flights gives that pattern. The tail is inferred from the logs: no per-flight value has been read yet.
- *What the reviews corrected in the finding.*
  - The packet-loss columns of the two log lines are different fields (C1p prints `clean_ref.plr`, the CRC loss; C1c prints `CK.clean_fer`, the true frame error): only the BER is like for like.
  - The 8-flight table is not too strict everywhere. 0 and 3 dB agree with the check pools, 6 dB is too lenient (about 2×), 9 and 12 dB are too strict, and at 15 dB both values lie under the 1e-4 floors (`C.ber_floor`, `C.deg_floor`; `C.ratio_ok` = 2, so the threshold at 9 dB is 2e-4).
  - The check pools stratify speed, altitude, K, the first interferer's direction and the receive correlation, not bank angle or attitude: their 36-flight value is itself noisy.
  - EDGE is not free of the table: its monitor reads `ber_est`.

**Decision, in five parts.**
1. *The source.* `clean_ref_rebuild.m` (`lect-kpi`) computes `clean_ref` and `PP.clean`, `PP.clean_fer` from `data/clean_val_pools.mat` only: 300 validation flights per grid Eb/N0 (seed block 4, families 1, 3 and 5 at 100 flights each, 20 frames per flight; the receiver, the Eb/N0 definition and the features are C1p's, `pool_cell.m`). The flight is the unit of a bootstrap (2000 resamples, a fixed seed per Eb/N0, 95% percentile interval, saved as `clean_ref.ci`). The function refuses another file, a set other than 'val', runs outside block 4, a file holding check pools and a different detector. The test sets and the check pools are not read for this. No `PP` field is added, so `rekey_pools` takes the file.
2. *Pre-registered* in `docs/clean_ref_protocol.md` on `lect-kpi` (code and tests `2ae5f46`, protocol `503b03a`, the tests' position in `test_core.m` `8b396e6`), written before any reading of the new table and before profile 1's C1d. Expected mean clean BER at 9 dB: 3e-4 to 6e-3 (the check pools' 7.5, 9 and 10.5 dB values give 6e-4 to 2.2e-3). The `'check'` verdict:
   - *confirmed* (inside the range, C1p's 2.4e-5 outside the new interval, no rise of the BER with Eb/N0 beyond the intervals): write.
   - *contradicted* (inside the range, C1p's value inside the interval): write anyway, so both profiles use one definition; record that the 8-flight cause did not hold.
   - *stop* (outside the range, or a rise beyond the intervals): no write. Profile 1 resumes on the C1p table and Adi is told. It is investigated on validation only (per-flight BER against the flights' draws), never on the test or check pools.

   The check is not repeated to obtain another answer.
3. *Profile 1: by hand in the software-only stop window (D94), step M of the stop checklist* (`STOP_SOFT.md`, session scratchpad `interim_report/core/`, not in this repo). It comes after the merged code is in the tree and before `rekey_pools` and C1d, so every reader sees one table.
   - M1: a 1-minute `'check'` through the wrapper and the gate (35 GB). It writes nothing and prints the verdict.
   - M2: `'write'` (45 GB). It backs up `data/policy_pools.mat` as `policy_pools_before_cleanref_<time>.mat`, refuses on `stop`, rewrites the file through `pool_checkpoint('commit')`, clears the `clean_ber_ref` cache and writes `results/clean_ref.txt`.
   - *File time.* `build_check_pools.m` and `build_clean_test_pools.m` rebuild their output when it is older than `policy_pools.mat` (about 650 + 120 + 120 min). No frame changes here, so the write sets the pools file's time to its old time plus 2 s (the old time itself where a C1c file is not newer), and the step checks that `policy_pools.mat` is the oldest of the four pool files. `rekey_pools` saves the policy pools first and the C1c files after, which keeps the order.
   - Rollback, only before `rekey_pools`: copy the backup back and set the logged time.
   - Under plan B there is no step M: profile 1 keeps the C1p table.
4. *Profile 3: inside C1c.* `run_stage.m` and `main.m` end C1c with `clean_ref_rebuild('chain')` after the validation clean pools, the clean test pools and the check pools; no verdict, no backup. Its C1d and every later stage read the new table.
5. *Unchanged:* C1p's frames, `mber`, `mfer` and `det_id`; the C1c, SURV and SURV3 outputs (none reads the table); the 'flight' reference of EDGE; the test-set rules.

**Code and trees (amends D94 points 2 and 3).**
- `lect-kpi` `5b4b970` becomes `8b396e6`: `clean_ref_rebuild.m`, the C1c call in `run_stage.m` and `main.m`, and two tests (`test_clean_ref_rebuild`, `test_clean_ref_in_c1c`), which `8b396e6` moves in `test_core.m` so that the merge with `d91-edgefix` has no conflict. The merged suite is 260 tests (258 + 2).
- The tree values of D94 point 2 change: merged tip (`v7-deploy-d91` + `d91-edgefix` + `lect-kpi`) `ac8d0d95` → `97a924f7` (the first merge, `ce4cd2ec`, is unchanged); profile 1's candidate `579f4baa` → `d8aa01a2`; `-q3` `c1081d1f` → `672a0bd7`; `code/` `dadeff6b` → `8b55d872`.

**Limits.**
- *In-sample.* The same 300 validation flights serve the validation false-alarm checks: C1d's whole-monitor bound (`choose_drop_threshold.m`) and C2's clean-validation check (`train_dqn.m`). Those readings are in-sample. One flight carries 1/300 of the mean; a single flight at BER 0.1 adds about 3.3e-4. The check prints the leave-one-family-out change; where it exceeds 0.5 at an Eb/N0 whose table is above the 1e-4 floor, the C1d and C2 reports state it beside their false-alarm numbers.
- *Floors.* The 1e-4 floors still rule at 15 dB: the change acts only where the table is above 1e-4.
- *KPI 4 at 9-12 dB becomes more lenient* (the 'mean' is the population's clean link, not the flight's: at 9 dB a restored BER up to about 4e-3 instead of 2e-4). EDGE's 'flight' reading stays beside it.
- *A table keyed on the measured Eb/N0* (`policy_monitor.m`, `M.ebno_est`) is a follow-up, not done: `ebno_est` moves with fading, attitude and the configuration's gain, it would absorb a drop of our own signal (path loss, antenna fault, shadowing), and it would change the monitor's operating point after the B-stage rehearsal. Cross-fitted references (a table per family) are a second follow-up. Both are for validation only (protocol sec. 6).

**Open.**
- The check's verdict is not read yet; step M runs in the stop window.
- The two new tests have not run (MATLAB was not run); they first run in the merged-tip suite of D94 point 3.
- `STOP_SOFT.md` takes the new tree values (its refs line, A2, A3, I2, `-q3` and section 7).
- `lect-kpi` is not pushed.

## D96 — The end-to-end plan of profile 1's stop and after (phases 0-4), approved on condition of a thorough examination with the lecturer's proposal first; the examination
**Why (2026-10-25, the user).** The user approved the end-to-end plan on 2026-10-25, on condition that it is examined thoroughly and puts the lecturer's proposal (D92) first. The examination is `plan_v2.md` (session scratchpad `interim_report/core/`, not in this repo): four audits (lecturer first, stop window, timeline, result quality), read-only checks of 2026-10-25 01:30-02:10 (summer time, UTC+3, as all times of 2026-10-25 before 02:00 in D96-D98), and an adversarial review. It changed the plan in 17 points, C1-C17 below. `STOP_SOFT.md` carries them as its V2 section (backup `STOP_SOFT.md.bak_before_v2`).
- *State at the approval.* SURV done 2026-10-25 01:49 in 386.3 min (p1.log:17628); SURV3 started 01:49:50 (p1.log:17630). The cut can come from about 05:30. The report gallery ended 2026-10-24 08:45:48 rc=0 (`v7_report_gallery.log:482`), so D94's open point on the waiting gallery job is void.
- *Memory read 2026-10-25 01:40-02:10.* Free commit 29.41 GB; the chain MATLAB 42560 11.15 GB (peak 38.06 GB); six workers 23.4 GB; zombies 8908 6.45 GB (peak 12.73) and 32824 4.63 GB; Chrome and WhatsApp 3.46 GB. Expected free commit after the cut (F0): about 63.9 GB if 42560 exits, about 52.8 GB if it lingers.

**Decision (the user's terms).**
1. *Phases* (as approved). 0: now to SURV3's end (no MATLAB; report draft 3, the noise-burst finding, local git merges). 1: the stop window: free memory after the cut; the edgefix tests; the merged suite, the proofs and the smoke; only if all pass, the code enters profile 1 (I2, the point of no return) and is pushed; the fusion swap and B3-B4s; the rehearsal and B3r once; B3c once; step M; the re-key and the resume from C1d. 2: profile 1's chain (C1d, C2 at 108 runs, C2e with the lecturer's KPIs, then to RPT). 3: the interim report (draft 4 with B3c about 2026-10-27; C2e lines if they arrive by about 2026-10-29; submission 2026-11-01). 4: after profile 1: CORE_BW, then profile 3, then the final LAT, KPI and RPT. (`plan_v2.md`'s steps 0-8 refine phases 0-1.)
2. *Memory floors* (supersede D94 point 4's 65 GB for these steps): 55 GB for the steps that can be rerun (B2F, the B stages, the rehearsal); the measured peak + 10 GB for the two read-once steps. C3 writes these as B3r `max(55, rehearsal peak + 10)` and B3c `max(55, train-only peak + 7 + 10)` (the train-only run does not include the reading's 6-7 GB), with a committed note in `W` (`uav-gcs-v7`) before L. The gate stays at 28 GB; the 35 and 45 GB floors stay.

4. *Notice:* a 30-minute notice before profile 1's resume; the resume goes ahead if there is no objection.

6. *After profile 1:* CORE_BW first, then profile 3.

**The examination (C1-C17, in short).**

Profile 3 takes the decoder with its own `core` and smoke (D98 d).

Basis: 65 GB was OOD 36 + the gather 20 + 10 (`v7_d91_takeover.md:52`), and neither runs in the window; 42560's measured peak with B3-B4s inside it is 38.06 GB (+10 = 48 ≤ 55). **Rejected:** an early lecturer test read or an earlier C2e (its readings depend on C2's `dec`); C2 at 72 runs (shortening; C2 stays at 108, closing D94's open point); a cut after C2e (the takeover accepts SURV3 only); CORE_BW pools beside C2 or profile 1's tail (paging); `f328e7b` in profile 1's window; M beside L, or anything beside B3r or B3c; B3c before B3r (it loses the hybrid's comparison).

**Expected dates** (estimates, no failure, boost off): the cut 2026-10-25 05:30-16:00 (expected 11:00); I2 2026-10-26 02:00-21:00; B3c recorded 2026-10-26 10:00 to 10-27 09:00; the resume from C1d 2026-10-26 10:00 to 10-27 11:00; C2e 2026-10-28 03:30 to 10-30 03:30 (expected 10-29 06:00-08:00); RPT about 2026-10-29 22:00; CORE_BW about 2026-10-31 midday (worst 11-02); profile 3 about 2026-11-14/16 (worst 11-18). ## D97 — Noise bursts: the rule's coded answer worsens the link; profile 1 chooses an uncoded answer on validation in C2, profile 3 gets a JSI decoder chosen on a dev set
**Why (2026-10-24/25).** The report gallery showed the rule's noise-burst answer `power_control+fec_interleave` (`rule_based_policy.m:67`) making the link worse: BER 4.39e-01 with no response, 4.95e-01 with it (`v7_report_gallery.log:474`). The finding was examined against the code by two reviews (`design_noise_burst.md`, session scratchpad `interim_report/core/`, not in this repo) and is **confirmed, with corrections**.
- *The defect.* Every coded configuration's BER, FER and CRC, in the pools and the gallery, is the decoded value under D78's erasure rules:
  - the antenna-1 energy test against the codeword's median, widened over 11 symbols: under J4 (duty 0.2, one-symbol bursts) it erases 1 − 0.8^11 = 91% of the symbols; at duty 0.5 or more the median sits inside the bursts and nothing is flagged;
  - it reads antenna 1's raw samples (`extract_closed_loop_frames.m:190`), so under `spatial_diversity` it erases symbols the MMSE null has already cleaned;
  - the pilot-error frame erasure (`pilot_err > 0.80`) was set on dev frames without bursts, so bursts on the pilots erase the whole frame.

  The flat 0.495 is total erasure: every soft value is 0 and the Viterbi output is a fixed tie-break.
- *Corrections from the reviews:* the widening covers j−7..j+3 around a burst, not j−3..j+7 (`movmax(..., [3 3+tm])`, `fec_packets.m:81`; same width); half the pool flights are J4 at duty 0.2 (`jam_timing.m:14-18`), while the gallery used duty 0.5; `rate_reduce+fec_interleave` is not one of the 36 configurations; the separable branch is broken too (the raw antenna-1 input).
- *Added:*
  - the receiver's front end uses the same median reference (robust training LS and pilot-block burst check, `build_threat_model.m:1299-1303, 1903-1905`), so at duty 0.5 or more a burst over the training corrupts the whole frame (`d84_rx.md:71`);
  - physics limits the code even with a perfect decoder. With ideal interleaving and per-symbol jammer state information, the capacity per coded bit (rate 0.503 needed, Eb/N0 9 dB, one branch) is 0.82 / 0.67 / 0.54 / 0.38 / 0.24 for J4 at 4 / 10 / 16 / 23 / 30 dB, 0.50-0.62 for the random jammer at duty 0.5, and 0.23-0.41 at duty 0.775 (our own calculation from the model's parameters). DuPree (1992) says the same in words. A better decoder therefore does not make FEC the noise-burst answer; it matters for J4 at 4-16 dB, the recoverable denominators and what the DQN, the table and the oracle learn;
  - the coded noise-burst mapping was reverted once before for the same kind of failure (D39, its rule-baseline note).

**Decision, in two parts.**
1. *Profile 1: the decision layer only, no pool rebuild* (`lect-kpi` `9122b8d`, in `TIP`; protocol `docs/noise_burst_protocol.md`, pre-registered before C1d and C2).
   - `rule_based_policy` and `rule_core_policy` take an optional `burst_action`; empty keeps the class mapping, so the detection-equivalence readers (B3, OOD, B4s, EDGE's DET) are unchanged. `policy_decide` passes `PP.rule_nb` and `PP.core_nb`; `decision_settings` and `policy_stamp` carry them.
   - *Chosen in C2's settings step* (after the nulling threshold and quiet slot), among **uncoded** candidates only, on C2's validation episodes at the reference monitor:
     - not separable: `power_control`, `rate_reduce`, `rate_reduce+power_control` (in force before the step), `channel_switch+rate_reduce+power_control`;
     - separable: `spatial_diversity`, `spatial_diversity+power_control` (in force), `spatial_diversity+rate_reduce+power_control`;
     - core rule: `rate_reduce` (in force), `channel_switch+rate_reduce`.

     Pick (`nb_pick`): one-sided 95% false-alarm bound ≤ 5% (if none, the three with the lowest bound are eligible); pooled held recovery within 0.005 of the best eligible; then the highest held recovery on the recoverable noise-burst episodes (the single threat and its four combinations), within 0.01; ties by pooled recovered, then return, then the cheaper; the setting in force has no preference; with fewer than 10 such episodes the primary is skipped. Cost: 7 validation evaluations plus one reference row (about 1-2 h of C2). Nothing is shortened.
   - *The class mapping's coded answer* is evaluated once and printed as a **reference row only**, never a candidate.
   - Tests: `test_rule_noise_burst_setting`, `test_rule_core_burst`, `test_settings_noise_burst`, `test_nb_pick` (T1b and T2).
2. *Profile 3: the JSI decoder behind `profile.json` `fec_rx`* (`lect-kpi` `f328e7b` with the fixes `c96d801`, `ad92110`, `e922d63`; not in profile 1's window, D96 C1).
   - Per-symbol residuals on the combiner output (after sync and any MMSE null), a codeword floor (q-quantile start, truncated exponential refinement), per-symbol JSI (Baldi et al. 2013), residual erasures without widening, frame variants a / b / c; no antenna-1 energy test. Without `p.fec_rx` every result is D78's bit for bit, and a full C1p refuses without a stated `fec_rx`. Profile 1 has no `profile.json` and builds no more policy pools, so it is off by construction.
   - *Chosen on dev set C* (`dev_fec_burst`, link seeds 7801-7860, 60 flights × 20 frames per point, disjoint from every pool, test, check and dev seed) **before profile 3's C1p**, by the protocol's fixed order (section 4.2) and its adoption checks (the floor check; J4 at 4 and 10 dB decodes; random duty 0.775 does not decode at 10-30 dB). The choice, or `"d78"`, is committed in profile 3's `profile.json` before `p3_c1p.hold` is released, with its own DECISIONS entry and dev table. Profile 3's C2 step then adds the two coded candidates.
   - Tests: `test_fec_rx_switch`, `test_jsi_floor`, `test_jsi_inputs`, `test_fec_packets_jsi`, `test_jsi_tails`, and dev set C's seeds in `test_seed_streams_disjoint`.

**Labels** (`design_noise_burst.md` 1.6; protocol 2.5 and 4.4):
- Rule baseline: "the rule's noise-burst answer was chosen on validation (C2) among uncoded configurations: <chosen> / <chosen, separable>; core rule <chosen>".
- Profile 1's coded configurations: "decoded with the D78 erasure rules (pilot-error frame erasure, set on dev frames without bursts; antenna-1 energy test against the codeword's median, widened by 11 symbols). Under intermittent interferers these rules erase symbols the code needs, so the coded entries understate a rate-1/2 code, most of all for J4 bursts at 4-16 dB." The DQN, the table, the oracle and ladder steps 3 and 6 carry this limit.
- KPI 4 and the envelope for noise bursts: "recoverable under profile 1's link model".
- B3, OOD, B4s and EDGE's DET keep the class mapping they ran with.
- Profile 3, when adopted: "FEC decoded with per-symbol JSI and residual erasures". Cross-profile tables show coded configurations and noise-burst rows apart; a difference there is not attributable to the antennas.

**Unchanged:** the pools, the 36 configurations, the DQN and table action set, `C.ladder`, C1d, SURV and SURV3 (already run; they do not read the rule), and the receiver's front end (part B of the design changes the features and belongs only to a rerun from A0).

**Open, optional, for the user:**
- `rate_reduce+fec_interleave` (overall rate 1/8) as a configuration: not added. It would change the action set, the DQN and the pools, and it still fails the random jammer at duty 0.775 from about 16 dB (capacity about 0.36 < 0.5). Recommended: not now.
- A profile 1 coded-sensitivity table (re-simulating only the coded configurations of its noise-burst cells after the chain, labelled as a sensitivity, never replacing C2e): compute time. Recommended: not now.

**Applied before profile 3's dev set C** (from the check of McEliece & Stark 1984, `b2_check.md`): in `lect-kpi` `3e7c945` (the J4 gate, the R0(1) row, the wording, the labels, the source; `dev_fec_gates.m`, `nb_cutoff_rate.m`, `test_dev_fec_gates`, `test_nb_cutoff_rate`), amended by `70f3c2d` (the 10 dB gate also passes within the clean bound, D100), both before dev set C runs. The items as decided:
- *J4 gate (protocol 4.2 step 4).* The 10 dB "J4 decodes" gate rests on capacity alone. For the fixed K = 7 code the cutoff rate R0(1) is the relevant measure: about 0.56 at 10 dB single branch with ideal JSI, and under profile 3's four-antenna MRC (about 4 dB left in-band) 0.73 with ideal JSI but 0.51 with the per-symbol estimate, the edge of the code; the K = 7 requirement (R0 about 0.64-0.68 for about 0.33% packet loss) still has to be sourced (Clark & Cain). A failure there would fall back to D78, which erases 91% of J4 symbols. Patch: J4 at 4 dB stays a hard gate (R0 about 0.87); at 10 dB the JSI decoder must lose more than Q/2 fewer packets than D78 (D100: or stay within the clean bound) and its loss is reported beside C and R0; add an R0 row to the printed table. Code: `dev_fec_burst.m` header, the J4 gate (`ok_j4`), the table, and the select tests in `test_core.m`.
- *Rate 1/8 arithmetic.* `rate_reduce` gives 6 dB (`cm_rate_factor` 4), not 10 dB: 10 dB leaves 4 dB in-band (capacity 0.41), 16 dB leaves 10 dB (0.28). The chain fails the random jammer at duty 0.775 from about 10 dB, not 16 dB; the decision not to add it is unchanged. The failure belongs to the modelled chain (fixed 6 dB gain, then the rate-1/2 code), not to rate 1/8: per channel use the capacity at duty 0.775 is at least 0.225 at any level, above 1/8.
- *Wording.* The capacity argument is narrowed: at duty 0.775 capacity is at most 0.41 from 4 dB; at duty 0.5 it tends to 0.5 and rules out rate 0.503 only from about 23 dB (by R0, 0.50-0.42, from 4 dB single branch); under four-antenna MRC, duty 0.5 at 4 dB is recoverable (capacity 0.80, R0 0.69).
- *Labels.* Formula (3.7) is cited as "applied to the marginal state distribution". Our JSI is estimated from hard-decision residuals (`jsi_inputs.m`), so the decoder runs without side information, as in the paper's Sec. VI; the capacity table is an upper bound for every memory length and decoder. The "11-symbol interleaver context" is D78's erasure widening, which makes J4 an erasure channel with capacity 1 - 0.91 = 0.09 (5.1).
- *Source.* McEliece & Stark 1984 is added to design Sec. 4 and protocol Sec. 5 (LITERATURE.md). Its Example 2 pulsed jammer matches J4 qualitatively (our off state has thermal noise, a random slot phase and RRC tails of about 3 symbols).

## D98 — Phase-0 pre-registrations, before any of the data they govern exists: CORE_BW's plan-A deviation, plan B's step M and B3c, profile 3's own core and smoke, the B3c floor and the rehearsal limits
**Why (2026-10-25).** `plan_v2.md` (D96) requires these to be committed in phase 0, before the cut: before CORE_BW's code, inputs or readings exist, before C2e's test reading of `rule_core − blind_core`, before the rehearsal and before B3c's training run.

**a. CORE_BW under plan A: a deviation from T5 and from `core_bw_protocol.md` (D96 C5).**
- *Base and inputs.* Worktree `uav-gcs-v7-bw`, branch `core-bw-p1` from `W`'s `v7-dev` commit at `##### STAGE C2e done`. Inputs copied from `W` and hashed (`P1_INPUTS.sha256`): T5's six files **plus the post-M `data/policy_pools.mat`** (re-keyed, about 14 GB); `core_cnn_model.mat` and `core_cnn.mat` from `W`. The hashes are checked again before `pools`.
  - Reason: without the pools file `clean_ber_ref` returns NaN (`clean_ber_ref.m:14-26`) and `policy_monitor.m:141` falls to `C.deg_floor` (1e-4). The degradation alarm then fires on the clean link at low Eb/N0 (clean BER 7.1e-2 at 0 dB, 1.0e-2 at 6 dB, p1.log:7904) for rule_core and blind_core alike. `clean_ref_protocol.md` already names CORE_BW as a reader.
- *One commit with tests on `core-bw-p1`:*
  1. an assert in `decision_inputs` (`experiment_core_bw.m:454-461`) that `clean_ber_ref(9, 'ber_est')` is finite; before `val`, the worktree's value must equal `W`'s;
  2. **blind_core's first response chosen on validation** among `channel_switch`, `channel_switch+rate_reduce` and `rate_reduce`, per hop rule. blind_core reads no class, so its one answer serves both types: it is scored on the recoverable validation episodes of every fixed-total cell (CW and the five wideband widths), equal weight per (B_J, JSR_tot). The rule is protocol 6.3's choice of W, quoted exactly:
     > 1. the lowest pooled PER after onset;
     > 2. a candidate whose paired 95% bootstrap interval of the PER difference to the best holds 0 ties;
     >    among the tied, the highest goodput, ties by the same interval rule;
     > 3. then the shortest median detection-to-restored time (never recovered counts as longest);
     > 4. then the order channel_switch, channel_switch+rate_reduce, rate_reduce.

     Reason: an equal tuning budget for the conventional system (past bug 4). Against the rule's validation-tuned W, blind_core's fixed `channel_switch+rate_reduce` first response (`decision_config.m:55`) would credit a 4x rate cut to the CNN;
  3. the fixed ladder (today's blind_core) kept as a secondary row; protocol 5's prediction on blind_core is read on that row;
  4. a deviation note `docs/core_bw_v2_note.txt`.
- *When.* The code is written and reviewed during C2 (no MATLAB), on a branch from `P1C` (`794bf2a`), and moved onto `W`'s `v7-dev` at C2e done (that adds only docs commits). Both happen before `##### STAGE C2e done`, so no CORE_BW choice follows C2e's test reading.
- *Slot.* The commit's tests (the full `test_core`, 35 GB) and CORE_BW's smoke (45 GB, no pool) run in the **p3 slot** (`V7G_ROOT` with an empty `uav-gcs-v7-p3` stand-in, as T5): the chain holds the p1 slot until RPT. `pools`, `dset`, `val`, `test` and `dread` run as T5, in the p1 slot after profile 1's end, never beside its tail.
- *Failure.* A failing test or smoke: a fix on `core-bw-p1` with a test, then both rerun. `pools` never start on an untested commit. Profile 3 waits (CORE_BW first, D96 point 6) and the user is told; profile 3 goes first only if the user chooses.

**b. Plan B keeps step M (D96 C9; amends D95 part 3's last bullet and `clean_ref_protocol.md` 5's last line).** `208602b` reads the same fields (`clean_ber_ref.m:19-26`, `link_env.m:190`). Under plan B, before `p1_resume.go`: M1 from `X`, then `clean_ref_rebuild('write', ...)` from `X` with absolute paths to `W`'s `data/clean_val_pools.mat` and `data/policy_pools.mat` (45 GB, p1 slot), with D95's pre-registered verdict rule (on `stop`, no write). Data only; no code change in `W`. Reason: otherwise every plan-B lecturer line rests on C1p's 8-flight table (2.4e-5 at 9 dB against 2.0e-3 on the check pools, p1.log:7904, :11440).

**c. Plan B runs B3c before its resume (D96 C16).** After plan B's step M and before `p1_resume.go`, the isolated B3c (RUNBOOK_NOSTOP T3: train-only run, check, then the single reading) runs alone.
- No B3r runs, so `docs/core_cnn_no_b3r.txt` (`e84a5fd`) holds.
- Code: `X`, or a clean worktree at a named commit whose suite passed. Floor as in e.
- Exception: if a T2 smoke still runs (about 51 < 55 GB free), the resume goes first and B3c runs at profile 1's end ("reading pending" in the report).
- Reason: T3's old trigger never opens at these floors beside the chain (about 63.9 − 16.7 ≈ 47 < 55 GB). The three-state macro-F1, recall per type and detector false alarms would otherwise come about 2026-10-30.

**d. Profile 3 runs its own `core` and smoke (supersedes D94 point 6's clause "`core` and the smoke come from profile 1's run on the same tip", `caee083`).** Profile 3's tip is `TIP3` = `TIP` + `lect-kpi` `f328e7b` through `e922d63` (D97 part 2). Its trees were recomputed for `e922d63` on 2026-10-25 (`TIP3` tree `4bb79654`, `Q3'` tree `eb197b55`, `code/` `778ef42e`, 269 tests; plan_v2's `ca5af7ee` was `TIP` + `f328e7b` alone and is stale); a later `lect-kpi` commit needs them recomputed again. It runs paths T2's D78 smoke never exercises, so it needs its own `core` and smoke. Its non-smoke jobs follow D96 C12. Dev set C runs in `uav-gcs-v7-p3` after its merge, before its C1p.

**e. The B3c floor and the rehearsal limits.**
- *B3c floor* `max(55, train-only peak + 7 + 10)`: the train-only run's measured peak, plus 7 GB for the reading beyond the training (the protocol's 6-7 GB), plus the 10 GB margin of the other floors.
  - `core_cnn_protocol.md` 9 says 65 GB. Its basis (OOD 36 + gather 20 + 10, `v7_d91_takeover.md:52`) does not run in B3c.
  - The deviation is committed in `W` as `docs/core_cnn_floor_note.txt` before the train-only run. It is a separate file, because L requires `code` and the protocol clean.
  - Without a sampler reading, the protocol's 14 GB peak gives 55.
- *Rehearsal limits*, committed in `W` as `docs/d91_b3r_expect_p1.txt` before the rehearsal, so before B3r. On fresh2 (in the fit set, so not a test read), with the deployed fusion:
  - fault F1 ≥ 88% (expected 84.35 + 5.80 = 90.15);
  - macro-F1 above the KPI 1 threshold ≥ 93.9% (expected 94.36 + 0.64 = 95.00);
  - clean read as a threat ≤ 3.45% (expected 2.79 − 0.29 = 2.50).

  Expected values: `d91_fuse.md`, the M4 out-of-fold block (the base line, the branch-evidence line, set fresh2's fault F1). A miss: no B3r; the deployment is reviewed (B2F log, J2), fixed from the sources and the rehearsal rerun. No set is spent.

**Open.** CORE_BW's commit is not written yet (during C2). `TIP3` is not built as a branch yet (its trees are known). `docs/core_cnn_floor_note.txt` and `docs/d91_b3r_expect_p1.txt` are written in `W` at their steps.

## D99 — Lecturer readings at link-limited Eb/N0 points: pooled verdict unchanged, a pre-registered non-L line and per-Eb/N0 rows; recovery time undefined where the restoration test cannot fail

**Why (2026-10-25, SURV review finding 3).** The packet-loss half of "restored" (link_env.m:191,
pl <= 2 x clean + one loss event) cannot fail where the clean link loses half its packets or
more: in profile 1's pools 0.99 / 0.84 / 0.62 at 0 / 3 / 6 dB (p1.log:7904). There
"recoverable" is BER-only. Recovery time from detection (per_metrics.m, on restored_plr) is 0 by
construction for every detected episode, under every policy including no response, which is
about half the recoverable jamming episodes. T_rec from onset and KPI 4 are BER-only there.
PER reduction and the CNN's share can move either way: an answer without link budget is diluted
there, while rate_reduce (+6 dB) also cures the clean link's own loss. Both weigh heavily because
the reduction is a ratio of sums and the static PER there is near 1. Goodput in bit/s is unbiased;
the normalized goodput already leaves out the points with clean loss > 0.9. Every lecturer reading pools all
six Eb/N0 points (policy_episodes.m; ebno_thr gates only KPI 6). No test data of these readings
had been read when this was decided.

**Decision.**
1. Mark: grid point s is link-limited (L) when C.ratio_ok x PP.clean_fer(s) >= 1, from the post-M
   clean table (D95), computed once and hashed before results/policy_evaluation.mat is opened.
   Descriptive tag beside it: working point (J) when clean_fer < 0.1 (IEEE 802.11a-1999
   sensitivity, PER < 10%), marginal (M) otherwise.
2. The lecturer's KPIs and the pooled all-points readings of lect_kpis.m (KP.lect) are unchanged
   and remain the verdict lines.
3. Reporting-only addition, always printed in this order: Line A (all points, the verdict), Line B
   (non-L points, labelled second line, same verdict words), per-Eb/N0 rows with tag and counts
   (their verdict words labelled descriptive). lect_kpis.m is called unchanged on subsets of the
   saved ALL. When Lines A and B differ in verdict words, the result sentence names both; Line A
   stays the verdict.
4. Recovery time from detection is undefined at L points (the lecturer's "below a defined
   threshold" has no threshold at >= 100%). The headline recovery time is Line B. Line A is shown
   with the count of its episodes set by construction (0, or never when detected in the last 4
   cycles). t_conf is reported everywhere. T_rec and KPI 4 rows at L points are tagged BER-only.
5. KPI 6 (system false alarms): verdict unchanged; per-Eb/N0 rows and the non-L line added,
   descriptive, from the same union RU the verdict reads (RES clean, clean2 and RW).
6. No code change in profile 1's tip (8b28bdf) or TIP3. A new script lect_kpis_by_ebno.m with its
   test, reviewed and tested before P1's STAGE C2e done, run once after it. Gate: on the full ALL
   it must reproduce KP.lect exactly, and its byte copy of far_report must reproduce KP.far on the
   full RU, or no subset is printed. Profile 3: the same rules on its own post-M table.
7. CORE_BW: verdicts and code unchanged. It reads against the same flight's clean link and judges
   harm on pooled means, so the post-M mark does not apply to it. Its mark is per episode: the
   flight's own threshold (2 x its clean loss + 1/n, exact for the core configurations) >= 1,
   from its pools' test-split clean cell, hashed before its test results are opened. The same
   rules R2-R3 apply, read from the saved RES/EPs.

**Code.** Branch `lect-ebno` (from `lect-kpi` `9122b8d`; not pushed), commit `9d5bfc8`:
`code/evaluation/lect_kpis_by_ebno.m`, `code/evaluation/core_bw_lect_by_link.m` (CORE_BW, a separate
function: its readings are `experiment_core_bw.m`'s `per_lines`, not `lect_kpis.m`),
`code/tests/test_lect_kpis_by_ebno.m` (the seven tests of the decision plus the single run, the
copies and the readers against `TIP`, and CORE_BW), the protocol `docs/lect_ebno_protocol.md`, and
pointer lines in `c2e_lect_protocol.md` 6 and `core_bw_protocol.md` 6.5. The readers it calls are
`TIP`'s blobs; `far_report`, `cp_upper` and `experiment_core_bw.m`'s seven readers are byte copies.
Tests not run yet: they run once in the stop window during T2's smoke, in the p3 slot.

**Rejected.** No change (t_rec_det wrong by construction). Non-L as the verdict (the pooled line
was pre-registered; the direction of the L points' effect is not known before the reading). A
guard in link_env.m (changes rewards, training and every reader; untestable before T2). A capped
absolute recovery threshold (a new KPI parameter; a P1 code change; measures recovery from outage,
not from jamming).

**Sources.** Xu et al. 2005 (lecturer ref [8]): poor link quality and jamming are separate causes
of low PDR. IEEE 802.11a-1999: sensitivity at PER < 10%; interference rejection tested 3 dB above
sensitivity. Baldi 2013: jamming varied at a fixed working Eb/N0 = 10 dB. Lecturer 20.10: recovery
time until PER falls below a defined threshold and stays below it.

**Update (2026-10-25, after M1 early and the script's first test run; no result of the decision
layer read).** The decision is unchanged; two facts it meets:
1. The post-M table (M1 early, verdict `clean_ref` confirmed, D95; review `m1_review.md`) gives
   clean_fer 0.99 / 0.79 / 0.42 / 0.14 / 0.044 / 0.018 at 0 / 3 / 6 / 9 / 12 / 15 dB. The rule of
   point 1 then marks L = {0, 3} dB only (at 6 dB 2 x 0.46 = 0.92 < 1 even at the top of the
   interval [0.38, 0.46]), tags M = {6, 9} (clean loss >= 0.1) and J = {12, 15}. 9 dB is M, not the
   working point the protocol's text expected from the old table; the rule computes the tag, so no
   rule changes, and the report must not call 9 dB a working point. Recovery time from detection
   is defined at 6 dB and enters Line B.
2. The first run of the script's tests gave 11/13: `maxNumCompThreads('automatic')` is illegal
   under the gated wrapper's `-singleCompThread`. `lect-ebno` `be89e74`: the call is made only
   where the session allows it, and `lect_ebno_protocol.md` is amended (one thread under the
   wrapper; the exact gate decides either way). If the gate fails on last-bit differences between
   a one-thread and a multithreaded run, the script runs multithreaded outside the wrapper; a
   tolerance is never added. Rerun 13/13.

## D100 — Profile 3's survivability map v2 and dev set C's gates: pre-registered design and two amendments before any data

**Why (2026-10-25).** The refutation pass over profile 1's map (session review `surv_review.md`, items 1-6, 10, 11, N1, N2) found a one-flight map with no interval, the noise bursts at the least severe duty of their range, and marks missing where the clean link itself fails. Profile 1 keeps its map (already run, SURV 386.3 min); profile 3's map is fixed now, before its SURV or any of its data exists. Protocol `docs/surv_p3_protocol.md` on `lect-kpi`.

**Design (`lect-kpi` `f345910`, test seeds `706fa79`).**
- *Switch.* `profile.json` `surv_v2` (`surv_options.m`): `true` runs the protocol; absent or `false` runs the one-flight map, every line, number and file unchanged (`test_surv_map_profile1_identity`). Profile 1 has no `profile.json` and its `TIP` does not take this code. Profile 3's `profile.json` gains `"surv_v2": true` before its SURV; the coded link is mapped with the stated `fec_rx` (D97).
- *Noise bursts: two entries per geometry* (`surv_nb_entries.m`), `jam_timing_random` off: Xu et al.'s random jammer at duty 0.775 (`burst_duty_range(end)`, the most severe of the measured range; profile 1 used 0.5) and Marti et al.'s J4 at duty 0.2 (`burst_j4(1)`, one-symbol slots).
- *Flights.* 5 flights of 57 frames per cell; common random numbers: seeds `900000 + 1000 t + r` (r = 1..5, t the entry), independent of level and Eb/N0, disjoint from the one-flight seeds and every other family (`test_seed_streams_disjoint`). Per-flight BER, packet loss and slack are kept; the unchanged status rule (R, M, X; KPI 4's) reads their means.
- *Marks and labels* (`surv_grid.m`, `surv_cell_label.m`, `surv_report.m`): L, an R cell where the clean link loses >= 50% of its packets (BER alone decides; counted apart, R + L also given); N, no_action passes; none, X with every configuration at BER >= 0.45; ?, not committed; "0 err" for a zero ratio.
- *Interval and verdict.* Per configuration and cell a 95% percentile bootstrap over the flights (`boot_cluster.m`, 4000 resamples, seed 74), widened to hold the mean, with a Clopper-Pearson floor at no event; committed when the status at the low and at the high end of the intervals agree. It is the spread over flights at one geometry, not the envelope's commitment.
- *Gap classes* (`surv_gap_report.m`): class 1 Map A X to Map B R or M; class 2 Map A M to Map B R.
- *Run time.* 386.3 min x 5 flights x 1.125 (16 added J4 jobs) x 1.15-1.31 (four antennas): about 42-48 h, plus up to 2 h of tail: 44-50 h. Nothing shortened.

**Amendments (`lect-kpi` `70f3c2d`, same day, still before any profile 3 data or dev set C reading).**
1. *Coded BER bound* (review item 5). A coded configuration's decoded errors come in whole lost packets, not independent bits, so the bit-level Clopper-Pearson floor (1/(5 x 28 x 1032) units) overstated what five flights show. For configurations with `fec_interleave`: BER upper bound = one-sided 95% Clopper-Pearson upper bound of the packet loss over the cell's packets x 0.5, the BER of a lost packet (a failed Viterbi packet is at most at chance level; D97's total-erasure tie-break measured 0.495). With errors it floors the bootstrap bound. At no lost packet in 140 it is 1.06e-2, above 5x any reference from 1e-4 to 2.1e-3, so a cell that only a coded configuration recovers will usually be not committed with five flights; that is the honest statement, and the protocol says so. Uncoded configurations unchanged.
2. *J4 gate at 10 dB* (D97, `3e7c945`). It now passes when the loss is within the clean bound (2 x the clean link's at 9 dB, floored at one packet) or more than Q/2 packets below D78. A gain over D78 alone would reject a perfect JSI decoder at a point where D78 happens to do well too. `noise_burst_protocol.md` 4.2.
3. *Minor.* N is shown on R and L cells only; M and X cells keep their best configuration's letters (both maps exclude no_action); the N count still covers every cell.

Tests updated: `test_surv_grid_marks` (the coded bound at no, some and every lost packet; coded-only cells not committed), `test_dev_fec_gates` (within the bound without gain passes; a perfect decoder beside a perfect D78 passes), `test_surv_cell_label`. Not run here (no MATLAB in this pass); they run with the next `test_core` run before profile 3's SURV and dev set C.

**Rejected.** Treating decoded bits as independent trials (understates the bound by about three orders of magnitude). More flights per coded cell (changes the pre-registered run and the CRN design; the not-committed mark already states the limit). A gate on D78's gain alone (rejects an ideal decoder).

## D101 — Profile 3's SURV3 v2 (three against four antennas): pre-registered changes from the SURV3 review, before any profile 3 data

**Why (2026-10-25; the times of that day from 02:00 on, here and in the log, are winter time, UTC+2; those before 02:00 in D96-D98 are summer time, UTC+3).** Profile 1's SURV3 ended at 10:49 (599.9 min, `p1.log:17630-20521`, code `208602b`). A refutation pass over it and over two earlier reviews (session review `surv3_review.md`, items 1-10, N1, N2; read only, nothing run) found that nothing but a detail line reads the file (`measure_all_kpis.m:236`): no lecturer KPI value and no later stage depends on it, so profile 1 keeps its run and takes report wording only (below). Profile 3's SURV3 is fixed now, before it or any of its data exists, as D100 did for its map. Only the n - 1 antenna comparison is reported (profile 3: three against four antennas); the relay path (dropped, D92) runs as before and is not reported. The findings that change the design (the others were refuted or need no action):
1. *Packet bound vacuous (item 1).* The packet half of the rule is 2 x the clean link's loss + a slack of 1/20 (uncoded). It reaches 100% for two antennas at 0, 3, 6 and 9 dB (exactly 1.0 at 9 dB, where the clean loss is 0.475, so "clean loss >= 0.5" is the wrong test) and for three antennas at 0 and 3 dB: BER alone decides there. The 6 dB inversion of the earlier reviews is not the criterion: the five path-loss combinations share the same 8 flights and each flight recovers in all five or in none, so "8/40 against 1/40" is 1.6 against 0.2 flights.
2. *Each receiver against its own clean link (item 2).* The three-antenna link is at or below the two-antenna link in BER and packet loss at every Eb/N0, so the two-antenna shares are upper bounds and the gain a lower bound. The relay is judged against the system's link, the n - 1 receiver against its own; both questions are legitimate, only the first was stated.
3. *Shadowing combinations not exercised (item 3).* SURV3's random flights have no roll-in (`roll_in_frames = []`), so none hid an antenna: jamming + path loss and jamming + airframe shadowing + path loss are identical in all 144 geometry cells of every variant, and jamming + airframe shadowing and tone + spoofing + airframe shadowing are 100% everywhere. Three of the 14 combinations are duplicates of their non-shadowing counterparts (one carries 100% rows into the mean). The pools reach this case only through selected candidates (`hides_antenna`).
4. *Singles not at the sources' most severe value (N1).* `iHigh = C.nominal + 1` is level 4, 23 dB (spoofing 12 dB), not the in-band cap of 30 dB (Liu et al.; level 5 of `decision_config.m`). They are saturated: jamming, reactive jamming, spoofing, sweeper and tone recover in 48 of 48 geometries with every variant. The project rule takes the most severe value within the sources.
5. *Only booleans saved (N2).* The binding test, its margin and each flight's own clean link cannot be read back; the reference is the mean of the same 8 flights.

What the pass confirmed (profile 1, 11 combinations without the shadowing ones): the third antenna's gain is real, 33 geometries better with three antennas against 3 worse, geometry-cluster sign test p = 2.3e-7 (the 672- and 960-cell McNemar tests of the earlier reviews count duplicated rows as independent); like for like at 12-15 dB, where neither clean link is link-limited, 50.0% (88/176) against 60.8% (107/176), and 53.0% against 64.6% over all Eb/N0. The gain sits in noise burst + antenna fault, 2 of 48 (4.2%) against 24 of 48 (50%), and it holds in the flights whose antenna 1 (the one the erasure flag reads) is healthy: 2 of 26 against 19 of 34, and 3 of 26 against 20 of 34 with spoofing (Fisher p = 1.0e-4 and 1.9e-4). Physics: one antenna is left after a fault, and N antennas null N - 1 interferers. The earlier claim that the gap is partly decoder-limited is refuted. Profile 1's erasure flag on antenna 1 (item 5) stays design Part A (D97), not this entry.

**Decision (`lect-kpi` `b958265`, amended by `53f7b24` the same day, before any profile 3 data; protocol `docs/surv_p3_protocol.md` section 10).** All behind `profile.json` `surv_v2` (true for profile 3 from D100); without it every seed, number, text and saved variable is profile 1's (`test_surv3_profile1_identity`), and profile 1's `TIP`, `P1C`, `P1O`, `-q3` and the deploy queue take nothing of it.
- *Link-limited mark (item 1).* A clean link is L at an Eb/N0 where 2 x its packet loss + 1/20 >= 1; each column of the per-Eb/N0 table carries L where its reference is L, with the clean packet loss printed. L points stay in the rule's count: marked, not removed. The shares are also given over the Eb/N0 where no clean link is L (profile 1: 12-15 dB), beside the shares over all Eb/N0. The status rule (KPI 4's) is unchanged.
- *Both references (item 2).* Every variant is also judged against the system's clean link (n antennas), on the saved per-geometry values, with no extra simulation; columns: n - 1 against its own link, n - 1 against the n-antenna link, n, relay. Stated now from physics: against the system's link the n - 1 share can only fall. The primary comparison is n - 1 against the system's link versus n, at the Eb/N0 where neither clean link is L.
- *Shadowing combinations on selected flights (item 3).* The three combinations fly flights of their own, selected as the pools select theirs (`surv3_shadow_flights.m`): candidates from their own seed range `62 000 000 + 100 000 (s - 1) + j` (above the pools' candidates up to 61 199 999, below 2^26; `shadow_pool_seed.m` takes no ninth block), speed uniform from the level's floor to 161 km/h, kept when the roll-in hides an antenna at the level (15.5 dB) with both n - 1 and n antennas. The roll-in brings the airframe into the line of sight just before frame 1, so every frame lies after the onset, as in the other cases. The same 8 flights per Eb/N0 serve the three combinations and the three variants (common random numbers); the candidates tried are printed and saved; the rows are conditioned on a flight that hides an antenna, as the pools' shadowing points are. *Amendment `53f7b24`:* their reference is the clean link of those same flights (two added clean jobs, under 1% of the cost, with L marks of their own), not the shared flights' link, as the pools restore their shadowing cells against the clean link of their own flights (`PP.ref_geo`): the selected flights differ from the shared ones in speed and attitude, so the shared link would mix that geometry into the threat's effect.
- *Singles at level 5 (N1).* 30 dB for jamming, reactive jamming, noise bursts, the sweeper and the tone; the spoofer 30 dB over our signal. Same jobs, same cost. Stated now: channel switching then leaves 0 dB (`cm_acr_db`), so nulling decides and the shares fall for both arrays; one emitter needs one null, which both arrays have, so the antenna count may still matter little. That is the result, not a reason to stay at 23 dB.
- *Saved values (N2).* Per job, BER, packet loss and slack per Eb/N0, geometry and configuration, and the clean link per geometry (`ber_geo`, `fer_geo`), about 2.5 MB of doubles: every criterion above can be read again without MATLAB. A clean BER below the floor is printed "<1e-4", not "0.0e+00".
- *Decoder.* Outside the smoke `surv_v2` refuses to start without `fec_rx` in `profile.json` (D97), as the map does (D100).
- *Run time (an estimate, protocol 10.9).* Profile 1's 599.9 min x 1.15-1.31 for the fourth antenna: 690-786 min, about 11.5-13.5 h on six workers. The selection runs no simulation and level 5 costs the same, so no date of profile 3 moves. Not timed: the cost of the JSI decoder if dev set C chooses it.
- *Tests* (`code/tests/test_core.m`): `test_surv3_profile1_identity`, `test_surv3_v2_job`, `test_surv3_link_limited`, `test_surv3_levels`, `test_surv3_shadow_flights`, and the new seeds in `test_seed_streams_disjoint`. Not run here (no MATLAB in this pass); profile 3 runs its own `core` of 283 tests (D98 d).

**Profile 3's tip.** `TIP3` = `TIP` (`85484b0`) + `lect-kpi` `53f7b24`: tree `19f2bfe6`; `29510e7` + `TIP3` `38d61145`; `Q3'` `8d0d07bf`; `code/` `f9f225b1`; 283 tests. They supersede D98 d's values (`4bb79654`, `eb197b55`, `778ef42e`, 269 tests, computed for `e922d63`) and the intermediate ones; the new trees differ from the ones for `9b29dcb` in the 11 files of `b958265` and `53f7b24` only. A later `lect-kpi` commit needs them recomputed again.

**Profile 1 (no rerun).** The report words what ran: the comparison at 12-15 dB first, where both clean links work, and the all-Eb/N0 shares second; 0-3 dB (and 6-9 dB with two antennas) marked as below the clean link's operating point, where the criterion reduces to BER; the 11 combinations, with the three shadowing ones named as not exercised (the shadowing case is covered by the survivability map's aimed turn); each antenna count against its own clean link, the two-antenna shares being upper bounds; the results by family (path loss, which is one link budget; noise burst with and without a fault; the rest) with the cluster sign test; the single threats at the high level (23 dB; spoofing 12 dB), recovered everywhere because one emitter needs one null. SURV3's percentages are not set beside SURV's (a different geometry). The same kind of wording covers profile 1's SURV (386.3 min): noise bursts mapped at duty 0.5 only, the least severe of Xu et al.'s measured 0.5-0.775; the coded entries' erasure flag read on antenna 1; the packet test vacuous at 0-3 dB (the totals move by 0.3-0.5 points only); one flight per cell; channel-switch cells without a follower or a comb. No lecturer KPI reads the map.

**Rejected.** A rerun of profile 1's SURV3 (nothing reads it; about 10 h on a machine whose stop window is reserved for the lecturer's items). Dropping the shadowing combinations from profile 3's cases (the review's fallback if the selected flights were not ready; they are, with their seed range and the disjointness test). Keeping the shared flights' clean link for the shadowing rows (amended: it mixes the selected flights' geometry into the threat's effect). Quoting 6-15 dB as the comparison (the two-antenna packet test is still vacuous at 6-9 dB; 12-15 dB is the only range where both tests are informative).

**Sources.** Liu et al. 2018 (the in-band level cap, 30 dB). Winters, Salz & Gitlin 1994 (N antennas null N - 1 interferers). Xu et al. 2005 (the noise-burst duty range, D100). The reviews: session scratchpad `interim_report/core/surv3_review.md` and `surv_review.md`, not in this repo.

## D102 — Profile 1's deploy queue: the five git-dependent tests that fail on the queue's export are read from the git worktree of the same tip

**Why (2026-10-25, the stop window).** The queue's `core` job ran the merged suite on its export of `85484b0` (`d91q/deploy2/tip`, made by `git archive`): 259/264, rc=1. The five failures are `test_rule_core_wide`, `test_rule_noise_burst_setting`, `test_rule_core_burst`, `test_settings_noise_burst` and `test_link_env_cover_default`. Each calls the test helper `parent_copy`, which runs `git show <rev>:<file>` in the test root to compare with an older commit. The export has no `.git`, so every one stops with `fatal: not a git repository`. The code they test is the same in the export and in the worktree.

**Evidence.** On worktree `X` (`uav-gcs-tip`, a git worktree detached at `85484b0`, the same tree `553658e4`) the same five pass: 5/5 on 25.10 (`uav-gcs-v7-p3/logs/hand_tip_git5_*`). They also passed in T0, the full suite on `X` at `66ec267` (the commit before, which differs only in `core_cnn_inputs.m` and three test assumptions). With the queue's 259 that is 264/264 on this tree.

**Decision.** The queue's `core` verdict, which reads BLOCKED because new tests are not passing, is taken as passed for these five tests only, on the evidence above. Any other failing test still blocks. `p3core` (the suite on the `P1C` export) is read the same way. No gate is lowered and no test is changed for profile 1.

**For profile 3.** `lect-kpi` `899e1c6`: in an export without `.git`, `parent_copy` reads the old commit from the repository that `V7_GIT_REPO` names. Profile 3's queue command sets `V7_GIT_REPO` to the deploy checkout (STOP_SOFT section 7), so its `core` runs every test on the export.

**Rejected.** Restarting profile 1's queue on a new tip that carries `899e1c6`: it changes only a test helper, and the restart would cost the core job, the proofs and the smoke, about a day, inside the time box.

## D103 — Profile 1's system-level clean false alarm: reporting, breakdown and a pre-onset sensitivity line, registered before C2e; the degradation path's fix registered for profile 3 and a profile 1 rerun

**Why (26.10, C1d).** The whole monitor at no_action breaks the 5% bound under every confirmation: 2/2 505/1800 (29.85%), 3/3 368 (22.07%), 4/5 329 (19.84%), 5/5 201 (12.46%). The other paths alone also break it: 484, 338, 297, 161 (`results/drop_threshold.txt:79-106`). The drop path adds 21-40 episodes, the sweeper path none. The lecturer's FAR KPI is read on the detector (B3c, B3r; `design_final.md` 6.5) and is unaffected. No C2 or C2e result had been read when this was decided.

**Decision for profile 1 (no code change in profile 1's tip):**
1. **Naming.** The system-level clean-episode false alarm is our KPI 6 (an extension). It is never called lecturer KPI 3. The lecturer's KPI 3 verdict lives with B3c (core CNN) and the hybrid's three-state view (`design_final.md` 6.5), with B3r beside them. The hybrid's 11-class share of clean frames read as any threat (5.36%, none as a jamming state) is printed beside the verdict, because it feeds the class path of KPI 6.
2. **KPI 6 verdict unchanged.** `KP.far` is read on the pre-registered union of clean test sets: Clopper-Pearson one-sided 95% upper bound ≤ 5%, every configuration, at least 600 flights, Eb/N0 ≥ the KPI 1 threshold. The per-Eb/N0 rows and the non-L line stay descriptive (D99 point 5).
3. **Breakdown (descriptive, saved files only).** Per policy (none, rule_esc, blind_esc, rule_core, blind_core, the deployed DQN), on the same clean episodes as `KP.far`:
   - (a) false-alarm episodes per Eb/N0 grid point: D99 point 5's rows from the same union RU, extended from `far_report`'s two policies to all six (one table, not a second definition);
   - (b) the path at the first change: hostile class, degradation with class none/benign/unknown, or drop. From `fc_cls`, `fc_deg` and `fc_dal` (the monitor's drop alarm, saved by `rollout_policy.m:128`). These are the change cycle's flags; an m-of-n confirmation built from mixed paths is attributed to that cycle's flags, and the row says so;
   - (c) the Xu split: false-alarm episodes with `false_sw > 0` (a change on a flight whose clean link passes the 2× test at no_action over the whole flight, `K.healthy`, `link_env.m:182`; a per-flight flag, not per cycle) against those with `switches > 0` and `false_sw = 0` (flights whose clean link itself fails it). The verdict line counts both; the split is descriptive;
   - (d) false changes per cycle and per hour (`far_report`'s existing line), beside the per-episode share (Xie 2021: FAR = 1/ARL);
   - (e) the cycle of the first false change (`fc_t`) as a histogram, with the share inside the first `C.win` − 1 cycles, where the BER window is not yet full (`policy_monitor.m:88, 131`: a 1-4 frame mean). A start-up transient is a simulation artefact of episode starts, not a property of a link that runs continuously, so it is reported as such and handled in section 4, never removed from this reading.
4. **Pre-onset sensitivity of the lecturer readings (descriptive, labelled "sensitivity").** On the recoverable jamming episodes of every lecturer set:
   - (a) per policy, the share with a first change before the onset;
   - (b) the lecturer readings (PER reduction vs static, t_conf, t_rec_det, goodput, both CNN-contribution pairs), recomputed with `lect_kpis.m` unchanged on the subset S0 of episodes with no change before the onset under any of the compared policies. Report S0's size and its Eb/N0 composition beside it.
   - (c) S0's readings also per Eb/N0 point and on D99's Line B (non-L points), beside the same rows of the full set, so S0's better-link mix is not read as bias.
   - The pooled Line A of D99 stays the verdict. If S0 and Line A differ in verdict words, the result sentence names both.
   - Episodes are excluded only on pre-onset behaviour, which no threat can affect. So S0 is not selected on the threat response, but it is selected on link quality, hence (c).
   - (d) Mechanism D, from C2's log (validation, read before C2e): for every step whose pick fell back to "the three with the lowest bound", the chosen variant against the variant with the highest weakest-threat held recovery without the FA filter, with their held recovery for jamming and tone_jamming and their FA, and whether the pick was decided by an FA tie. Descriptive; it names which lecturer readings were read on FA-constrained settings.
5. **Carried-over alarms.** Per policy, the count of episodes whose `t_conf` is below m−1 cycles (m of the deployed confirmation), where the confirmation did not come from the sweeper's own path. t_conf is also reported without them. Descriptive.
6. **Gate.** All of 3-5 except 4(d) (read from C2's log) come from a new script `system_fa_breakdown.m` that calls the readers unchanged. On the full sets it must reproduce `KP.far` and `KP.lect` exactly, or nothing of 3-5 is printed (as D99 point 6). It is written, reviewed and tested on the smoke's saved `policy_evaluation.mat` before it is run once on profile 1's.
7. **Report wording, fixed now.**
   - If KPI 6 is not met: "System-level false alarms on clean flights (extension, our KPI 6): NOT MET, X% [upper Y%]", with the breakdown as the cause.
   - Then: "the degradation path's fix (D103 part 2) is chosen on validation and read on a fresh clean test set in profile 3 and in the profile 1 rerun".
   - No sentence calls the miss acceptable or out of scope.
8. **EDGE.** If `fa_bound_met` is false, EDGE commits no point at system level. That is the pre-registered rule and is reported as it is, without a patch.


**Part 2: the degradation path's fix (profile 3 and the profile 1 rerun P1R).** Root cause, from the code: the degradation test looks up its reference with one noisy frame's Eb/N0 estimate while the BER is a 5-frame mean, and its threshold is 2x a population mean, keyed on the nominal Eb/N0 but read at the measured one. Low BER alone is not evidence of an RF threat (Xu 2005); the quiet-slot measurement that separates the two exists per frame and the monitor does not use it. Candidates, each behind `C.deg_mode` (default `'ratio'` = the current code, bit-identical):
- **F1 `'win'`:** the lookup uses the median of the measured per-frame Eb/N0 estimates over the BER window's frames (a new `C.win`-frame memory, reset with `mem.ber` at a change's arrival).
- **F2 `'win_q'`:** F1, and the threshold is the (1-alpha) quantile of the clean 5-frame-mean `ber_est`, binned on the measured windowed Eb/N0 (new clean-table field `ber_q`), with `deg_floor` kept; alpha in {1e-2, 1e-3}. A bin merges with its neighbour until it holds at least 10/alpha clean cycles from at least 30 flights; what that leaves is reported, not worked around.
- **F3 `'win_q_gate'`:** F2, and the degradation flag raises an alarm only if `q_iot_db >= C.q_alarm_db` in at least one frame of the window; otherwise the state is "degraded link, no RF evidence": logged, no alarm. The class and drop paths are unchanged.
- **F0 (conditional):** if point 3(e) shows the false changes front-loaded in the first `C.win` - 1 cycles, F0 goes ahead of F1: the degradation test only on a full window at an episode's start, with the frames before cycle 1 simulated so the window is full. F4 (confirmation over n > `C.win` or non-overlapping windows) is not a candidate unless F1-F3 all fail the threat rule.

**Adoption rule.** In the order F0 (if added), F1, F2 (each alpha), F3 (each alpha), C1d's whole-monitor analysis runs on the validation episodes, every monitor, the drop threshold re-chosen as now. A candidate is admissible if, against `'ratio'` under the same monitor: (a) for jamming and tone_jamming, median and p90 delay and the never share do not grow; (b) for every other threat, the never share grows by at most 1 point and p90 by at most 1 cycle. Adopt the first admissible candidate whose bound is <= 5% for at least one monitor; if none reaches 5%, adopt the admissible candidate with the lowest bound, and KPI 6 is read and reported as it falls. The rule reads alarm delays only; F3's cost in recovery (a quiet-slot degraded link no longer gets `freq_diversity+power_control`) is read in C2's validation rows and reported beside the adoption, and does not reopen the choice.

**Fit/check split.** The 300 validation clean geometries are split by geometry hash into a fit half (150) and a check half (150). `ber_q` and its bins come from the fit half only; adoption is decided on the check half (900 clean episodes: a bound <= 5% needs about 34 or fewer false-alarm episodes). C2 reads its own FA filter on all 300 geometries, mildly optimistic for C2's selection only. KPI 6 is read on a fresh clean test set (a new seed family, generated after adoption, at least 600 flights). *[Amended 30.10.2026 by D112 (registered under decide-after-30min, Adi silent since 30.10.2026 19:37; profile 3 only): after the F choice the check half is read a second time, for D112's DCONF coordinate step under D112 point 4.]*
