*[Amended 31.10.2026 by D121 (approved by Adi, 31.10.2026; profile 3 only): after the F choice the check half is read for CLT's τ_hi scan (D121 item 1), so D112's DCONF step is its third use (disclosed, the D90 and D112 precedent); the drop threshold's re-choice after CLT follows D121 item 4.]*

**Tests, committed before any data they govern.** Bit-identity of `'ratio'` on a recorded no_action trace; F1 (window median on a trace with one +3 dB frame, measured axis under a known gain, reset with `mem.ber`); F2 (quantile reproduces alpha on synthetic Gaussian log-BER, small bins merge, fit and check halves disjoint by a hash assert); F3 (quiet-slot interference alarms, a quiet slot does not; class and drop alarms unchanged by `deg_mode`); one smoke cell per candidate (2 h cap). The DQN's false-switch penalty definition (`healthy` geometries only) is not changed: a reward redesign without a source. *[Amended 29.10.2026 by D107 (approved by Adi): the default penalty form is unchanged; a second form restricts the penalty's excess over w_false to no-threat cycles, sourced as the Lagrangian of the KPI 6 constraint (Altman 1999; Paternain et al. 2019; Tessler et al. 2019); scored in P1R, a candidate in profile 3. The reason "a reward redesign without a source" is answered by the citation.]*

**Where the fix goes.** Profile 3: a commit on top of TIP3 before profile 3's queue starts, counted as its tested tip; the choice is made at profile 3's own C1d on its validation flights and read in its C2e on clean test pools no earlier step has read. P1R: a profile 1 decision-layer rerun after CORE_BW (C1d, C2, C2e with the fixed monitor, on a fresh clean test set); its lecturer readings are reported only as "with the fixed monitor, second reading, descriptive", and C2e's stay the verdicts. The order of P1R and profile 3 is to be confirmed by Adi (default: P1R first). Not in CORE_BW (it keeps profile 1's monitor, so CORE_BW and C2e stay on one monitor) and not in the running profile 1. Optional cheap evidence before profile 3 freezes (PC8, about 1 h, validation only): C1d's whole-monitor analysis with F1-F3 on profile 1's validation episodes, reported as a design check.

**Rejected.** Stopping C2 to fix and rerun C1d-C2e (about 2-3 days, a monitor changed under time pressure, no effect on the lecturer's FAR verdict already met on the detector); rerunning only C2e with a new monitor (the DQN is trained on the old one); reporting KPI 6 without 0 and 3 dB or redefining "false alarm" after reading (gaming; D99 points 2 and 5 keep the pooled verdict); bringing the fix into CORE_BW (a protocol change after C2e's comparison is known).

**Why these lines and not more.** They answer the three questions the report must answer:
- Is the lecturer verdict biased, by the pre-onset changes or by FA-constrained rule settings? (point 4)
- What drives KPI 6? (point 3)
- Is the miss a threat-detection miss or a link-quality response? (point 3c)

Each reads saved data once. None can move a verdict.

**Sources.**
- Xu et al. 2005: consistency of delivery ratio with signal strength or interference.
- Kazim 2026: power plus quality, threshold from interference-free samples.
- López-Benítez 2016 and Grimaldi 2019: CFAR from the noise power alone.
- Norouzi 2006: k-of-n under independent samples.
- Xie 2021 and 2022: ARL, window-limited CUSUM with a known pre-change law.
- Barajas 2026: false triggers inflate recovery; band-specific thresholds.
- Thulin 2014: Clopper-Pearson.

## D104 — P1R, profile 1's decision-layer rerun with the fixed monitor: order, isolated worktree, archive, stop rule, fresh clean test set for KPI 6, labels, smoke-only forced cells and the F0 trigger, registered before the smoke and any P1R data

**Why (26.10, during profile 1's C2).** D103 part 2 registered the degradation path's fix for profile 3 and for a profile 1 rerun (P1R) and left P1R's place in the order to Adi. P1R reads a monitor that profile 1's first C2e does not have, on test pools that C2e has already read, so its design, its fresh set and its labels are fixed now, before the smoke, before P1R's C1d and before any data they govern exists. Protocol `docs/p1r_protocol.md` on `lect-kpi` (`e170c8e`, up to `316230d`; not pushed); the design is the session review `core/p1r_design.md` (read only, nothing run; not in this repo). No P1R run, no smoke of it and no profile 1 C2e result existed when this was decided.

**Decision.**
1. **Order.** Profile 1's chain (C2, C2e and the tail to RPT, untouched) → CORE_BW → P1R → profile 3. D103 left P1R before or after profile 3 to Adi; no objection came within 30 min on 26.10, so his default stands: P1R first. P1R itself, in this order: (a) copy the inputs and check them with `sha256sum -c`; (b) C1d with `deg_select`, then its review; (c) the stop rule of point 4; (d) the fresh clean test set; (e) C2, whose grid line and first two run times re-date profile 3 at once; (f) C2e with `"kpi6_set": "fresh"`; (g) `system_fa_breakdown` once on P1R's outputs, then the review and the report lines. Profile 3's queue is released after P1R's C2e and its review; nothing of profile 3 runs beside P1R (31.2 GB of physical RAM). No run is shortened. *[Amended 2026-10-29 by D108 (approved by Adi, before any P1R data): see "D104 amendment" under D108.]* *[Amended 30.10.2026 by D110 (registered under decide-after-30min, Adi silent since 30.10.2026 19:37): beside P1R's C2, and never beside its C1d, fresh build, C2e, `link_fresh` or breakdown, one profile 3 hand-run at a time may run in the p3 slot. The allowed runs are D110's single-thread measurement on scratch copies, the D110-D117 branch tests, and K2-1 (D111). Each starts only when three conditions hold: free commit ≥ max(35, peak + 10) GB; free physical memory ≥ its peak + 4 GB; and P1R's C2, projected from its per-run times, still has at least the job's upper estimate + 2 h to run. A running job is stopped (from its checkpoint where it has one) if P1R's C2 time per run rises above 1.10 × the mean of its first two runs. Profile 3's chain stages still never run beside P1R.]* *[Amended 31.10.2026 (registered under decide-after-30min, Adi silent since 30.10.2026 19:37): the D3 dispatch of `rx_fix_rule3.md` (tests, fresh pool, D3; link-floor, not a profile run) may also run beside P1R's C2 under this line's three conditions and stop rule, one hand-run at a time.]* *[Clarified 31.10.2026 (registered under decide-after-30min, Adi silent since 30.10.2026 19:37), before any P1R data: "the mean of its first two runs" (T_ref) is measured from C2's run cache (`data/run_cache/C2`, one file per finished run) as (t3 − t1)/2, where t1 and t3 are the file times of the first and third finished runs, that is the mean of runs 2 and 3. Run 1's file time also holds C2's setup readings before run 1 (the rule settings and the 8 or 12 monitor readings), and C2's log prints no per-run time. Side runs therefore start only when C2 has n ≥ 3 finished runs. C2's grid size N in the third condition is the count of `=== Monitor` lines at C2's start × 9 (72 or 108); the log has no single grid line. The same two readings serve (e) and point 11. A side run is also stopped as soon as P1R's `uav-gcs-logs/p1r/stage.txt` no longer reads `C2 running`, so it never runs beside C2e, `link_fresh` or the breakdown, and P1R waits for it only about a minute.]*
2. **Where it runs: an isolated worktree, never W.** `C:\Users\Adi Suliman\uav-gcs-v7-p1r`, branch `p1r` from `d4623ed` (profile 1's code of record). It takes the degradation fix `c7fe8f9` and `fcdc199` as cherry-picks (with `docs/deg_fix_protocol.md` and its tests); `sysfa` (`4beefb7`: `system_fa_breakdown.m`, its tests, `docs/system_fa_protocol.md`) as a merge; and the P1R commits of `lect-kpi`, as the protocol lists them, as cherry-picks: `e170c8e` (the protocol), `69cbc18` (the fresh set), `48f120a` (the forced candidate), `f28a19a` (C1d refuses a forced test before it reads or writes anything), `316230d` (the smoke's layout). In `build_clean_test_pools.m` and `test_core.m` the context of these commits includes `lect-kpi` lines that `d4623ed` does not hold (the coded link's decoder), so those two hunks are applied by hand and the tests rerun. Written on `p1r` with their tests before the smoke: `kpi6_set` in `evaluate_policies.m`, `KP.far_set` in `system_fa_breakdown.m`, and `profile.json` = `{"profile": "profile 1, P1R", "deg_select": true, "kpi6_set": "fresh"}`. Not taken: `core-bw-p1` (CORE_BW's change to the core rule is not part of P1R; its `blind_core` stays profile 1's) and the rest of `lect-kpi` (the JSI decoder, `surv_v2`), so P1R differs from profile 1 in the monitor and in the KPI 6 reading only. `v7-dev` is never written. Profile 1's tree (`uav-gcs-v7`, W) is only read; P1R writes only in its own tree and in `uav-gcs-logs/p1r/`. *[Amended 2026-10-29 by D108 (approved by Adi, before any P1R data): see "D104 amendment" under D108.]* *[Amended 31.10.2026, path only (Adi's folder rule; the runbook's path amendment of 27.10.2026), registered under decide-after-30min, Adi silent since 30.10.2026 19:37: `C:\Users\Adi Suliman\uav-gcs-v7-p1r` is now `C:\Users\Adi Suliman\uav-gcs-adaptive-secure-comms\worktrees\p1r` (P1R's run tree) and the smoke worktree `uav-gcs-p1r-smoke` of point 8 is now `C:\Users\Adi Suliman\uav-gcs-adaptive-secure-comms\worktrees\p1r-smoke`; no other word of this decision changes.]*
   - Runs go through the gated wrapper only (no `run_stage(`), in the p1 slot through the empty `V7G_ROOT` stand-in, one MATLAB at a time at 45 GB.
   - Inputs copied from W after its RPT, hashed in W and checked in P1R (about 22.7 GB): `params.mat`; `data/policy_pools.mat`; `data/clean_val_pools.mat` (C1d's fit and check halves, C2's false-alarm filter); `data/clean_test_pools.mat` (C2e's union line, descriptive); `data/trained_detector.mat`, `data/fusion.mat`, `data/ood_thresholds.mat` (the fresh build's detector check); `data/splits.mat` (its training z-score); `results/eval_detector_metrics.mat` (the KPI 1 threshold). `data/drop_threshold.mat` and `data/trained_dqn.mat` are not copied: P1R writes its own.
3. **Archive of profile 1's first reading.** At `STAGE C2e done` and again after RPT, profile 1's first-chain outputs are copied (`cp -p`) with a `sha256sum` manifest (`MANIFEST.sha256`) to `C:\Users\Adi Suliman\uav-gcs-logs\p1_first\` and set read-only. At C2e: C1d's `data/drop_threshold.mat` and `results/drop_threshold.txt`; C2's `data/trained_dqn.mat`, `results/dqn_training.txt` and `results/dqn_training_curves.png`; C2e's `results/policy_evaluation.{txt,mat,png}` and `results/policy_breakdown.png`; C1d's stage log and p1.log's part. After RPT: the tail's outputs (C2g, C2u, GAL, EDGE, LAT, `results/kpi_summary.{txt,mat}`, DASH, WEAK, RPT) and the `system_fa_breakdown` reading copy. The manifest's hash goes into PROJECT_LOG before P1R's C1d. W's own final phase reads these first-chain files later, so the archive at RPT is the record of the first reading.
4. **C1d and the stop rule.** C1d runs with `deg_select`: it reads every candidate (F1, F2 and F3, each at alpha 1e-2 and 1e-3 where it has one) beside `'ratio'` on the check half and the validation threats, and adopts by `deg_adopt.m` under D103 part 2's adoption rule, fit/check split and tests, unchanged. **If C1d keeps `'ratio'` (no candidate admissible), P1R ends after C1d.** The fresh set is not built and stays unread for F4 (D103's registered next step), and the report says so. Profile 3 then starts about two days earlier. C2 trains on the adopted monitor. *[Applied 31.10.2026 08:52 (P1R C1d at p1r `2a4d19b`, after the smoke's PASS): no candidate admissible, `'ratio'` kept, so P1R ended after C1d; the fresh clean test set is not built and stays unread for F4. Every confirmation's whole monitor breaks KPI 6's 5% bound through its other paths alone (clean validation episodes with a false change and no drop alarm: 161-484 of 1800, bounds 10.1-28.7%), so no drop threshold or degradation form can meet it in profile 1; the sweeper's own confirmation is adopted. A' (D107) goes to profile 3 only (D107 point 6); profile 3's class-path answer is D113. Log `uav-gcs-logs/p1r/driver_20261031_084440.log`, `worktrees/p1r/results/drop_threshold.txt`.]*
5. **The fresh clean test set (KPI 6 only).** Geometry families 7, 8 and 9 of seed block 15, 100 geometries each, laid out as the clean sets are (`clean_set_layout.m`, `clean_pool_seed.m`, whose families argument defaults to `[1 3 5]`, so the validation and test sets keep their seeds bit for bit). *[Amended 2026-10-29 by D108 (approved by Adi, before any P1R data): see "D104 amendment" under D108.]*
   - *Seeds* 1 941 501 to 1 986 600. No commit has flown these families, `pool_seed_owner.m` lists them as this set's alone, and they are disjoint from every pool, clean-link, check, bandwidth-study, survivability, dataset and shadowing seed (`test_seed_streams_disjoint`, `test_band_seeds_disjoint`); below 2 000 000 and 2^26, their streams below 2^32. Blocks 4 and 15 under families 1, 3 and 5 (the clean validation and test sets) are never reused: the layout stops on any pair another set owns.
   - *Size.* 300 geometries per Eb/N0 point × 6 points (0 to 15 dB, all at or above the KPI 1 threshold of 0 dB) = **1800 independent clean episodes**, each with its own seed. A one-sided 95% Clopper-Pearson upper bound of at most 5% allows **at most 74** false-alarm episodes (74 of 1800 gives 4.97%, 75 gives 5.03%).
   - *Build.* `CLEAN_SET = 'fresh'` in `build_clean_test_pools.m` writes `data/clean_fresh_pools.mat` with `CT.set = 'fresh'` and `CT.fams = [7 8 9]`, in the other clean sets' recipe (detector, frames, features, every configuration, the per-flight draws kept); about 2.0-2.3 h on 6 workers at 45 GB, never beside C2. The chain's C1c never builds it.
   - *Built only after adoption.* `clean_fresh_guard.m` stops the build unless `data/drop_threshold.mat` holds C1d's `DEG` with a candidate adopted (`pick > 0`) and no forced mark. The smoke skips this check.
   - *The smoke flies other seeds.* Under `SMOKE` the fresh build takes families 7-9 of block 18, the smoke's own block (runs 1801-1804), so no smoke ever flies a fresh-set flight.
   - *Read once,* in P1R's C2e. No earlier step and no smoke reads it. *[Amended 2026-10-29 by D108 (approved by Adi, before any P1R data): see "D104 amendment" under D108.]*
6. **KPI 6 (our extension): the verdict on the fresh set alone.** D103 point 2's definition and bound, per policy: one clean episode per geometry and Eb/N0 point at or above the KPI 1 threshold, one-sided 95% Clopper-Pearson upper bound at most 5%. `evaluate_policies.m` with `"kpi6_set": "fresh"` loads `data/clean_fresh_pools.mat` (detector check, the test split), flies it under every policy (`RWf`, split id 7) and stops if the file is absent: it never falls back to the already-read union. `KP.far` and `KP.far_diag` are `far_report` on `RWf` alone; `KP.far_set = 'fresh'`, `KP.far_src` names the set, `KP.reading = 'P1R'`; `RWf` is saved. The old union (the test splits' clean flights and `clean_test_pools.mat`) is `KP.far_union`, printed under "already read in profile 1's C2e: second reading, descriptive". Without the key `evaluate_policies.m` is unchanged bit for bit. `system_fa_breakdown.m` takes `KP.far_set`: under 'fresh' its KPI 6 flights are `RWf`, its exact gate reproduces P1R's `KP.far`, and it runs once on P1R's outputs with D103 points 3-5 labelled P1R.
7. **Labels, and what stays the verdict.** *[Amended 2026-10-29 by D108 (approved by Adi, before any P1R data): see "D104 amendment" under D108.]*
   - Head of P1R's `policy_evaluation.txt`: "P1R: profile 1's decision layer with the degradation test adopted at C1d (`<deg_mode>`). The lecturer readings and every test-split line are with the fixed monitor, second reading, descriptive; profile 1's C2e keeps the verdicts. KPI 6 (our extension): verdict on the fresh clean test set (families 7-9, block 15), read once."
   - Every lecturer line of P1R (`lect_kpis.m`): "with the fixed monitor, second reading, descriptive".
   - Report line: "Profile 1 with the fixed degradation test (P1R, `<mode>`, chosen on validation): system-level false alarms on fresh clean flights X% [upper Y%], MET / NOT MET".
   - The verdicts stay with profile 1's first C2e: every lecturer KPI verdict and every test-split reading, and profile 1's first KPI 6 line, worded as D103 point 7 fixes it. P1R's KPI 6 line is reported beside it, never in its place.
   - EDGE is not rerun in P1R: its envelope would be a second reading of test pools. D103 point 8 stays with the first chain.
8. **The smoke's forced candidates (smoke only, never an adoption).** `deg_force` (`profile.json` or `CFG.deg_force`) names one of `'ratio'`, `'win'`, `'win_q:0.01'`, `'win_q:0.001'`, `'win_q_gate:0.01'`, `'win_q_gate:0.001'` (`deg_force.m`); any other value stops. C1d still reads every candidate and runs `deg_adopt.m`, then deploys the named one: `DEG.mode`, `DEG.name` and `DEG.ber_q` are the forced candidate's, `DEG.forced` holds its name, `DEG.rule_pick` the name the rule adopted, `DEG.pick` stays the rule's index, and the report adds "Degradation test FORCED (smoke only, not an adoption): ...; the rule would adopt ...". C2 prints the mark and carries it in `dec.deg_forced`, which enters the policy stamp, so a forced DQN never carries an adopted one's stamp. C1d stops on a forced value outside `SMOKE` (and without `deg_select` or clean validation flights), before it reads or writes anything; C2 stops on a forced `drop_threshold.mat` and C2e on a forced `trained_dqn.mat` outside `SMOKE`; the fresh build stops on a forced C1d file. Without the switch C1d, C2, C2e and the stamp are unchanged. *[Amended 2026-10-29 by D108 (approved by Adi, before any P1R data): see "D104 amendment" under D108.]*
   - Where: a smoke worktree `uav-gcs-p1r-smoke` at the tested `p1r` tip, inputs copied (never written) from `d91q/deploy2/smoke` (profile 1's deploy smoke, whose code equals `d4623ed`'s); each command `SMOKE = true; [CFG.deg_force = '<name>';] run('<script>')`.
   - Cell A (default): C1d's adoption, the fresh build (4 geometries, block 18), C2, C2e with `kpi6_set` fresh, `system_fa_breakdown`. Checks: six candidate readings in `drop_threshold.txt`; `CT.set` fresh and `CT.fams`; `KP.far_set`; the breakdown's gate passes on the fresh definition.
   - Five forced cells (F1; F2 at 1e-2 and 1e-3; F3 at 1e-2 and 1e-3): C1d forced, C2, C2e. Checks: the FORCED line; `dec.deg_mode` and `ber_q` are the forced candidate's; a distinct stamp per cell; for F3, `R.deg_quiet` and C1d's quiet line; C2e ends. *[Smoke note 31.10.2026 (registered under decide-after-30min, Adi silent since 30.10.2026 19:37), smoke only: cell A's C2e reads the smoke's fresh set once, and its marker then refuses every later fresh reading (`kpi6_fresh_marker:read`, the protocol's 27.10 "read once, enforced" amendment). The forced cells therefore run with `CFG.kpi6_set = 'union'` after cell A's single fresh read (`kpi6_set.m` reads `CFG` first; no code changes); their checks do not read KPI 6. P1R's real run reads the fresh set once, in its C2e, with `profile.json`'s `"kpi6_set": "fresh"`.]*
   - Refusals: the same commands without `SMOKE`, on a scratch copy (C1d forced, C2 on a forced file, the fresh build without an adoption, `kpi6_set` without the fresh file): each stops.
   - The smoke's quantile tables are flagged `short` (2 fit geometries): expected in the smoke, no gate. Cap 2 h; about 1-1.5 h from the deploy smoke's stage times.
9. **F0 trigger (read on profile 1's first C2e, before P1R's C1d).** Tested on the deployed DQN's clean episodes of the KPI 6 union (D103 point 3e). The per-cycle hazard h of a first false change is estimated on cycles `C.win` to T of the episodes with no false change before cycle `C.win` (first changes there over the cycles at risk there). With n episodes, the count X of first false changes in cycles 1 to `C.win` − 1 is compared with Binomial(n, 1 − (1 − h)^(`C.win` − 1)). The false changes are **front-loaded** when P(X ≥ observed) < 0.05 (one-sided). A share above 4/30 alone is not the reference: a stationary first-passage law is front-loaded by itself. If front-loaded, F0 (D103 part 2) joins ahead of F1, with the code, tests and smoke cell it needs (`link_env.m`'s episode start; about 0.5-1 day) done before P1R's C1d: the code written during CORE_BW's pools, its tests and smoke only when the gate allows (not during CORE_BW's pools or dset). If not, F0 is not a candidate.
10. **Tests, before the smoke.** `test_core`: `test_seed_streams_disjoint` and `test_band_seeds_disjoint` (the fresh seeds and their smoke block 18's: unique, below 2 000 000, disjoint from every other seed, streams unique; family 6 against the fresh set), `test_clean_pool_draws` (the families argument; 'test' and 'val' give the parent commit's seeds and speeds for every geometry of blocks 4 and 15; geometries outside 1-300 stop), `test_clean_fresh_guard` (the three layouts and the smoke's block 18 with 'test' and 'val' unmoved; the owner table; a fresh layout on a taken pair stops; the guard in every state; the builder stops before any load without an adoption; only the fresh set saves `CT.fams`; C1c never builds it). `test_deg_mode`: `test_force_values`, `test_force_needs_smoke`, `test_force_sets_mode`, `test_switch_off_keeps_outputs`, `test_settings_and_stamp`. Written on `p1r` with the code: the tests of `kpi6_set` (unchanged without the key, the fresh file with it, the absent file, the labels) and of the breakdown on both KPI 6 definitions on synthetic data. Then `test_core` once on the tip. Not run here (no MATLAB in this pass); they run in the p3 slot stand-in during profile 1's tail, after CORE_BW's tests and smoke. *[Amended 2026-10-29 by D108 (approved by Adi, before any P1R data): see "D104 amendment" under D108.]*
11. **Time and profile 3's date.** About 44-49 h at C2's 72 runs, 57-64 h at 108 runs (108 if the fixed monitor moves C1d's CUSUM pick off the top of its grid; C2's 72 is derived from the code), plus an unmeasured factor from the wrapper's single-thread MATLAB (`-singleCompThread`), read from C2's first two runs. By step: C1d about 0.3-1 h, the fresh build 2.0-2.3 h, C2 38-42 h at 72 runs and 51-57 h at 108, C2e 1.5-2.5 h, the breakdown 0.5-1 h. C2's grid line and its first two run times are read at once and Adi is told profile 3's new date. Profile 3's end moves from about 16-18.11 to **about 18-21.11** (worst about 22-23.11, at 108 runs with a single-thread slowdown). Under the stop rule P1R ends after C1d and profile 3 starts about two days earlier. *[Amended 2026-10-29 by D108 (approved by Adi, before any P1R data): see "D104 amendment" under D108.]*

**Rejected.** P1R in W itself, or the fix merged into `v7-dev` and run in W: P1R's stages write `data/drop_threshold.mat`, `data/trained_dqn.mat` and `results/policy_evaluation.*`, which the final phase's KPI summary, EDGE, report numbers, weak-point analysis, dashboard, latency and `result_status` read; the code of record would change under the final phase, C2g, C2u, EDGE and LAT would stay stamped to a DQN that no longer exists, and the first reading would survive only as copies. A whole `lect-kpi` merge into P1R (the JSI decoder `f328e7b` and `surv_v2` come with it, so P1R would differ from profile 1 by more than the monitor). KPI 6 on the already-read union as P1R's verdict (read by profile 1's first C2e; not a fresh reading). Building the fresh set before the adoption, or leaving it built when `'ratio'` is kept (it stays unread for F4). Rerunning EDGE in P1R. Judging F0 by a share above 4/30 alone. A forced candidate outside the smoke. Shortening any run.

**Sources.** As D103: Thulin 2014 (Clopper-Pearson; the 74 of 1800 is exact); Xie 2021 (FAR = 1/ARL, D103 3d; the F0 test's reference is a constant per-cycle hazard, the stationary first-passage law). `lect-kpi`: `docs/p1r_protocol.md` (`e170c8e`, up to `316230d`), `docs/deg_fix_protocol.md` (`c7fe8f9`), `docs/system_fa_protocol.md` (`4beefb7`). The design: session scratchpad `interim_report/core/p1r_design.md`, not in this repo.

## D105 — The lecturer's verdict pairs: rule_core against none and blind_core on the core action set; rule_esc and blind_esc an extension; C2e's blind_core a secondary, weakened baseline; KPI 8's headline from CORE_BW; the PER verdict on Line A; written before C2e's test reading

**Why (2026-10-26, 21:50 +02:00; gap audit M4 and M3).** Profile 1's C2e evaluates two CNN / conventional pairs:
- `rule_esc` / `blind_esc` on the 36 configurations;
- `rule_core` / `blind_core` on the core action set.

Three documents name the pairs differently:
- D92 and the README map conventional = `blind_esc` and CNN-based = `rule_esc`;
- `c2e_lect_protocol.md` 6 lists both pairs;
- `lect_kpis.m` prints the word "PRIMARY" in both blocks (`L.verdict` and `L.core.verdict`).

The baseline also differs in tuning budget. In C2e, `blind_core`'s first response is fixed. In CORE_BW (D98 a), it is chosen on validation. This entry fixes, before any C2e test reading exists, which pair carries each lecturer verdict and what is secondary.

*State when written.* `uav-gcs-logs/v7_d86/p1.log` (20 939 lines, last written 2026-10-26 21:46 +02:00) last shows `##### STAGE C2 (train_dqn) 26-Oct-2026 16:03:02`, with no done line. C2 is training (episode 50048/50048 of one run). There is no `STAGE C2e` line after it. `W/results` holds no `policy_evaluation.*`. **STAGE C2e has not started; no C2e reading exists.**

**Facts from the code (read only).** W is `v7-dev` `d4623ed`. `uav-gcs-lectkpi` is `lect-kpi` `316230d`.
1. *`lect_kpis.m`* is the same in both trees, apart from line endings. The lecturer KPIs below are numbered in D92's list order: 5 PER, 6 recovery time, 7 goodput, 8 the CNN's contribution. The project's own KPI 6 (system false alarms, D103/D104) is a different item.
   - **Full action set.** `L.verdict` holds the PER-reduction verdict against the static link (`none`) for `rule_esc`. It is read per lecturer scenario: narrowband = `tone_jamming`, wideband = `jamming`. The episodes are those where some configuration of the 36 restores the geometry.
     - `L.per`: the five jamming threats pooled, an extension.
     - `L.cnn`: `rule_esc` against `blind_esc`.
     - `L.rec`: recovery times for `none`, `blind_esc`, `rule_esc` and the DQN.
     - `L.gput`: goodput in bit/s for the same policies.
   - **Core action set.** `L.core` holds the same readings for `rule_core` and `blind_core` against the same `none`.
     - The mask is `recoverable_core`, the episodes some core configuration restores.
     - `L.core.all36` is the second line, on the 36-configuration mask.
     - The CNN's share is `rule_core` against `blind_core`.
2. *`evaluate_policies.m` (C2e, line 173-183)* runs `none`, `random`, `fixed_mmse`, `fixed`, `rule`, `rule_esc`, `blind_esc`, `table`, the DQNs `dqn_g*`, `dqn_esc`, (`rule_sel`) and `oracle`. It then appends `rule_core` and `blind_core` on the same episodes. It calls `lect_kpis` once (line 384) and saves `KP.lect`.
3. *`rule_core`* is `rule_esc` with `opt.core` set (`policy_setup.m:23`). It does **not** read B3c's three-state CNN. Its class is the policy monitor's class (`policy_monitor.m:160-167`): the hybrid detector's 11 classes from `data/trained_detector.mat` (10 threats and `none`), temporally fused, with the unknown-threat flag. `rule_core_policy.m` maps that class, together with link metrics, onto `C.core_actions` (`decision_config.m:54`). The link metrics are degradation, the drop alarm, the quiet-slot interference and the link budget.
   - `tone_jamming`, `jamming`, `reactive_jamming` and `spoofing` map to `channel_switch`.
   - `sweeping_jammer`, `noise_burst`, `path_loss`, `antenna_fault` and `airframe_shadowing` map to `rate_reduce`.
   - A degraded link without a known cause maps to `channel_switch+rate_reduce`.
   - Escalation runs over `C.ladder_core` = `channel_switch+rate_reduce` → `rate_reduce` → `channel_switch` (`decision_config.m:55`).
   - In C2e, `PP.core_wide` is not set, so the class `jamming` keeps `channel_switch`.
   - `PP.core_nb` (the class `noise_burst`) is chosen on validation in C2 (`train_dqn.m:196-233`).
   - No decision-layer file in W, `uav-gcs-lectkpi`, `uav-gcs-v7-bw` or `uav-gcs-v7-p3` calls the three-state CNN. It is read only by B3c (`train_core_cnn.m`, `results/core_cnn.*`) and by CORE_BW's analysis stage `dread` on set D (`experiment_core_bw.m:337-355`). CORE_BW's own `rule_core` also reads the hybrid (`experiment_core_bw.m` `stage_pools` loads `data/trained_detector.mat`).
4. *`blind_core`* is `blind_esc` with `opt.core` set (`policy_setup.m:24`). `policy_decide.m:79-82` zeroes the class probabilities, sets the unknown flag and sets `unk_mode = 'all'`, so every cycle reads `unknown`. Its alarm therefore comes from measured degradation only. For `unknown` on a degraded link, `rule_core_policy.m:74` gives `channel_switch+rate_reduce`. A confirmed drop alarm with a quiet slot gives `rate_reduce`. The escalation ladder also starts at `channel_switch+rate_reduce`.
   - Its first response is therefore fixed: a 4x rate cut on every incident, with no validation choice.
   - It runs the same deployed decision settings as `rule_core` (dwell, monitor, `esc_drop`).
   - On `core-bw-p1` (`ac55080`, `0e0cc7a`, `79286f5`; D98 a), CORE_BW chooses `blind_core`'s first response on validation per hop rule. The candidates are `channel_switch`, `channel_switch+rate_reduce` and `rate_reduce`, under protocol 6.3's rule, the same rule as the rule's W. The fixed ladder stays there as the secondary row `blind_core_fixed`.

**Decision.**
1. **The lecturer's verdict pairs are on the core action set.** The proposal maps:
   - static = no action;
   - conventional adaptive = link metrics only;
   - CNN-based adaptive = the CNN's class plus rule-based channel switching and rate adaptation.

   The pairs follow that mapping as closely as the code allows:

   | Lecturer KPI | Carried by | Reading |
   |---|---|---|
   | 5, PER reduction ≥ 30% vs static | `rule_core` against `none` | `KP.lect.core.verdict`: narrowband (`tone_jamming`) and wideband (`jamming`), each on its own, on `recoverable_core`; MET (committed) / MET (not committed) / NOT MET as `lect_kpis.m` words it |
   | 6, recovery time (detection → restored; onset → detection apart) | `rule_core`, with `blind_core` and `none` beside it | `KP.lect.core.rec`, measured and compared, no target |
   | 7, goodput | `rule_core`, with `blind_core` and `none` beside it | `KP.lect.core.gput` in bit/s, measured, no target |
   | 8, the CNN's contribution | `rule_core` against `blind_core` | analysis, no promise; headline from CORE_BW (point 2) |

   - **Extension rows, never in the verdict's place:**
     - *[Amended 31.10.2026 by D121 (approved by Adi, 31.10.2026; profile 3 only); also P1-v2: a paired onset-referenced recovery difference (t_conf + t_rec_det) in `lect_kpis.m`'s CN block, descriptive (D121 item 10, P-C); KPI 6's registered reading is unchanged.]*
     - `rule_esc` / `blind_esc` on the 36 configurations (`KP.lect.verdict`, `.per`, `.cnn`, `.rec`, `.gput`): the full action set the proposal does not ask for;
     - the five jamming threats pooled;
     - the core set on the 36-configuration mask (`KP.lect.core.all36`, second line);
     - the DQN.
   - `lect_kpis.m` is not changed, and it still prints "PRIMARY" in the full-set block. The report and the result sentences label that block "extension (full action set)".
   - This supersedes D92's mapping clause (conventional = `blind_esc`, CNN-based = `rule_esc`) for the verdicts. Those two remain the extension pair.
2. **The conventional baseline: C2e's `blind_core` is a secondary, weakened baseline.**
   - C2e's `blind_core` has no tuning budget. Its first response is the fixed `channel_switch+rate_reduce`. The rule side, in contrast, has its noise-burst answer and the deployed settings chosen on validation (past bug 4, unfair baselines).
   - Its rows in C2e, and in P1R and profile 3 where the same code runs, are labelled "secondary, weakened baseline (fixed first response channel_switch+rate_reduce, no validation choice)". KPI 7 and KPI 8 readings against it are reported only with that label. Part of a goodput gain over it is the baseline's 4x rate cut, not the classification.
   - **The headline KPI 8** is CORE_BW's `rule_core` against its validation-tuned `blind_core` (D98 a, `core-bw-p1`): equal tuning budget, read under `core_bw_protocol.md` 6.5. CORE_BW's `blind_core_fixed` row is secondary there too.
   - CORE_BW's own per-type PER verdicts (`rule_core` against `none` on its controlled cells) stay its own (protocol 6.5) and are reported beside C2e's KPI 5 verdict as the controlled-bandwidth reading. Neither replaces the other.
   - If CORE_BW has not been read when a report is written, KPI 8's headline is "pending (CORE_BW)", and C2e's reading appears only with the secondary label.
   - The tuned first response is not ported into pre-registered trees (`p1r`, `TIP3`) unless Adi registers it there before their data.
3. **The PER verdict stays Line A (D99, decided 2026-10-25).**
   - KPI 5's verdict words come from `lect_kpis.m` on all six Eb/N0 points pooled. Within that, the verdict is per lecturer scenario, as in point 1.
   - Line B (the non-link-limited points) and the per-Eb/N0 rows (`lect_kpis_by_ebno.m`, D99 point 3) are printed beside it, in D99's order, for the core pair and the extension alike. When Lines A and B differ in verdict words, the result sentence names both, and Line A stays the verdict.
   - The report states that Line A pools link-limited points.
     - By D99 point 1 on the post-M clean table (D99 update: clean loss 0.99 / 0.79 / 0.42 at 0 / 3 / 6 dB), the link-limited points are 0 and 3 dB. 6 dB and 9 dB are marginal (M).
     - The gap audit's "0 / 3 / 6 dB" uses the old table (0.99 / 0.84 / 0.62). The mark computed and hashed before `policy_evaluation.mat` is opened decides, and the report uses that mark's words.
   - Recovery time from detection keeps D99 point 4: its headline is Line B, and Line A is shown with the count of episodes set by construction. Goodput is read on Line A (D99: unbiased).
4. **Timing.** This entry is committed before any C2e test reading exists (state above). Nothing in it depends on a C2e, CORE_BW, P1R or profile 3 result.

**What C2e can and cannot deliver.**
- *It can deliver:* the lecturer's KPI 5-7 on the proposal's core action set (channel switching and rate adaptation), and a KPI 8 reading against a link-metrics-only baseline. The rule acts on a detector class and link metrics, as the proposal's rule does ("from the class and the link metrics", D92).
- *It cannot deliver:*
  - **a closed loop driven by the three-state CNN.** "CNN-based adaptive" in C2e (and in CORE_BW) is the hybrid detector: the spectrogram CNN with link features, 11 classes, temporal fusion and the unknown-threat flag. `rule_core_policy.m` maps its classes onto the core actions. In the two lecturer scenarios, the classes `tone_jamming` and `jamming` get what a three-state rule would give (`channel_switch`, or W in CORE_BW). The two differ when the hybrid names another class, for example `noise_burst` or `sweeping_jammer` → `rate_reduce`, or `unknown` on a degraded link → `channel_switch+rate_reduce`.
  - **a fair KPI 8 against a tuned conventional baseline** (point 2).
- *Where the lecturer-faithful readings come from:*
  - the three-state CNN's own detection KPIs (macro-F1, recall per jamming type, clean false alarms): B3c (`results/core_cnn.*`, its single reading of the fourth test set);
  - the hybrid seen as three states: `three_state_view.m` on the saved KPI 1 reading;
  - the CNN's contribution with an equal tuning budget: CORE_BW.

  No run puts the three-state CNN in the decision loop. The report says KPI 5-8 are read with the hybrid detector's classes on the core action set, and it does not attribute them to the three-state CNN.

**Rejected.**
- `rule_esc` / `blind_esc` as the lecturer pair: the 36 configurations go beyond the proposal's two actions.
- C2e's fixed-ladder `blind_core` as KPI 8's headline: unequal tuning budget.
- Re-registering the PER verdict on Line B: D99 pre-registered Line A, and the direction of the link-limited points' effect is not known before the reading.
- Changing `lect_kpis.m`'s labels in W: W is frozen while the chain runs, and the labels are fixed in the report instead.
- Calling KPI 5-8 the three-state CNN's: no code path supports that.

**Open.**
- README lines 44-45 (the configuration table) and 101 still map conventional = `blind_esc`, CNN-based = `rule_esc`. They are to be aligned with point 1.
- A closed-loop row driven by the three-state CNN does not exist. Adding one is new work: code, tests, a registration before its data and a test-read rule. It is for Adi to decide; nothing here schedules it.
- CORE_BW's pools, validation and test run after profile 1's end (D98 a).

**Sources.** Lecturer's revised proposal of 2026-10-20 (D92): the three configurations, KPIs 5-8, and the CNN's contribution as an analysis metric. D98 a. D99. `plan_v2.md` risk 8 ("The fair comparison is CORE_BW's"). `quality_checklist.md` finding 5 and PC4. Gap audit M3 and M4 (session scratchpad `interim_report/core/gap_audit.md`, not in this repo). Code: W `d4623ed`: `lect_kpis.m`, `evaluate_policies.m`, `policy_decide.m`, `policy_setup.m`, `policy_monitor.m`, `decision_config.m`, `rule_core_policy.m`, `experiment_core_bw.m`. `core-bw-p1` `ac55080`.

## D106 — rule_core3: the proposal's three-state CNN drives the core rule in CORE_BW and carries its lecturer verdicts; rule_core (the hybrid) a secondary row; KPI 8's headline amended from rule_core to rule_core3; registered before the smoke and any CORE_BW data

**Why (2026-10-27; Adi approved on 27.10).** D105 left a closed-loop row driven by the three-state CNN open, as new work for Adi to decide. In every closed-loop study so far the core rule reads the hybrid detector's 11 classes; B3c's three-state CNN (spectrogram; clean, narrowband, wideband) was read only open-loop. The proposal's CNN-based system is that CNN plus rules. CORE_BW's threat set is exactly the lecturer's two states (CW and 4-83.5 MHz, `core_bw_protocol.md` section 3), its conventional row is validation-tuned (D98 a), and none of its data exists. It can therefore carry the literal system now, at small cost: about 1-1.5 code days and 2-4 more run hours. Analysis: session scratchpad `interim_report/core/core_cnn_loop_decision.md` (read only; not in this repo).

*State when written.* `core-bw-p1` was at `6ef3693`, clean. `uav-gcs-v7-bw` holds no `results/core_bw`, no `results/core_bw_smoke` and no `data` folder, so no CORE_BW pools, smoke output, `protocol.json` or test marker exists. No log of a CORE_BW stage (smoke, pools, dset, val, test) exists in `uav-gcs-logs` or in the p3 slot's logs; the only CORE_BW logs there are `test_core` runs. Profile 1's `p1.log` shows `STAGE C2 (train_dqn)` running and no `STAGE C2e` line. **No CORE_BW data exists.**

**Facts from the code (read only; W `d4623ed`, `core-bw-p1` `6ef3693`).**
- The pools keep no spectrogram or IQ. `pool_cell.m` `add_run` builds the image with `spec_image`, scores it with the hybrid (`detect_scores`) and stores `probs`, `maha` and `feat`; the loop sees only these (`link_env.m`: `obs.probs`, `obs.unknown`). CORE_BW's pools load `data/trained_detector.mat` only. Its test runs `none`, `blind_core` and `rule_core`, with `blind_core_fixed` and `oracle` in the primary set.
- `rule_core` is `rule_esc` with `opt.core` (`policy_setup.m:23`). Its class is the monitor's argmax over the hybrid's classes, with the unknown override (`policy_monitor.m:162-167`).
- The monitor's window (`mem.wn`) only grows within an episode. Only the BER and CRC windows restart on a new configuration (`policy_monitor.m:127`, `:255`).
- `onset_shape('on', [])` returns true, so a three-state mode must switch the latch off explicitly. `spec_scores` uses a GPU when one exists, so pool-time scores must be forced to the CPU.
- B3c's model is W `data/core_cnn_model.mat` (686 584 bytes, 26.10 14:50), **SHA-256 `7c9cccbfe9b7fa000ca462e04812a008584990b3478c60086ea2713a50b41689`**. Its states, in order, are clean, narrowband and wideband (`core_cnn_config.m` groups `none`, `tone_jamming`, `jamming`). Its window is **N = 3**: the mean log-probability log(max(p, 1e-6)) over the last N frames (`core_cnn_eval.m` 'logp'). `results/core_cnn_validation.txt` gives N = 3 as the shortest N within one standard error of the best.

**Decision.**
1. **The policy `rule_core3`.** `kind = 'rule_esc'`, `opt.core`, `opt.core3`. It shares `rule_core`'s decision layer; only the class source differs.
   - *Scores.* The pools stage stores `probs3` (frames × 3) per frame, computed on the CPU from the same spectrogram image the hybrid reads. The model's SHA-256 is recorded and must equal the one above.
   - *Class per cycle.* The argmax of the mean log-probability over the last 3 cycles (equal to `core_cnn_eval 'logp'`), over fewer cycles at the episode's start. **The window is not reset at a configuration change**, only at the episode's start, as the hybrid's window in the loop. The two rows then differ only in the class source. B3c's per-sub-run window was a device for the open-loop reading.
   - *Mapping.* clean → `no_action`; narrowband → `channel_switch`; wideband → **W3**.
   - *Off.* No fusion model, no unknown-threat flag, the lost-branch latch off, the sweeper's k-of-n path dormant.
   - *Shared with `rule_core`.*
     - the alarm: a jamming class, the degradation test, the drop/CUSUM alarm;
     - M-of-N confirmation, dwell and hold;
     - escalation over `C.ladder_core`, none while the class reads clean unless `esc_drop` is set;
     - `rule_core_policy.m`'s degradation, quiet-slot and drop branches with their defaults. `PP.core_blind` stays `blind_core`'s only.
   - *Settings.* The decision settings of `data/trained_dqn.mat` as they are, not tuned again.
   - *Refusals.* The stage stops when the pools hold no `probs3` (never a fallback to the hybrid), when the model's hash is another, and when `core3` is set on a blind or DQN policy.
2. **W3, chosen on validation.** Per hop rule, among `channel_switch`, `channel_switch+rate_reduce` and `rate_reduce`. It is chosen on `rule_core3`'s own validation runs of the recoverable episodes of the wideband fixed-total cells, with equal weight per (B_J, JSR_tot), by protocol 6.3's rule. That is the rule of `rule_core`'s W and of `blind_core`'s first response (D98 a), so the tuning budget is equal. `protocol.json` freezes W3 together with W, the model's SHA-256, N and the D106 note's hash. The test stage refuses if any of them changed.
3. **The verdict pairs in CORE_BW** (numbered as in D105):

   | Lecturer KPI | Carried by | Reading |
   |---|---|---|
   | 5, PER reduction ≥ 30% vs static | `rule_core3` against `none` | per jamming type (narrowband = the CW cells, wideband = 4-83.5 MHz pooled), in protocol 6.5's verdict words; pooled is a second line |
   | 6, recovery time (onset → detection; detection → restored) | `rule_core3`, with `none` and `blind_core` beside it | median, p90 and the share never restored; no target |
   | 7, goodput | `rule_core3`, with `none` and `blind_core` beside it | bit/s, geometry bootstrap; no target |
   | 8, the CNN's contribution | `rule_core3` − the validation-tuned `blind_core` | PER, goodput and t_rec_det, paired 95% intervals, pooled, per type and per B_J; analysis, no promise |

   - `rule_core` (the hybrid) gets the same lines, labelled "hybrid, secondary".
   - `rule_core3` − `rule_core` is descriptive only, with no verdict word. The decision layer is the same; only the class source differs.
   - The sensitivities' flip reads `rule_core3`'s primary verdicts.
   - Liu's occupied-reading lines carry `rule_core3` too.
   - Predictions P1-P5 stay on `rule_core`, as written.
   - The detector-by-bandwidth analysis adds `rule_core3`'s windowed class beside the hybrid's.
   - `rule_core3` runs wherever `rule_core` runs in the test stage: the primary set of every hop rule, the 30 dB residual set and the follower set, with the same episodes and frame draws.
4. **D105 amended.** D105 point 2 named CORE_BW's `rule_core` against its validation-tuned `blind_core` as the headline KPI 8. It now reads **`rule_core3` against the validation-tuned `blind_core`**, and CORE_BW's own per-type PER verdicts are `rule_core3`'s. This is allowed because no CORE_BW data exists (state above): there is no output in `uav-gcs-v7-bw/results` and there are no pools. Nothing else in D105 changes. C2e's rows stay as registered and are described as the hybrid's. C2e's KPI 5 verdict and CORE_BW's controlled-bandwidth verdict are reported side by side; neither replaces the other.
5. **Read once,** in CORE_BW's own single test reading (geometry family 6, test block 14; marker and record as in protocol 6.6 G4). It is never read on the fourth test set (spent by B3c), in C2e or in P1R (D104 point 2 unchanged). The interim report (1.11) does not depend on it.
6. **Link-limited lines (D99; `core_bw_lect_by_link.m` on `lect-ebno`): `rule_core3` gets them,** as the verdict's row:
   - Line A and Line B per jamming type, with the verdict-word comparison;
   - recovery time from detection, with Line B the headline and Line A with N;
   - goodput.

   `rule_core` keeps its lines as the secondary row, and Line A stays the verdict. The seven readers are retaken byte for byte from the `experiment_core_bw.m` of the test stage's commit (`lect_ebno_protocol.md` section 7, unchanged). The recovery-time loop in `readings` (`{'none', 'blind_core', 'rule_core'}`) is extended to `rule_core3`. Both go into a tested commit on `lect-ebno` before CORE_BW's test stage.
7. **Where it is written.** Both commits come before any code that produces CORE_BW data.
   - `core-bw-p1` `5d7847f` amends `docs/core_bw_protocol.md` in sections 3, 5, 6.3, 6.5, 6.6 and 7-10. It adds `docs/core_bw_core3_note.txt`, which holds the definition, W3, the rows, the link-limited lines, the tests and the fallback. The val stage writes the note's SHA-256 into `protocol.json`.
   - `core-bw-p1` `7c5ad4c` makes `test_band_protocol_pointer` pin the protocol to `5d7847f`'s file.
8. **Tests** (`test_core`, run at the runbook's row 18 before the smoke, about +15-20 min):
   - `test_band_core3_off_identical`: with the switch off, `pool_cell`, `link_env` and the monitor are bit for bit as before, and `none`, `blind_core`, `blind_core_fixed` and `rule_core` are unchanged with `probs3` present;
   - `test_band_core3_probs3`, `test_band_core3_window`, `test_band_core3_inert`, `test_band_core3_mapping`, `test_band_core3_refusals`, `test_band_core3_freeze`, `test_band_core3_rows`;
   - the smoke's G2 timing includes the extra inference.
9. **Fallback.** If the code and its tests are not green at the runbook's row 18 (CORE_BW tests and smoke), `rule_core3` is not run. A dated line in `docs/core_bw_core3_note.txt` says so before the smoke, and `rule_core` carries the verdicts as D105 registered them. The report then says: "The closed-loop KPIs were measured with the hybrid detector. The three-state CNN was evaluated open-loop (fourth test set, set D per bandwidth). Putting it in the loop is a registered next step, read on profile 3's own C2e and registered before that study's pools."

**Rejected.**
- Rebuilding C2e's pools with `probs3` and reading them after C2e. It would be a second use of C2e's test flights, whose other rows are read on 28.10. The bitwise reproduction of the C1p frames is not assured. It costs 1.5-2 code days plus 4-6 h of the 6-worker slot.
- Keeping CORE_BW as registered, with only an honest statement: CORE_BW is unread and can be amended cheaply now.
- Resetting the three-state window at each configuration change: the hybrid's window in the loop is not reset, so the two rows would differ in more than the class source.
- Re-tuning the decision settings for `rule_core3`: `rule_core` and `blind_core` run `trained_dqn.mat`'s settings as they are. W3 is `rule_core3`'s only choice, with the same budget as W and `blind_core`'s first response.
- Falling back to the hybrid's class when `probs3` is absent: the row would silently become `rule_core`.

**Open.**
- The code and the tests of point 8, on `core-bw-p1`, 27-29.10, before row 18: `pool_cell.m`; `experiment_core_bw.m` (pools, val, test, report); `link_env.m`; `policy_setup.m`; `policy_decide.m` and `policy_monitor.m`.
- The `lect-ebno` commit of point 6.
- README lines 44-45, 101 and 107 name `rule_core` as CORE_BW's CNN-based row. They are to be aligned with point 3.

**Sources.** Lecturer's revised proposal of 2026-10-20 (D92): CNN classification plus rule-based recovery, KPIs 5-8. D98 a, D99, D104, D105. B3c: `docs/core_cnn_protocol.md`, `results/core_cnn_validation.txt`, `docs/core_cnn_read_p1.txt` (W `d4623ed`). **30.10.2026 - mitigation 15 (descriptive row, registered before the CORE_BW test reading).** Proposal mitigation 15 asks that the CNN contribution also compares the number of configuration changes. One descriptive row is added to the CORE_BW report, registered here before `results/core_bw/test_readings.mat` is written or read.

- *Computed.* From the saved test readings, per hop rule and per set (primary, pipe30, follower) and per policy: the mean number of configuration changes per episode (`R.switches`, whole episode) and of false changes (`R.false_sw`), each with a 95% cluster-bootstrap interval (clusters `R.geom`, `boot_cluster.m`, B = 2000, fixed seed 91). The paired differences `rule_core3` minus `rule_core` and `rule_core3` minus `blind_core`, paired by episode index `R.ep` (seed 92, same clusters), for both metrics. Policies on different episode sets or geometries are refused.
- *Reader.* `code/decision/core_bw_switches.m` on branch `core-bw-sw` (commit `206b439`, branched from `core-bw-p1` at `f84384c`), with its synthetic test `code/tests/test_core_bw_switches.m`. It is a separate reader: the frozen CORE_BW code in `core-bw-p1` is not changed.
- *No verdict.* The row carries no threshold and no pass/fail, and does not change KPI 8, the D106 verdicts, W3, the protocol or any frozen choice. Printed lines are labelled `descriptive, mitigation 15, no verdict`.
- *When.* Once, after the CORE_BW test stage and its record step, on the saved `test_readings.mat`. It reads no new data and is not rerun with other settings.

**31.10.2026 - CORE_BW test reading: result (runbook row 20 review; read once on 30.10.2026; nothing tuned, selected or rerun after it).**

- *Reading.* Test block 14 read once: marker 30.10.2026 20:20:42 (commit `1d2806b`, params_id `add18af2b7532a0bd2f7fbc78f06e526`), 139.1 min, `V7TEST OK`; record `docs/core_bw_read_p1.txt` (`75bea00`). `protocol.json` is byte-equal to `docs/core_bw_frozen.json` (sha256 `9b314039...9697`); protocol, both notes and the recoverable list hash as frozen; report sha256 `02e26241...768d`, equal to the test log's print. Receiver r1 throughout (D109).
- *KPI 5 (rule_core3 against none; H1, residual by offset, fixed total power, static jammer; the 85 recoverable H1 scenario points, Eb/N0 6-15 dB; Line A, the verdict).* Narrowband (CW, n = 1440) 62.3% [59.1, 65.4], MET (committed); wideband (4-83.5 MHz, n = 4680) 69.2% [67.8, 70.5], MET (committed); both pooled, second line, 67.4% [65.8, 68.9]. Line B gives the same words. Only the follower sensitivity flips a primary verdict: narrowband 28.6% [26.3, 30.7], NOT MET (blind_core 28.9%, NOT MET too). H3, H4, the 30 dB residual and the equal in-band level flip nothing; the equal-level narrowband "NOT MET" means no recoverable episodes. 27 harmed H1 points (70 and 83.5 MHz, JSR_tot 10-30 dB) are recoverable by no core action and stay outside the verdict.
- *KPI 8 headline (rule_core3 minus the validation-tuned blind_core; analysis, no promise, no verdict word).* Line A pooled: PER -0.0232 [-0.0289, -0.0174] (7.3% [5.6, 9.0]), goodput +3583 [+3277, +3891] bit/s, never restored 3% vs 11%, detection to restored -0.11 [-0.18, -0.03] cycles on the 5431 episodes both recover in. Per type the signs differ: narrowband PER +0.0807 [+0.0552, +0.1085] (adverse) with goodput +19487 [+17702, +21099] bit/s; wideband PER -0.0551 [-0.0667, -0.0451] with goodput -1310 [-1704, -894] bit/s. Line B keeps the signs (+0.0286 [+0.0135, +0.0447]; -0.0374 [-0.0439, -0.0314]). The pooled line is quoted only beside the two type rows, because its weights are the grid's. Under H1 and H3 the tuned blind_core equals the fixed ladder (chosen minus fixed = 0). This replaces D105 point 2's "pending (CORE_BW)". C2e's -3.2% [-5.3, -1.5] stays the secondary, weakened-baseline row and is not combined with it.
- *Narrowband deficit, as far as established (printed lines and code; derived numbers carry no interval).* The deficit does not come from detection, the CNN's class or the first hop: onset to detection 4/4 vs 4/5, detection to restored 2/2 vs 2/2, first-hop escape 1.000. The two rows hold different configurations by registered design:
  - narrowband maps to channel_switch with no validation choice (protocol 6.3);
  - blind_core's single answer, channel_switch+rate_reduce, was chosen on validation over CW and the wideband cells;
  - rate_reduce trades 6.02 dB of processing gain for x1/4 rate.

  About 70% of the gap sits on the 220 link-limited CW episodes (PER about 0.77 vs 0.40). The sign follows blind_core's first response: under H4 (channel_switch alone) rule_core3 is better on CW (points 0.4354 vs 0.4839). The rest of the gap, on episodes that are not link-limited (+0.0286), cannot be split into causes from the printed text. This is a PER-versus-rate trade, not a CNN or detection defect. Wideband is different: both rows run the same configuration (W3 = blind_core's answer). The CNN acts where link metrics confirm late or never (never detected 2% vs 10%), and the gain grows with B_J.
- *KPI 6 and 7, mitigation 15, by link.* rule_core3 confirms at 4/5 cycles (80/100 ms), the hybrid at 1/1. The "-3.52 cycles" against the hybrid is mostly rule_core3's later detection clock and is never to be quoted as "faster recovery". Goodput (Line A): rule_core3 15659 [15003, 16293] bit/s, blind_core 12075, none 5042.
  - Mitigation 15 (descriptive, no verdict; whole episode, CRC-triggered re-hops included), H1 primary, changes per episode: rule_core3 3.718 [3.501, 3.944], blind_core 2.518 [2.407, 2.628], rule_core 4.375 [4.198, 4.558]. rule_core3 minus blind_core +1.200 [+0.986, +1.425], false changes +0.020 [+0.016, +0.026]; rule_core3 minus rule_core -0.657 [-0.716, -0.596]. The same order holds in all six sets.
  - `core_bw_lect_by_link`: GATE PASS, Line A equal to the report, Lines A and B give the same words; 89 of 216 flights link-limited (848 of 6120 verdict episodes).
- *Gate (D109).* No CORE_BW result number enters the book before D2c (`rx_fix_rule3.md` section 4, as committed on `rx-ne-fix` before any D2c data) gives U_s. Each jammed-state number then carries "r1; receiver loss on the restored link <= U_s (D2c, validation)", and the KPI 5 words are read by rule 4.6. Quotable now: design, geometry and bookkeeping only. The interim report (1.11) is unaffected (point 5).
- *Defects.* Reporting only; the frozen tree is not changed.
  - The prediction lines print their criterion as fixed text ("(holds 0)", "(longer)", "(0)", "holds 0") beside computed values. The computed words are right (P1, P4, P5 do not hold; P2, P3 hold), and the book writes them in words.
  - Per-B_J goodput and t_rec_det differences, named in protocol 6.5, were not computed.
  - No decision-layer, CNN or hop-model defect was found.
  - None is a profile 3 candidate under the registered rules: CORE_BW runs on profile 1 only; D113 keeps `rule_core_policy.m` unchanged in profile 3; D105 point 2 keeps blind_core's fixed first response in TIP3; profile 3's `lect_kpis.m` already prints recovery from the onset.

## D109 — Delay-line receiver defect: found on validation, D1 diagnosis result, receiver per run (29.10.2026)

**Found (validation only, after profile 1's test readings).**
- On the 27 validation flights (`link_floor_partA/partB.mat`, runs 411-698 at 9, 12 and 15 dB) three frames collapse in the delay-line (`NE > 1`) receiver at 12 dB: 420/5, 452/10, 459/16, with BER 0.30, 0.29, 0.16. Their true post-MRC Es/N0 is 11.9, 8.5 and 9.7 dB, so they are not noise-limited. A flat-channel re-simulation of the same flights (a different fading realization) decodes them, but a correctly run flat receiver on the same samples does not (D1 below). At +30 dB, 420/5 and 452/10 still fail: the first pass fits one channel profile on the training only, and on these frames the training sits in a within-frame fade.
- The fault is in the first estimation pass of the `NE > 1` branch: with the decision-directed passes off, no frame of the four improves and FER rises from 0.3648 to 0.4593. The unwrapped-slope frequency step is a confirmed contributor in code and partly in effect, and is not the whole cause.
- Size on the clean validation link as stated by the ruling: clean FER at most 0.25 pp at 12 dB (at most 15 of 6000 frames); CREF mean BER 20-34% higher at 12 dB. The rate under interference is unmeasured.

**D1 (diagnostic run, 29.10).**
- Valid: `RXD GUARDS G1 PASS G2 PASS G3 PASS G4 PASS -> D1 valid`.
- 11 isolated arms and a correctly run flat shadow receiver: none recovers any of the three frames (0 of 3 each, recovered = frame BER <= 0.05). The rule's last bullet of reading point 3 sends to D2 (ruling 3.4). No candidate is named and nothing is adopted. Fresh held-out frames: n = 0.

**Receiver per run.**
- CORE_BW, P1 and P1R use the current receiver r1 (receiver blob `40dc1f9`, as built at `d4623ed`). The fix branch is not merged into `-bw`.
- Profile 3 takes a fix (r2) only if one passes the pre-set adoption rule (`rx-ne-fix\docs\rx_fix_rule2.md`, ruling sections 3.5-3.6) before its deployment; otherwise it runs on r1 with the defect disclosed with its measured size (ruling 4.4 timebox, 3.7).
- Every result names its receiver. Size is from validation only; no test number shows a fix's effect; profile 1's test sets are not re-read.
- A candidate born after D2 is read under a new pre-set rule with new fresh frames (ruling 3.7, last bullet; reading open point 4).

**D2 (noise-free twin, 30.10.2026).**
- Valid: six tests passed; rule sha256 `55e44f27...b9a8` as registered; `RXE GUARDS G1 PASS G2 PASS G3 PASS G4 PASS -> D2 valid`; the twin truth is valid on all 16 arms (`hand_rx_d2_20261030_1846.txt`).
- Reading under the pre-set rule (ruling 3.4, interpretation d): none. No rung recovers any of the three frames (`twinf`, `twing`, `twin` 0 of 3; `twin` BER 0.146, 0.380, 0.102 against 0.301, 0.292, 0.161 as built). The registered sentence applies: "none means the cause lies after the estimates (gain smoothing or the equalizer)", read with the D1 reading section 4.
- Established: noise in the frequency and training-template estimates is not the cause. An exact noise-free training template changes nothing (0.297, 0.371, 0.167). The frequency step helps on 420/5 and 459/16 and is not sufficient.
- Observed in the twin truth (reported only): the true lag shape changes within the frame on all three frames, so the training-time shape, even exact, carries 18%, 9.5% and 59% of the own-signal energy over the pilot blocks (63 clean frames: at least 89%). The change follows a training fade on 420/5 and 452/10 and occurs mid-frame without one on 459/16, so a training fade is not necessary. The per-antenna phase slopes differ by 1.2-1.8 kHz, so the kHz offsets are not an oscillator offset.
- Open: whether a model that follows the shape decodes the frames; which of the block gain, its smoothing, the MRC matched filter and the training-designed equalizer breaks; the rate under interference.
- Census (`none`, `tone_jamming`, `jamming` at level index 3 under `no_action`, 10 split-2 flights, 200 frames each): no rung decodes a frame that r1 loses, in any cell. Not informative as a bound: the count cannot see this defect (0 of r1's 14 losses on the 27 flights, the three defect frames included), both jammed cells are saturated (every arm loses 200 of 200), and the clean cell (0 of 200) is weaker than the validation bound. The rate under interference stays unmeasured and is never reported as 0%.
- Consequences: CORE_BW and P1R stay on r1. Ruling 4.2's bound comes from 3.6 after an adoption or, without one, from a new pre-registered r1 census on CORE_BW's core configurations in the jammed states, before CORE_BW's numbers enter the book. A post-D2 candidate is read under a new pre-set rule (`rx_fix_rule3.md`: a per-block genie D3 first, new fresh frames from a new validation seed block, stage 1a's frequency clause dropped), per ruling 3.7's last bullet; profile 3 follows the 4.4 timebox. The delivered interim paragraph (draft 7, section 3.1.3) is not contradicted; `interim_rx_sentences.md` is superseded.
- Sources: `rx_d2_reading.md` (official reading), `rx_d2_read_rule.md`, `rx_d2_read_phys.md`, `d2_dispatch.md`.

P1R never waits for it, and the D3 reading can precede ruling 4.4's timebox.

## D108 — The edge commitment's clean-link criterion becomes LINK ≤ 5.7% (802.11a's criterion through the simulated block fading) and REC/PROT are read per onset by the registered rate rule; written after profile 1's EDGE reading and before any reading under it (approved by Adi, 29.10.2026)

**D108 (2026-10-29, approved by Adi): the edge commitment's clean-link criterion becomes LINK ≤ 5.7%, the fading-consistent translation of 802.11a's criterion, and REC/PROT are read per onset by the registered rate rule. Written after profile 1's EDGE reading, which stays as reported ("no point committed"), and before any reading under it. First read on P1R's fresh clean set (LINK) and on profile 3's unread pools (all parts).**

1. **Why.**
   - Profile 1's EDGE committed no point (0/300, 0/252; `edge_summary.txt:12-26`; review `core/chain_review_p1.md` T1-T3, T16).
   - LINK ≤ 1.4% (DEC:682, 691, 740) came from IEEE 802.11a-1999's sensitivity test (PER < 10% at a 1000-byte PSDU, conducted, coded link), carried to our 1032-bit frame through an AWGN bit error rate (1.317e-5). Block fading does not keep that bit error rate. Carried through the simulated block fading, the same criterion is about 5.7% on our frame (model range 2.1-6.3% across K; `core/d108_final.md` 3.3).
   - The simulated clean link loses 1.8% [0.92, 2.9] at 15 dB on validation (`results/clean_ref.txt`, 26.10). The validation breakdown (`core/link_floor_val.md`) places its floor in the model's physics: steep bank tilting all three parallel dipoles together, worst at low K, a loss MRC cannot recover, stated as a worst case (attitude loss on the scattered paths) under a uniform heading-rate envelope; against the textbook at the flights' own draws the packet loss is about 0.8 dB worse at 9 and 12 dB and 0.55 dB at 15 dB (pooled), consistent with the real receiver's measured ~1 dB, and not uniform across cells. So LINK at 1.4% could not commit at any grid point, by the target's derivation.
   - REC/PROT printed the per-onset share beside the hull of its bootstrap and the Clopper-Pearson interval of fully held flights (`edge_verdict.m:40-49`): the value and its interval measured different quantities. With 2 onsets per flight the registered rule's per-onset bar lay between 90% (onsets fully correlated) and about 95% (independent); the design effect is not yet measured.
   - **This amends a registered target after the reading that failed it.** It is a correction of the derivation, not a looser target: the value is derived from the registered source on the channel LINK reads, it was computed before any D108 reading, it is not recomputed from any later reading, and the pre-D108 verdicts are printed beside. The looser 10% (D99's J tag) was rejected as the gate.
2. **LINK.** Clean no_action packet loss ≤ 5.7% (3-antenna MRC; model range 2.1-6.3% across K; the ρ range U[0.3, 0.9] names no source, `init_params.m:333`), read by `edge_verdict` (rate, `'le'`, 4000 resamples, n ≥ 36). The commitment otherwise stays the proposal's relative criterion. Printed beside every clean-link reading: the clean loss with its interval and distance; the verdict at 1.4% on the same interval ("registered before D108", reported only); the tag J (loss < 10%, D99, descriptive); and "C&C reliability: up to 1e-3 packet error loss rate (3GPP TR 36.777, Table 5.1-1), a service requirement after the link layer; not modelled, not claimed".
3. **LINK_T** is unchanged: max(2 × the same flights' clean loss, 1.4%), the floor kept as registered (DEC:734) in `TGT.linkt_floor`.
4. **REC, PROT** (and REC_h, PROT_h, the random policy's and no response's PROT, the band PROT) are per-onset rates read by D74's rate rule (DEC:738): the hull of the cluster bootstrap over flights and the Clopper-Pearson interval on the effective sample size. Target 0.90, N_MIN 36. Size at a true 0.90 fixed by `test_edge_rec_rate` (≤ 3% for within-flight correlation 0, 0.5, 1). Sources: Davison & Hinkley 1997, section 3.8; Field & Welsh, JRSS B 69(3), 2007; Kish 1965; Clopper & Pearson 1934; Thulin 2014.
5. **Both versions.** Printed beside, reported only, labelled "registered before D108": LINK ≤ 1.4% (per point, per band and its walk), REC/PROT per flight, SYSTEM and SYSTEM+LINK (REC per flight, LINK ≤ 1.4%), and the static commitment at every delay and at the check points under those definitions, also in the KPI summary (DEC:740). Under D108 only: the floor without signalling, the follower and comb commitments, the band table and the REC, PROT and SYSTEM walks. Git blob hashes of `edge_map.m`, `edge_verdict.m` and `held_verdict.m` are logged before profile 3's C2e (`f6f6e03`: `ffd90f56808e`, `00a25c17b4ac`, `516a03d62e23`).
6. **Fixed on validation:** the predicted LINK region from each profile's post-M clean table (profile 1 and P1R's fresh set, both r1: 15 dB COMMITTED; 12 dB on the bound and leaning to UNDETERMINED, since CREF's intervals are flight-bootstrap percentiles only and EDGE's hull with Clopper-Pearson on n_eff can only widen them; 9 dB and below NOT COMMITTED; profile 3 from its own C1c, on its own receiver); the REC/PROT size from the analytic table and the synthetic test; their power from the design effect on C2's validation scoring through the V0 reader (reported, never gating).
7. **Readings.** P1R: LINK once on the fresh clean set by `link_fresh.m` right after C2e, labelled "D108, fresh set, first reading"; no REC/PROT reading (no fresh threat set). Profile 3: all parts once at EDGE on its unread pools. Profile 1: not re-judged. No target, rule or threshold changes after any D108 reading.
8. **Not changed:** the Eb/N0 grid, the K floor and the K ≥ −3 dB reading, ρ, speed and altitude ranges, the FA, DET, DET_h and SURV targets, N_MIN, the bands, the walks, KPI 4 and every policy.
9. **Code:** `d108-edge` `f6f6e03` (`b7bebcc` and the review's fixes, on TIP3, tree `9e89d869`): `edge_map.m`, `held_verdict.m`, `measure_all_kpis.m` (text); tests `test_edge_rec_rate`, `test_edge_link_gate`, `test_edge_linkt_unchanged`, `test_harmed_readings` updated; SMOKE EDGE in profile 3's smoke. `d108-p1r` `c9eeaa3` (`c6d9f0a` and the review's wording fixes, on `p1r` `198f82f`): `link_fresh.m`, `docs/p1r_protocol.md` amendment; tests `test_link_fresh_flights`, `test_link_fresh_verdicts`, `test_link_fresh_run`; smoke cell A check `link_fresh('check', true)`.
10. **Receiver (D109, ruling 4.6).** The LINK target is 5.7% in every receiver outcome. Profile 1's LINK prediction and reading stay on r1, like for like, and so does P1R's fresh-set LINK. If a receiver fix (r2) is adopted, `link_floor_check` is re-run on r2 on validation for the D108 wording only, and profile 3's LINK is predicted on its own receiver before its registration. The antenna count is outside the ruling: the 5.7% is derived with 3 antennas and profile 3 has 4 (about 5.4% by the same conversion); Adi chooses, before profile 3's registration, between one system target of 5.7% with the 4-antenna value disclosed and profile 3's own model-only conversion (6.9 item 3).
11. **Rejected, with reasons** (`core/d108_final.md` section 5): LINK on a coded configuration; a coded baseline; an extended grid; relative-only; 10% on our frame as the gate (a post-reading relaxation, not the 802.11a criterion at our packet length); C2 service targets as a gate; the flight-level print fix as the definition; a bootstrap-only interval.

## D110 — Profile 3's fusion: a lost-branch and level head, gated coherence columns and floor-relative idle columns as pre-registered candidates, chosen by nested cross-validation on the enlarged fit set, re-checked at B2F and read once on the fourth test set; written before any of their data (30.10.2026)

**Why.**
- airframe_shadowing F1 is 84.5% on profile 1's fourth set (`kpi_summary.txt:6`), below the 85% floor Adi set on 17.10. On profile 3's fit set d91 reaches about 83.8% (`d91_fuse.md`:404), so profile 3's unread fourth set will probably also fall below 85% without a change. D91's branch evidence added +0.39 / +0.30 points (nested, interval touching 0).
- D91's M3 (DEC:1269) is a bound, measured out of fold by sub-run. Bagged trees over the receiver's coherence and LOS columns raise shadowing recall at the fusion's own clean budget:
  - overall: +4.7 [+3.9, +5.6] on profile 1 and +7.0 [+6.1, +7.9] on profile 3;
  - at onset age 0-2: +10.8 on profile 1 and +15.7 on profile 3;
  - at link K ≤ 0 dB: +2.7 [−0.3, +5.5] on profile 1 and +7.7 [+4.6, +10.7] on profile 3 (FUSE:274, :393). With four antennas the cue still carries information at low K.
- The linear fusion cannot use these columns, because they carry meaning only once a branch has stepped.
  - A faulty connector scales everything its branch receives, so the coherence and LOS share are kept. This holds while the weak branch stays measurable after frame integration.
  - A hidden antenna loses its line of sight (K about −16 dB; Sun, Matolak & Rayess 2017, Part IV, Fig. 12).
- path_loss at the 4 dB step reads 41.4% [33.1, 49.8] (`weak_points.txt:243-246`). By link K band it reads 28.9 / 33.6 / 68.7% at K < 0 / 0-10 / ≥ 10 dB (profile 1's second test set, `docs/runs/analysis/audit1_confirmed.json:313`).
  - At K ≥ 10 dB the measured per-cycle level spread is 0.69 dB, so the level-step statistic alone has δ ≥ 5.5 there. The 31-point gap at K ≥ 10 dB is therefore not physics.
  - At K < 10 dB the same statistic alone reaches about 5-26% (K < 0) and 13-66% (K 0-10), at about 1% per-frame false alarm. The fusion already reads 28.9% at K < 0, so these figures are that statistic's reach, not an information bound.
- benign_interference reads 88.1%. The floor-relative crest reaches AUC 0.993 where the fixed busy rule carries nothing (audit lit-2). `opt.cfar` was measured on validation only (D90: weak-WLAN recall 15.3 → 49.2% on profile 3; nested +0.04 [0.00, +0.08]; kept in 2 of 5 folds). It was never measured on the enlarged fit set (`fusion_pick.m:13`: `cfar []`).
- `d107_final.md` point 10 (`:40-42`, approved by Adi on 29.10.2026) deferred M3 to after profile 3 because it "needs a detector refit". In fact M3 needs only a fusion refit, which profile 3's step 5 (B2F) runs anyway: no CNN retraining, no new pool field, no rebuild. Bringing M3 into profile 3 reverses an approved deferral, so LBH is conditional on Adi's explicit yes (point 8b).

**Decision.**
1. **Base B.** The d91 pick ("branch evidence, phi = 1", `fusion_pick.m`), refitted on the same 5 folds by sub-run (seed 9101).
2. **Candidates, fixed now; no hyperparameter search.** LBH and LBH+CFAR enter only under point 8b.
   - **LBH, the lost-branch and level head.**
     - Classes L = {none, path_loss, antenna_fault, airframe_shadowing}.
     - Inputs, all already in the fusion row and built by `fuse_classes('windows')`: the linear fusion's log-probabilities of the four L classes and the log of their sum; branch cues 1-16; level-step cues 17-21; `margin_mean` and `sinr_gap_w`; coherence and LOS columns 43-52; the 18 branch-evidence columns of the d91 pick (iso, glr without gl_phi, post, held).
     - Not used: idle columns, antenna index or position, truth fields (K, loss, geometry), IMU, GNSS.
     - Learner: bagged classification trees, 100 trees, MinLeafSize 20, the toolbox's default predictors per split, fixed seed. These are M3's settings (`d91_fuse\m_all.m:267`).
     - Stacked on the linear fusion's **out-of-fold** probabilities.
     - Combination: p_L = Σ_{c∈L} p_fusion(c); p(c) = p_L · q(c | x) for c in L; every other class keeps p_fusion(c) exactly.
   - **GCC, gated coherence columns (linear).** Six columns s × {coh_contrast, los_contrast, los_k, coh_weak, k_contrast, ps_k_contrast}, with s = 1[gl_db ≥ 3 dB], the threshold `branch_coherence` already uses (`window_cues.m:52-53`).
   - **CFAR, floor-relative idle columns** (`window_cues.m` `opt.cfar`): crest (max − p10) and spread (p90 − p10) over each idle gap's own floor. T1 and T2 are the 99th percentiles of the training folds' clean gaps; m0 likewise.
   - **LBH+CFAR** and **GCC+CFAR**.
3. **Rule S** applies to LBH, GCC and the head part of a combination. It is read out of fold, with a paired bootstrap over sub-runs: 1000 resamples, seed 9102.
   - Every bound is from the two-sided 95% percentile interval (lower = 2.5th, upper = 97.5th percentile). Gains are in percentage points.
   - Margin basis: D91's profile 3 nested SEs are about 0.22 for shadowing F1, 0.064 for macro-F1 and 0.077 points for clean frames (DEC:1251-1256). S1's +1.0 is therefore about 4.5 SE.
   - S1, airframe_shadowing F1 gain: point ≥ +1.0 and lower bound > 0.
   - S2, macro-F1 gain over all frames and above the KPI 1 threshold: both lower bounds > 0.
   - S3, every other class's F1: upper bound ≥ 0 and point ≥ −0.5.
   - S4, antenna_fault and airframe_shadowing F1 at link K ≤ 0 dB: point ≥ −1.0 and upper bound ≥ 0.
   - S5, clean frames read as any threat and as a lost branch: lower bounds ≤ 0 and points ≤ +0.05.
   - S6, shadowing F1 point gain ≥ 0 in each of the four component sets (validation, first test split, fresh, fresh2).
   - S7 (LBH only): the packed head takes p95 ≤ 1.0 ms per single-row call on one thread, and the detector still passes `kpi7_admit.m` (median ≤ 5 ms, p95 ≤ 10 ms; D80).

   **Rule W** applies to the CFAR part.
   - W1, benign_interference F1 gain: point ≥ +0.5 and lower bound > 0.
   - W2, macro-F1: above the threshold, lower bound ≥ −0.1; over all frames, lower bound > 0.
   - W3: S3 and S5 hold, and "clean read as benign_interference" has lower bound ≤ 0.
   - W4 (gating): the realized exceedance of T1 and T2 on the held-out folds' clean gaps is ≤ 1%.
   - W5: benign F1 point gain ≥ 0 in each of the four sets.
4. **Procedure.** It is nested, because the enlarged fit set was already used for D91 and for M3, which motivates LBH.
   1. Out of fold on the whole fit set, each candidate is compared with B. Of the candidates passing their rule(s), the pick is the one with the largest macro-F1 gain over all frames. If none passes, the pick is B.
   2. Step 1 as a whole goes through nested cross-validation with 5 outer folds (Varma & Simon 2006).
      - Inside each outer fold, **everything fitted is refitted on its training folds only**: the level-0 linear fusion and the out-of-fold probabilities that feed the head; the KPI 1 threshold; and CFAR's T1, T2 and m0 (likewise in the inner folds).
      - Step 1 runs on inner out-of-fold readings of the training folds. The inner pick is then fitted and predicts the held-out outer fold.
   3. The nested reading is the outer out-of-fold prediction of the whole of step 1. Each outer fold contributes its own inner pick (B where the inner step picked none), and the reading is compared with B refitted on the same outer folds.
      - It must pass rule S if any outer fold picked LBH, GCC or a combination, and rule W if any picked CFAR or a combination.
      - The five outer picks are printed.
      - Step 1's pick is adopted only if the nested reading passes. Otherwise B deploys unchanged.

   The rule is code (`fusion_adopt('head')`, `fusion_adopt('cfar')`), committed with its SHA-256 before the measurement. The new seed streams of the trees and of the inner folds join `test_seed_streams_disjoint` (past bug 9).

   The nested reading guards only the choice among the five fixed candidates. LBH and GCC were designed after M3's out-of-fold reading of this same fit set, so every fit-set gain here is optimistic by an unknown amount and is never quoted as the effect. The fourth set's single reading is the only unbiased one.
5. **Deployment at profile 3's B2F (STOP_SOFT step 5).**
   - B2F deploys the fixed pick and **re-checks its rule out of fold on the fit set it actually uses**: the r1 extracts, or the rebuild if r2 is adopted (`rx_fix_ruling.md` 4.4).
   - A failed re-check deploys B alone. This is recorded as a fallback, not as a no-go of d91.
   - `FM.head` holds the packed arrays only, walked one vector step per level like D80's isolation-forest scorer (`iforest_pack.m`, `iforest_score.m`). It is never a TreeBagger object, so DET_ID stays a stable byte hash.
   - B is stored beside the pick as `base_fm`.
   - `fusion_pick.m` names D110's pick (B on a failed re-check). These guards then check it with their form unchanged (test): `select_fusion.m:93`; B3r's `fresh3_pick` (`eval_detector.m:59-63`, already in the code); and `fresh3_fusion_rev` (`eval_detector.m:52-58`, `fusion_rev_id.m:7`).
   - The head lives inside `fuse_classes('apply')`. That is the single call site of `policy_monitor` (`:304`), `fused_class`, `det_frames` (`:62`), `eval_detector`, `compare_architectures` and `emitter_reading`.
6. **Cost gate.**
   - The head runs wherever the fusion runs per cycle: `policy_monitor.m:304` (C1d, C2 and its retrains, C2e, C2g, C2u, EDGE, GUI) and `det_frames.m:62`. C1p only stores the keys (`build_policy_pools.m:128`).
   - Profile 3's smoke measures the monitor's cost per cycle with and without the head. From each stage's cycle count it prints the projected increase of C1d + C2 + C2e + C2g + C2u + EDGE.
   - Above 2% of their sum, B deploys. The projected increase is counted on profile 3's path in D118.
7. **Timebox.**
   - The nested verdict must be written before B2F would otherwise start. Under r1 that is right after deployment steps D1-D4; under r2, when the A5 rebuild reaches B2F.
   - If the verdict is late, or if waiting would move profile 3's projected end past 24.11 (D118), B deploys unchanged and the reason is recorded.
   - No per-fold or partial output is read before the verdict line is written. A measurement still running at the timebox is stopped, and its partial output is never read or used later.
8. **Where the measurement runs.**
   - `m_head.m` runs on read-only copies of `scratchpad\d91_fuse\data_p3\sets.mat` and `ck_p3.mat`: one thread, at most about 10 GB, a gated hand-run in the p3 slot stand-in. Estimate: 10-20 h.
   - Before loading, `m_head.m` asserts that no input row comes from set 4 (run ids of the 6,000,000 block; `splits_fresh3.mat` never on its path).
   - **Dated line on D104 point 1:** "Amended 30.10.2026 by D110 (registered under decide-after-30min, Adi silent since 30.10.2026 19:37): beside P1R's C2, and never beside its C1d, fresh build, C2e, `link_fresh` or breakdown, one profile 3 hand-run at a time may run in the p3 slot. The allowed runs are D110's single-thread measurement on scratch copies, the D110-D117 branch tests, and K2-1 (D111).
